# Changelog — November 2026

> Archived entries for November 2026, newest first. For recent work see [the live changelog](../changelog.md).

## 🐛 fix(engine): a conversation start refused by the agent's configuration answers 422, and unresolved vault references name their tenant (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/conversation-start-config-errors`) — external security review finding B5 (low)

### What changed and why

An httpcall on `CONVERSATION_START` that EDDI refused — a `${vault:…}` reference naming no secret, a secret sent to a host its `allowedHosts` does not list — reached `POST /agents/{agentId}/start` as an opaque `500 Failed to start conversation`: `ApiCallExecutor` wraps the refusal in a `LifecycleException`, the pipeline wraps that again, and `ConversationService` turns it into a `ResourceStoreException`. A client (the Manager, the Chat UI, the Platform Operator) could not tell "fix the agent" from "the server broke".

- **New marker, [`AgentConfigurationRefusal`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/lifecycle/exceptions/AgentConfigurationRefusal.java)**, implemented only by exceptions that certainly mean "this configuration cannot run", with a bounded, cycle-safe `find(Throwable)` over the cause chain.
- **[`RestAgentEngine`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestAgentEngine.java)**: when a start fails and the chain holds such a refusal, it answers **`422`** `{"error": "agent_misconfigured", "message": "...", "retryable": false}` — the flat envelope this endpoint already uses for its `503`, which the Manager's `apiErrorFromResponse` reads as code + sentence with no change. The message is the refusal's own (names only), passed through `SecretRedactionFilter` again and capped at 1000 characters; no stack trace. Everything else stays a `500`.
- **[`SecretResolver`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/SecretResolver.java)**: `diagnoseUnresolved` says why a reference stayed unresolved (`VAULT_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `RESERVED`, `NOT_FOUND`, `PROVIDER_FAILED`) from one metadata read on the failure path, never the value; `unresolvedReferenceFailure` builds the error — a `MisconfiguredVaultReferenceException` (an `IllegalArgumentException`, as before) for the first three, a plain `IllegalArgumentException` for a vault outage. `requireResolved` (LLM parameters) and the "not found" log line now name the tenant too.
- **[`SecretReference.describe`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/model/SecretReference.java)**: `${vault:name} (no tenant given, so tenant 'default')` / `${vault:acme/name} (tenant 'acme')`. A not-found short-form reference adds that it always looks in `default` and how to write `${vault:<tenant>/name}` — the tester's `${vault:name}` for a secret stored in tenant `eddi`.
- **[`ApiCallExecutor`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallExecutor.java)**: `resolveSecrets` throws the diagnosed failure; `buildRequest` prefixes a refusal with `API call '<name>':` so the 422 says which call. Two small hunks.
- **[`VaultHostAllowlist`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/VaultHostAllowlist.java)**: new `HostRefusedException extends HostNotAllowedException` for the missing-secret, not-on-`allowedHosts` and plaintext-transport refusals. The "allowed hosts could not be read" case keeps the parent type and stays a `500`.
- **Platform Operator**: the test-drive section explains a `422 agent_misconfigured` start; revision bumped.
- **Docs**: [`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md#errors) (422 row), [`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md#when-a-reference-does-not-resolve) (new "When a reference does not resolve"), [`troubleshooting.md`](../troubleshooting.md).

### Design decisions

- **422, not 409.** The request is well-formed and nothing about the target's *state* conflicts (409 is what this API uses for an awaiting-approval or busy conversation); what cannot be processed is the agent the request addresses, and repeating it fails the same way until the configuration changes. `WebhookEventHandler` already answers 422 for "the routed target cannot run".
- **A marker, not message matching or a blanket `IllegalArgumentException` rule.** The SSRF validator, a bad URL and many genuine bugs all throw plain `IllegalArgumentException`; only exceptions written for a configuration refusal opt in. A vault that could not be read, and an `allowedHosts` list that could not be read, are deliberately left out: calling an outage a misconfiguration sends an admin to fix a config that is fine.
- **The message reaches the caller.** It already did on a say turn (the same sentence lands in `taskErrors`); the start path now matches. It carries reference names, hosts and the call name — never a value.

### Not done

- **The httpcall SSRF refusal at run time is still a 500.** `UrlValidationUtils` throws a plain `IllegalArgumentException`, and "Cannot resolve hostname" (DNS, possibly transient) shares that type, so it has no clean signal; another branch is moving the httpcall SSRF check to deploy time (`ApiCallsTask`). A run-time refusal type that implements `AgentConfigurationRefusal` would get the 422 with no further change here.
- **Only `POST /agents/{agentId}/start`** maps the refusal. A say turn still reports it as `TURN_FAILED` in the snapshot's `taskErrors`; MCP, A2A, `/v1` and the other start paths are unchanged.
- An unresolved reference in **LLM parameters** (`UnresolvedSecretReferenceException`) now names its tenant but is not marked: `requireResolved` cannot tell a missing secret from a vault outage.
- **Two paths keep the marker through redaction and forEach**: `ApiCallExecutor.withoutSecrets` re-marks its secret-free copy of a refusal (the cause is still dropped), and a forEach step with `onItemFailure: "fail"` carries the first refusal behind an item whose request was not built, so both still answer 422.

## ✨ feat(groups): a human-approved backlog task can count as done at cadence writeback (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/cadence-approved-counts-as-done`)

### Why

`TeamCadenceService.writebackCompleted` kept a pulled backlog task done only when the matching
discussion task was `VERIFIED`; everything else went back to `PENDING` with feedback appended
(the cross-run retry loop). A task approved by a person at a TASK-granularity HITL gate becomes
`COMPLETED`, not `VERIFIED`, so a standing team that uses human approval instead of a VERIFY
phase had every approved task pulled and executed again on its next run — for a team that
publishes, the same post twice. The only workaround was an extra VERIFY phase with a verifier
agent.

### What changed

- **New switch `hitlConfig.approvalCompletesBacklogTask`** (boolean, default `false`) on the
  group configuration ([`AgentGroupConfiguration.HitlConfig`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/model/AgentGroupConfiguration.java)).
  With it on, the writeback also keeps a task done when a person approved it: the backlog task
  lands `COMPLETED` (not `VERIFIED`, `verified=false`) with the approved result — the approver's
  edit when `review.allowResultEdits` was used — and no retry feedback is appended.

  ```json
  "hitlConfig": { "granularity": "TASK", "timeoutPolicy": "WAIT_INDEFINITELY", "approvalCompletesBacklogTask": true }
  ```

- **A durable approval marker.** The task list had no record of *how* a task became `COMPLETED`.
  [`SharedTaskList`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/model/SharedTaskList.java)
  gains a side map `approvals: taskId → TaskApproval(decidedBy, at, edited)`, written by
  `approveTask(taskId, decidedBy)` and `approveTaskWithEditedResult`, cleared by
  `resetFromAnyToAssigned` (a new attempt supersedes the approval, as it already superseded the
  reviewer edit). `GroupHitlCoordinator` now passes the decision's `decidedBy` on both approval
  paths (explicit `taskApprovals` and the approve-all / timeout path).
- **Metrics.** `WorkspaceMetrics.tasksApproved` and `MemberStats.tasksApproved` count these
  tasks; `tasksVerified` is left meaning verified. Before, an approved task counted as a
  `tasksFailed` for its member.
- **Docs:** [`group-conversations.md`](../group-conversations.md#standing-teams-i13) (Standing
  Teams, new "Human approval as done" paragraph), [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md) (group config table).
- **Manager:** a checkbox with help text in the group HITL editor, shown at TASK granularity;
  `GroupHitlConfig.approvalCompletesBacklogTask` in the API types; two i18n keys in all 11 locales.
- **Platform Operator:** a bullet explaining the writeback rule and the switch; revision bump.

### Design decisions

- **Where the switch lives — `hitlConfig` on the group, not the cadence.** It qualifies the
  approval gate, so it sits next to `granularity` and `onTaskRejection` where that gate is
  configured, and it is versioned with the group. A cadence-level flag would have needed the
  workspace to record which cadence started the running discussion (it does not), and the
  workspace is deliberately unversioned. The writeback reads it from the group's **current**
  version (field-injected `IAgentGroupStore`, like `teamBudgetService`); an unreadable config
  falls back to the default rule, logged.
- **How "approved by a person" is told from "merely COMPLETED".** The outcome must be
  `COMPLETED` (a later VERIFY that failed it leaves it `FAILED`) **and** carry an approval record
  whose `decidedBy` is not a `system:*` actor. A task completed in an ungated phase has no record.
  A side map rather than a 16th `TaskItem` component, following `reviewerEdits`, `awardedBids`
  and `reworkCounts`: no positional-constructor churn, and the map is persisted with the
  discussion by the same Jackson document path on MongoDB and PostgreSQL. A document written
  before it reads back with an empty map (such tasks simply do not count as approved).
- **Timeouts do not count.** `timeoutPolicy: AUTO_APPROVE` approves with
  `decidedBy=system:timeout`; that is a decision nobody made, so its tasks return to `PENDING`
  as before — the same line earned-autonomy evidence draws (`TIMEOUT`/`SYSTEM` refused).
  `system:` is a reserved principal prefix, so no caller can claim it. A `null` decider (auth
  disabled) is a person, consistent with the coordinator's existing fresh-turn-budget rule.
- **A distinct counter**, `tasksApproved`, rather than crediting `tasksVerified`.

### Tests

`TeamCadenceApprovalWritebackTest` (new): default and explicit `false` unchanged; approved task
stays done with its result and counts; edited approval keeps the edit; ungated completion still
returns to PENDING; timeout approval does not count; unnamed approver counts; approved-then-failed
VERIFY retries; VERIFIED untouched; unreadable config and missing store fall back to the default.
`SharedTaskListHitlTest.Approvals` (new): marker recorded/cleared, `byPerson`, old JSON without
`approvals` loads, round-trip. `GroupConversationServiceHitlTest`: the timeout approve-all path
records `system:timeout`. Manager: `group-hitl-editor.test.tsx`.

### Not done

The workspace page does not display `tasksApproved` yet (it is in the workspace JSON).

## ✨ feat(mcp): admin tools for prices, the tenant quota, team cadences and vault names — looser spend waits for a human (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/mcp-admin-spend-tools`)

### What changed and why

An administrator's AI client operating EDDI over MCP could not set the model price catalog, the tenant
monthly cost quota or a team's cadences, so it had to drive the Manager in a browser. Those writes were
left off MCP on purpose — an MCP client is often a model, and lowering a price to $0 or raising a quota is
raising a budget, which is exactly what a prompt injection wants. This change adds the tools without
giving that up: **a write that only makes spending stricter applies at once; a write with any loosening
part applies nothing and becomes a pending change that an `eddi-admin` approves in the Manager**, with the
approver as actor. See [Spend-Control Changes](../spend-control-changes.md).

- **12 new MCP tools** (108 → 120). Admin only: `read_tenant_quota`, `update_model_prices`,
  `update_tenant_quota`, `list_spend_control_changes`, `withdraw_spend_control_change`, `list_vault_keys`.
  Author tier (`eddi-admin`, `eddi-editor`, as REST): `list_team_cadences`, `create_team_cadence`,
  `delete_team_cadence`, `read_lesson_consolidation`, `schedule_lesson_consolidation`,
  `unschedule_lesson_consolidation`. No tool approves or rejects.
- **Classifier** ([`SpendControlDiff`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/spendcontrol/SpendControlDiff.java)),
  conservative by construction: a removed entry, any lower or cleared price, a looser policy, a raised or
  disabled quota loosen; a new entry is stricter only under `ALLOW` and only if it costs at least every
  existing entry of the same provider (or of provider `*`) — whatever the two model patterns. Unset prices are
  compared as metering charges them: an unset long-context cache price is the base cache price if set, else
  the tier's input price (not the base input price, which made a cut from 20 to 15 read as a raise from 10).
- **Pending changes** ([`SpendControlChangeService`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/spendcontrol/SpendControlChangeService.java)):
  stored with the whole proposed document and its base (catalog revision / stored quota); approval applies
  it only while the base is unchanged (else `STALE`, nothing applied). At most 20 pending, 7-day expiry,
  90-day retention, idempotent repeat. MongoDB `spendcontrolchanges`, PostgreSQL `spend_control_changes`.
  REST `/administration/spend-control-changes` (admin): list, read, approve, reject.
- **Approval claims, then writes.** `PENDING → APPLYING` by compare-and-set (of two concurrent approvals
  one wins, the other is `ALREADY_DECIDED`), then the write, then `APPLIED`, `STALE` or `FAILED`. A write
  that throws makes the change `FAILED` (terminal; the outcome text names only the exception type), not
  `PENDING` again. A change still `APPLYING` was interrupted mid-write (a crash): its outcome is unknown, it
  is never re-applied and retention keeps it.
- **Approval is human-only, and four-eyes with authentication.** Only an `eddi-admin` identity applies
  anything, and not the principal that proposed the change (`403 SELF_APPROVAL`, case-insensitive) — an MCP
  client acts with its user's credential, so this stops a steered model approving its own proposal over
  REST. Without authentication every caller is `anonymous` and the single operator still approves. The
  approve/reject paths join the `AGENT_ORIGINATED` request filter and the self-ungating guard; both now match
  the path without matrix parameters (`/spend-control-changes;x=1/c1/approve` routes to the same method), which
  also closes that gap for the existing `/hitl/samples`, `/hitl/autonomy` and `/hitl/analytics/rebuild` guards.
- **Compare-and-set for quotas.** `ITenantQuotaStore.compareAndSetQuota`, atomic in the MongoDB (filter on
  every stored field), PostgreSQL (one conditional statement) and in-memory stores; the MCP side reads a
  `fingerprint`. Creating the first quota of a tenant on MongoDB is an upsert keyed on `tenantId` alone with
  `$setOnInsert`, true only when it inserted — like every other write there that may insert.
- **Audit.** Every direct write, proposal and decision writes a `taskType: spendcontrol` ledger entry
  naming actor, reason, changes and the loosening parts.
- **Manager:** a *Pending changes* panel on Model Prices and Quotas (admins only) with Approve / Reject
  behind a confirmation; 11 locales. A failed first read shows an error with Retry (not an empty page); a
  selected change that someone else decided is dropped, so its dialog cannot reopen for a later proposal; on
  Quotas, Approve is disabled while the quota form has unsaved edits (Save sends the whole form, so it would
  overwrite the approved quota).
- **`list_team_cadences`** reports `"schedule": "missing"` only when the schedule does not exist; a schedule
  store failure fails the call instead of reading as a cadence that never fires.
- **Platform Operator:** may read `GET /administration/spend-control-changes` (never decide) and its prompt
  explains why a proposed price did not take effect. Revision 62 (61 is taken by three open PRs).
- **Claude Code plugin:** the `eddi-admin` skill teaches the new tools and the PROPOSED outcome.

### Design decisions

- **A new price under strict mode is a proposal.** The brief said "adding an entry applies"; under
  `REFUSE` / `REFUSE_WHEN_BUDGETED` a price *admits* calls refused today (even a $0.0001 price), so that is
  loosening. Under `ALLOW` a new entry still has to be at least as expensive as every entry of its
  provider — an exact cheap entry under a dear wildcard lowers that model's price, and so does an entry
  whose pattern overlaps nothing: a call is priced by the reported model name before the requested one, so
  a cheap `gpt-4o-2024*` takes over calls that the `gpt-4o` entry prices today. (Pattern-overlap
  detection was dropped for this reason.)
- **A failed write is `FAILED`, not `PENDING` again.** Whether it took effect is unknown; a later approval
  of a re-opened change would answer "nothing was applied", which may be false.
- **Four-eyes needs authentication.** With `authorization.enabled=false` principals cannot be told apart,
  so the check is skipped rather than blocking the single-operator deployment. With authentication, give
  the MCP client an account of its own, or have a second administrator approve.
- **Position is a price.** `ModelPriceMatcher` breaks a tie between same-provider globs of equal literal
  length by catalog order (`abc*` before `*xyz` prices `abcxyz`), and removing an entry and upserting it
  again moves it to the end. So `SpendControlDiff` also compares the relative order of kept globs whose
  order decides a match, and any such move is loosening — a note-only re-upsert can be a proposal for that
  reason alone. Two prefixes (or two suffixes) of equal length share no model unless equal, so their order
  is not judged.
- **Long-context prices are compared on every change of a tiered entry**, not only when the tier record
  changed: an unset tier cache price falls back to the base cache price, so setting only a base cache price
  (input 10, tier input 20, base cache read 15) lowers long-context cache reads from 20 to 15.
- **Approve and reject wait for the refetch.** The Manager's decision mutations return the invalidation
  promises, so the dialog stays open until the Quotas and Model Prices pages hold the approved document; a
  Quotas form edited in the gap would otherwise save the pre-approval quota over it.
- **Mixed writes are proposed whole**, not split, so an approver sees one coherent document and nothing
  half-applies.
- **Cadences need no proposal**: a run is skipped when the team budget is spent and charged to the tenant
  budget; consolidation is capped at $5 per run. Over MCP a cadence must still carry `maxCostPerRun`
  (≤ $100), narrower than REST.
- **Vault:** names, grants and references only (the existing REST listing, which already drops value and
  checksum); writing secrets stays off MCP.

### Not done / next

- No Slack or e-mail notification of a pending change yet (logged at `WARN`, metric
  `eddi.spendcontrol.changes`); the HITL Slack channel is conversation-bound and needs its own card type.
- The change stores have a shared contract (`SpendControlChangeStoreContract`: round trip, status filter
  and order, CAS under contention, retention), run in memory as a unit test and against real databases by
  `MongoSpendControlChangeStoreIT` / `PostgresSpendControlChangeStoreIT` in CI. The quota CAS still has no
  real-database test.
- The Manager shows only `PENDING` changes; an `APPLYING` or `FAILED` one is visible over REST and
  `list_spend_control_changes`, not yet on the page.
- Review fixes: the `eddi.spendcontrol.changes` series are pre-registered at zero so the first change after
  startup shows in the panel's `rate()`; `TenantQuotaService.quotaFor` publishes a cache fill only if no
  invalidation happened since it began, so a CAS tightening cannot be undone by an overlapping stale fill.

**Files:** [`engine/spendcontrol/`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/spendcontrol),
[`McpSpendControlTools`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpSpendControlTools.java),
[`McpTeamCadenceTools`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpTeamCadenceTools.java),
[`McpVaultTools`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpVaultTools.java) (tested by `McpVaultToolsTest`),
[`docs/spend-control-changes.md`](../spend-control-changes.md), [`docs/mcp-server.md`](../mcp-server.md).

## 🔍 feat(tools): Brave Search API as the `websearch` paid provider; Google Custom Search deprecated (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/websearch-paid-provider`)

### Why

The built-in `websearch` tool had one paid provider, `google`, which calls Google's Custom
Search JSON API. Google [closed that API to new customers and shuts it down on
2027-01-01](https://developers.google.com/custom-search/v1/overview), so a new deployment could
not get a key. The other provider, `duckduckgo`, calls DuckDuckGo's Instant Answer API. It needs
no key, but it returns an abstract and related topics, not a ranked results page, which is too
thin for research agents. (The docs said it did "HTML scraping"; it never did. Corrected.)

### What changed

- **`WebSearchTool`** gains `eddi.tools.websearch.provider=brave`, which calls the Brave Search
  API web endpoint (`https://api.search.brave.com/res/v1/web/search`) through `OutboundHttp`
  (same `HOPS_ONLY` SSRF policy, 15 s timeout and 2 MB body cap as the other providers). The key,
  `eddi.tools.websearch.brave.api-key`, may be plain or a `${vault:...}` reference resolved by
  `SecretResolver`. A reference the vault cannot resolve fails the search and is never sent. The
  key goes in the `X-Subscription-Token` header, never in the URL. Results come back in the same
  shape as Google's (`N. title / snippet / url`), with Brave's `<strong>` highlighting and
  entities stripped. The query is cut to Brave's 400-character limit.
- **`OutboundHttp.SENSITIVE_HEADERS`** now includes `x-subscription-token`, so a cross-origin
  redirect drops the Brave key. This covers httpcalls too.
- **Startup WARNs** (`WebSearchTool.configurationWarnings`, observed on `StartupEvent`):
  `provider=google` names the 2027-01-01 shutdown and the replacement. A paid provider without
  its key names the missing property and says searches go to DuckDuckGo. An unknown provider
  value is named. `google` itself still works for existing keys.
- **Default price** (`ToolCostTracker.defaultPriceOf`): `websearch` costs $0.005 per call when
  the configured provider is `brave` or `google` (both list $5 per 1,000 requests), and stays at
  $0.001 for `duckduckgo`. A per-agent `toolPricing` still wins. The price follows the
  *configured* provider, so a paid provider that fell back for lack of a key is still charged as
  paid. That errs on the safe side for budgets.
- **Price is configurable.** `eddi.tools.websearch.price-per-call` (dollars per call, empty by
  default) replaces the provider default, so a deployment on a different plan corrects the price
  without a rebuild; an invalid value (negative, non-finite, non-numeric) falls back to the
  provider default with a startup WARN. Per-agent `toolPricing` still wins over both. The
  `google` price rise ($0.001 to $0.005) is called out in
  [Upgrading](../upgrading-from-6.5.md#changes-after-670), with how to keep the old price.
- **Docs:** `langchain.md` (Web Search Tool section rewritten: provider table, Brave setup, terms
  note, Google deprecation; default-price line), `configuration-reference.md`, `docker.md`,
  `developer-quickstart.md`, `security.md` (pricing line), `README.md` feature table.
- **Platform Operator:** a web-search bullet in `system-prompt.ts` (provider property, what
  DuckDuckGo returns, Brave key, Google deprecation, default prices). The operator revision is
  bumped.
- **Tests:** `WebSearchToolProviderTest` (provider selection, the Brave request (header, URL,
  no key in the URI), result shape, the `maxResults` cap, query truncation, non-200, vault
  resolve and refuse, startup warnings before and after the shutdown date) and
  `ToolCostTrackerTest.WebSearchProviderPrice`. HTTP is mocked at `OutboundHttp`.

### Design decisions

- **Brave over Tavily, Exa and SerpAPI.** Prices checked 2026-11-09:
  - Brave *Search*: $5 per 1,000 requests, $5 of free usage a month
    ([brave.com/search/api](https://brave.com/search/api/)). Brave runs its own index and
    authenticates with a header key. Its terms allow use inside the customer's application, but
    forbid storing results beyond "transient storage required for operation" and training on them.
  - Tavily: $0.008 per credit pay-as-you-go, 1 credit per basic search, 2 per advanced search
    ([docs](https://docs.tavily.com/documentation/api-credits)).
  - Exa: $7 per 1,000 for Auto/Fast search, $4 for Lite/Instant ([exa.ai/pricing](https://exa.ai/pricing)).
  - SerpAPI resells scraped Google results, which carries its own terms risk.

  Brave is the cheapest of these with an independent index, and its response maps one-to-one
  onto the shape the tool already returns.
- **The conversation memory keeps tool results.** Whether that counts as "storage" under Brave's
  terms depends on how long a deployment keeps conversations. The docs say so and point at
  Brave's storage-rights plans, rather than EDDI dropping search results from memory.
- **Fallback stays silent at call time but loud at startup.** This matches how `google` already
  behaved without credentials. Failing every search on a missing key would break agents on a
  config typo.

### Next

- `searchNews` still appends " news" to a web search. Brave has a dedicated news endpoint, which
  could back `searchNews` when `provider=brave`.

### Review follow-up (2026-11-15)

- A Brave query is now cut to 75 words as well as 400 characters, so a short query with many
  one-letter words stays inside the API contract.
- `docker-compose.yml` passes `EDDI_TOOLS_WEBSEARCH_PROVIDER` and
  `EDDI_TOOLS_WEBSEARCH_BRAVE_API_KEY` into the container (documented in `.env.example` and the
  developer quickstart); before, setting them on the host left Compose on DuckDuckGo.
- The Platform Operator prompt now says that search pricing follows the configured provider even
  when a missing key makes the search run on DuckDuckGo, and states the precedence: the agent's
  `toolPricing`, then `eddi.tools.websearch.price-per-call`, then the provider default.

## ✨ feat(groups): approved-result actions — `phase.onApproved` runs one call with each human-approved task (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/group-approved-result-actions`)

### What changed and why

A member agent's approval-gated tool call is auto-rejected inside a group turn (`inGroupTurns: REJECT`), so a
team that may publish only human-approved text could not gate the posting call. The workaround was a separate
"Publisher" member re-posting the approved text in a later phase: an extra LLM turn per post, a judge to check it
did not reword the text, and phase plumbing to file publish tasks after the approval (a TASK pause resumes in the
same phase). The gate now sits where it belongs — on the draft — and the group itself makes the call:

```json
{ "name": "Draft", "type": "EXECUTE", "requiresApproval": true,
  "onApproved": { "action": { "type": "HTTPCALL",
                              "uri": "eddi://ai.labs.apicalls/apicallstore/apicalls/<id>?version=1",
                              "call": "postIssueComment" },
                  "onFailure": "FAIL_TASK" } }
```

- **Model** (`AgentGroupConfiguration`): `DiscussionPhase.onApproved` (`OnApprovedConfig` → `ApprovedAction`
  `{type, uri, call}`, `ApprovedActionFailurePolicy` `FAIL_TASK` default | `KEEP_COMPLETED`). A 13-argument
  compatibility constructor keeps every existing call site; the two full-copy sites (facilitator EXTEND_PHASE,
  NEGOTIATION arbitration repair) carry the field. `null` = nothing runs.
- **Record** (`SharedTaskList.approvedActions`, taskId → `ApprovedActionRecord`): status `PENDING` / `RUNNING` /
  `SUCCEEDED` / `FAILED` / `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` / `SKIPPED` / `NOT_SENT`, target, `onFailure`, approver, edit flag, idempotency
  key, timestamps, HTTP code, `html_url`/`url` of the response, a short redacted summary. Persisted with the
  discussion; old MongoDB/PostgreSQL documents load with an empty map.
- **Runner** (`GroupApprovedResultActions`, wired into `GroupHitlCoordinator` by a setter like the tie-break
  pauses): `recordApprovals` runs inside `resumeDiscussion` after the task approvals and before the resume CAS,
  for the tasks that went AWAITING_APPROVAL → COMPLETED in the paused phase; `runOwed` runs on the resumed leg
  (approval and human-input resumes) after the drift guards and **before** `executeDiscussion`, so a dependent
  never starts before its dependency's publish settled.
- **Executor:** the existing `IApiCallExecutor` (`ApiCallExecutor`), the path httpcall rules and httpcall LLM tools
  use — vault resolution, the reference guard, redaction, response caps, the agent-test mock registry — with a
  throwaway `ConversationMemory` (a group has no conversation of its own). Template data is `task.*`, `group.*`,
  `action.idempotencyKey`, nothing else. MCP-tool actions are not in v1 — one executor done properly, and the
  `type` field leaves room.
- **Validation** (`ApprovedResultActionValidation`, called from `AgentGroupStore.create/update`, `400` with the
  phase index and the fix): EXECUTE phase, `requiresApproval`, `hitlConfig.granularity: TASK`, `uri` an httpcalls
  URI with an explicit version, the call exists and makes one request (no fire-and-forget, batch, forEach), it has
  no `preRequest.propertyInstructions` (the executor would rebuild the template data from memory and drop
  `task.*`/`group.*`/`action.*`), the saver holds USE access to the httpcalls configuration, every `${vault:...}` is
  granted to all agents (`ServiceJudgeVaultGrants`, its message now names approved-result actions too) — checked on
  each field and on the URL as the executor joins it, so a reference split across `targetServerUrl` and the path is
  caught — and `${connection:}`, `${caller:}`, `${context:}` are refused. The reference half is re-checked before
  every send.
- **Audit/metrics:** with the audit ledger enabled, one ledger entry per settled action (`group.approved_action`,
  approver, target, status, result SHA-256 and length, HTTP code, URL); counter
  `eddi_group_approved_action_total{outcome}` on every settled action.
- **Docs:** new `docs/group-approved-result-actions.md` (in `SUMMARY.md`) with a Content Studio example posting an
  approved GitHub comment; pointers from `group-conversations.md` (per-phase controls), `hitl.md` (group config table,
  Group members) and `hitl-review-and-audit.md` (edited results). Platform Operator prompt gained the field
  (revision bump). Manager: the `DiscussionPhase` type declares `onApproved`; the phase editor already spreads
  unknown fields through, so a save keeps it — editing it stays JSON-only. MCP: `update_group` takes the full
  config JSON already; `create_group` builds phases from a style and needs nothing.

### Design decisions

- **At most once, honestly named.** The record is created in the approval's own state+pauseId CAS write (a retried
  approval loses it), claimed as RUNNING and persisted *before* the send, and a RUNNING
  record found later becomes OUTCOME_UNKNOWN and is never resent — the tool-call execution journal's contract.
  `{action.idempotencyKey}` lets a deduplicating target make it effectively once across a crash. The claim is a CAS
  on the discussion still being IN_PROGRESS, so a cancel stored first makes it fail; a claim that cannot be written
  sends nothing and settles the record SKIPPED (a cancel seen on this pod or read back from the store) or FAILED,
  rather than leaving it PENDING for a leg that may not come. The metric always counts that outcome; an audit entry
  carries it only when the audit ledger is enabled and its submission succeeds; the stored record shows it only if
  the follow-up write succeeds — a cancelled discussion refuses it, and then the next rule settles it.
- **An ended discussion owes nothing** (`GroupConversationStore.settleOwedApprovedActionsIfEnded` →
  `SharedTaskList.settleOwedApprovedActions`): on every read and write of a document in a terminal state (COMPLETED,
  FAILED, REJECTED, CANCELLED, CLOSED) a PENDING record becomes `NOT_SENT` with the state it ended in, and an orphaned
  RUNNING claim becomes OUTCOME_UNKNOWN. In the store because it is the one place every ending passes — the leg's own
  terminal writes, cancels by another pod, cadence reclaims and crash recovery (#1326) — on MongoDB and PostgreSQL
  alike. Never sent afterwards: a crash must not turn an approval into a publish nobody watched. No audit entry or
  metric for these (no leg is running). A read settles without persisting, so an ended document that is never
  written again is settled anew on every read: the finish time is the discussion's own `lastModified` (falling back
  to the record's claim or approval time), never `now()`, so every read — and the write that finally persists it —
  shows the same record; a read logs at DEBUG (the Manager polls), a write at INFO. Rejected: auto-sending on recovery (breaks at most once);
  calling a settle hook at each of the nine terminal-transition sites (one missed site, or #1326's, leaves it stuck).
- **A task is approved once per discussion; a re-approval, should one happen, owes a new attempt only when nothing
  was published.** In the configuration `onApproved` requires (TASK granularity, gated EXECUTE phase) no engine path
  returns an approved task to approval: `onTaskRejection: RETRY` resets only a task rejected while AWAITING_APPROVAL
  (`GroupHitlCoordinator` refuses a decision on any other status), and VERIFY rework is skipped when the EXECUTE phase
  gates tasks (`TaskForceEngine`). So a task whose action FAILED under `FAIL_TASK` stays FAILED for the rest of the
  discussion; the way to publish it after all is by hand, or — on a standing team — through the backlog in a later
  discussion. The docs, the failed-claim summary and the Platform Operator prompt used to say "send the task back
  (retry or rework) and approve it again", which no path does; they now say what works. `recordApprovedAction` still
  replaces a record whose status `allowsNewAttempt()` — FAILED, SKIPPED, NOT_SENT — and keeps every other:
  PENDING/RUNNING (still owed or in flight), SUCCEEDED (never sent twice), OUTCOME_UNKNOWN (never resent), and the new
  attempt reuses the **earlier record's idempotency key** rather than recomputing it, so a workspace that was
  unreadable at the first approval and readable at the second cannot give the same publish two keys.
- **Idempotency key from the stable identity.** A cadence discussion's task pulled from the standing team's backlog
  is keyed `sha256("backlog/" + groupId + "/" + backlogTaskId)` — the backlog task found the way writeback matches it
  (by subject among the workspace's `pulledTaskIds`, while `runningDiscussionId` is this discussion) — so a task that
  returns to the backlog after a FAILED or OUTCOME_UNKNOWN publish offers the target the same key in its next
  discussion. Only when the subject match is unambiguous on both sides — exactly one pulled backlog task and exactly
  one task of the discussion have it — since two publishes sharing a key would let a deduplicating target drop one.
  Other tasks (an ambiguous subject, an unreadable workspace) keep `sha256(conversationId + "/" + taskId)`. The
  workspace is read once per approval decision, and only when it records a task. At most once remains per
  discussion; across discussions only a deduplicating target makes it once (documented).
  The Platform Operator prompt says the same (revision 61, fingerprint updated).
- **"Approved by a person"** is one private helper, `approvedByPerson`, whose comment names the sibling PR #1374's
  `TaskApproval.byPerson()`: whichever merges second unifies the two.
- **Only human approvals publish.** A `system:*` approval (timeout `AUTO_APPROVE`) records SKIPPED.
- **Identity:** the call runs as the group (`runAs: group:<groupId>` in the audit entry) with author-written,
  all-agents secrets only; the approver is recorded, never impersonated.
- **Cost:** an HTTP call; tool pricing, tool rate limits and the cost ceiling do not apply (documented); bounded by
  one call per approved task.
- **Cadence writeback** (`TeamCadenceService`, not modified): under `FAIL_TASK` a failed publish leaves the task
  FAILED, so the run does not count it done; under `KEEP_COMPLETED` it stays COMPLETED.

### Rolling upgrade

No schema bump: the fields are additive. A pod older than this release ignores `onApproved` (approvals publish
nothing on it) and drops `approvedActions` when it re-saves a discussion, which can lose an owed (PENDING) action but
never sends one twice.

### Open

- A pod crash after the approval but before the claim leaves the record PENDING while the discussion stays
  IN_PROGRESS; it is sent only if a later leg runs, and reads NOT_SENT once the discussion ends (crash recovery
  cancelling it included).
- Retries configured on the referenced call (`postResponse.retryApiCallInstruction`) still apply and can repeat a
  request the target processed.
- No in-discussion way to retry a publish that ended FAILED: whether a person should be able to send such a task back
  for another approval (and how that interacts with at most once) is a product decision not taken here.

## 🐛 fix(httpcalls): a literal target the SSRF check refuses fails at deploy, not on the first turn (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/httpcall-target-check-at-deploy`)

### What changed and why

External security review, finding B1 (real, low). With
`eddi.security.ssrf-protection.enabled=true`, an httpcall whose literal
`targetServerUrl` the SSRF check refuses (`http://127.0.0.1:7070/...`) saved
with 201, deployed with 200, and only failed when the first conversation
started: `ApiCallExecutor.buildRequest` threw and `POST /agents/{id}/start`
answered a bare 500. At deploy, `ApiCallsTask.configure` only refused templated
hosts.

- **One decision, two callers.** The SSRF block of `buildRequest` is extracted
  unchanged into `ApiCallExecutor.checkOutboundTarget(URI, HostResolver)`;
  `buildRequest` calls it with the JDK resolver, so runtime behaviour is the
  same. The new `IApiCallExecutor.rejectRefusedLiteralTarget` runs it at deploy
  on the scheme and authority of `targetServerUrl` and of every absolute
  `request.path` (`ApiCallsTask.rejectRefusedTargets`, formerly
  `rejectTemplatedHosts`). A refusal is a `WorkflowConfigurationException`
  naming the host, the `host:port` entry to add to
  `eddi.security.ssrf-protection.allowed-hosts`, and the alternative of a
  public address. A cloud-metadata target fails to deploy whatever the flag,
  with the metadata message (the allow-list cannot admit it, so the advice
  would be wrong).
- **Skipped at deploy:** a templated host (still handled by
  `rejectTemplatedAuthority`) and a `${vars:…}`/`${vault:…}` host — both are
  only known per call, where the same check runs.
- **DNS:** a host that does not resolve at deploy is logged, not refused. The
  execution path still refuses it with protection on; but a failed lookup at
  deploy is as likely a transient resolver problem (agents auto-deployed at
  startup) as a wrong name, and an unresolvable name cannot be reached anyway.
  A blocked name (`localhost`, `*.internal`, `*.local`) is refused without a
  lookup, as at execution.
- **DNS lookups are bounded.** The check resolves the host (deploy and, as
  before, every call), and `InetAddress.getAllByName` has no timeout: a
  black-holed name server would hold the deploying or turn thread for the OS
  default. Both now go through `BoundedHostResolver` (small bounded daemon pool,
  deadline `eddi.security.ssrf-protection.dns-timeout`, default `5s`). A timeout
  or a full pool is an `UnknownHostException`, i.e. "does not resolve": refused
  at execution with protection on, logged at deploy, never allowed. No default
  of any security flag changes. New property documented in
  `configuration-reference.md`; `BoundedHostResolverTest` covers fast, slow,
  failing and saturated.
- **Upgrade note** added to `upgrading-from-6.5.md` (Changes after 6.7.0).
- **No self exception for httpcalls — documented, not changed.** The
  `application.properties` comment on `eddi.self.base-url` claimed loopback was
  always refused with protection on; it now says that `OutboundTargetPolicy`
  targets accept the self URL while httpcalls need the host on the allow-list.
  `OutboundTargetPolicy`'s Javadoc no longer says it applies the flag "the way
  ApiCallExecutor" does without naming that difference, and the LLM httpcall
  tool's refusal message (`HttpCallToolsProvider.describeToolFailure`) now
  names the allow-list as well as `eddi.self.base-url`.

The Platform Operator needed no change: its prompt and allow-list say nothing
about SSRF, and a refused deploy surfaces through the deployment status it
already reads.

**Files:** [`ApiCallExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallExecutor.java),
[`IApiCallExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/IApiCallExecutor.java),
[`BoundedHostResolver.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/net/BoundedHostResolver.java),
[`ApiCallsTask.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallsTask.java),
[`OutboundTargetPolicy.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/httpclient/OutboundTargetPolicy.java),
[`HttpCallToolsProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/HttpCallToolsProvider.java),
[`application.properties`](../../src/main/resources/application.properties),
[`security.md`](../security.md),
[`httpcalls.md`](../httpcalls.md),
[`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md),
[`ApiCallTargetDeployCheckTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallTargetDeployCheckTest.java),
[`ApiCallsTaskTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallsTaskTest.java)

## 🐛 fix(apicalls): redact encoded echoes of a substituted secret (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/redact-encoded-secret-echoes`)

### What changed and why

External security review finding B11 (low). Response redaction for httpcalls is by value: the
executor knows every plaintext it substituted into a request and removes it from the response
before the response reaches conversation memory, template data, the tool result or the log. It
removed exact occurrences only. A server that echoed the secret header back verbatim was redacted;
URL-encoded it was redacted only because the tester's value was alphanumeric; **base64-encoded it was
stored in conversation memory as it came**.

`SecretEncodings` (new, next to `SecretValueScrubber`) expands a set of secrets into the needle set
the scrubbers search for: every value of at least the minimum length also contributes its base64
(standard and URL-safe, padded and unpadded), percent-encoding (`+` or `%20`, upper or lower hex,
`~` escaped or literal, and the characters `URLEncoder` leaves literal — `*` `-` `.` `_` — left alone, only `*` escaped as `%2A`/`%2a`, or all four escaped), JSON string escaping (`/` escaped or not, non-ASCII literal or `\u`-escaped
in either case) and HTML entity forms, and a whole `Basic <base64>` credential also contributes its
decoded `user:password` and password. The set is built once per redaction and handed to the existing
scrubber, so the walk itself is unchanged.

`ApiCallExecutor` uses it in four places, each a one-line change in the helper section: the success
text and the long-secret tree pass (floor 8, `MIN_SUCCESS_BODY_REDACTION_LENGTH`), the short-secret
exact pass (floor 4, `MIN_SEARCHED_SECRET_INPUT_LENGTH` — whole-value matches only), response header
values and the error body (floor 8 for the encoded forms; the plaintext itself is still redacted at
every length there).

The error body goes through one helper, `redactErrorBody`, for a single call **and** for every
`forEach` item. The item path used to redact its error body with the plaintexts only, so an item
whose 401 echoed the key base64- or URL-encoded was stored with the echo while the same response to a
single call was redacted; `ApiCallExecutorForEachTest` now covers both forms and asserts the two
paths store the same text.

### Design decisions

- **Same floor for the forms as for the value.** Every encoded form is at least as long as the value
  it encodes, so the 8-character floor that keeps a short username from being rewritten inside
  ordinary text also bounds its encodings. A short secret's forms are matched only where a JSON value
  *is* one — the rule its plaintext already follows.
- **Not covered, documented instead:** reversed or transformed values, hex/base32/double encoding,
  and a secret inside a longer base64 blob (`base64(user:secret)` does not contain
  `base64(secret)`). The redaction protects later readers of a conversation from an accidental
  echo; it is not a defence against the target server.
- **Follow-up (review):** the per-call response log line (`RequestRedactor.safeResponseLog`: status message, body and headers) and the non-2xx status-message fallback now use the same encoded-form needles as the error-body path.
- The change lives in a new helper rather than in `SecretValueScrubber` or `SecretScrubber`, and the
  `ApiCallExecutor` edits stay in its helper section, away from open PRs touching those files.

**Files:** [`SecretEncodings.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/SecretEncodings.java),
[`ApiCallExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallExecutor.java),
[`SecretEncodingsTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/SecretEncodingsTest.java),
[`ApiCallExecutorEncodedEchoRedactionTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallExecutorEncodedEchoRedactionTest.java),
[`ApiCallExecutorConfigReferenceTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallExecutorConfigReferenceTest.java),
[`ApiCallExecutorForEachTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/apicalls/impl/ApiCallExecutorForEachTest.java),
[`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md)

## 🐛 fix(spaces): a space id that matches none of the caller's spaces no longer says "not a member" (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/space-errors-and-descriptor-patch`)

### What changed and why

External security review, findings B9 and B10 — both message-only. The checks
themselves are unchanged: still exact, still fail-closed.

- **B9 — `?space=` on `/spacestore/**`.** Space ids are opaque and may contain
  `%` (group `a|b` is `team:a%7Cb`). Sent raw, the id is decoded once by the
  container and arrives as `team:a|b`, which matches none of the caller's
  spaces — and the 403 told a member "You are not a member of that space." It
  now quotes the id as received (sanitised, capped at 200 characters) and says
  to send the id exactly as `GET /workspaces` lists it, URL-encoded with
  `encodeURIComponent` — what the Manager already does.
- **B10 — `X-EDDI-Space`.** The header is compared exactly and
  case-sensitively (Keycloak group names are case-sensitive), so
  `TEAM:engineering` or `team:engineering/` is refused — correctly — with
  "You are not a member of the space …". The refusal now says the value
  matches none of the caller's spaces, that the comparison is exact and
  case-sensitive with only surrounding whitespace ignored, and points at
  `GET /workspaces`.

Both texts live in one new class, `SpaceRefusals`. Neither lists the caller's
spaces: `GET /workspaces` already does, and an error body ends up in client
logs. The OpenAPI 403 descriptions on `IRestSpaceResources` say the same.
`docs/workspaces.md` documents the exact header match and the encoding of
`space`.

The Platform Operator quotes neither message (checked
`ui/manager/src/lib/operator/`), so its prompt and allow-list are unchanged.

**Files:**
[`SpaceRefusals.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/SpaceRefusals.java),
[`SpaceHeaderFilter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/SpaceHeaderFilter.java),
[`RestSpaceResources.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/rest/RestSpaceResources.java),
[`IRestSpaceResources.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/rest/IRestSpaceResources.java),
[`SpaceHeaderTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/SpaceHeaderTest.java),
[`SpaceResourcesTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/SpaceResourcesTest.java),
[`workspaces.md`](../workspaces.md)

## 🐛 fix(descriptors): PATCH refuses a document that sets anything but name and description (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/space-errors-and-descriptor-patch`)

### What changed and why

External security review, PATCH side note. `PATCH /descriptorstore/descriptors/{id}`
applies `name` and `description` only, and answered 204 to a document that also
set `visibility`, `spaceId`, `grants` or anything else — so a client making an
agent private was told it had, while its visibility stayed as it was. It now
answers **400** and writes nothing. The message names every unsupported field
and where it is changed instead: visibility, space, owner and grants under
`/descriptorstore/descriptors/{id}/shares/…`; `deleted` by a DELETE on the
resource's own store; `resource`, the timestamps, `originId`, `syncedVersion`
and `accessIndex` are maintained by EDDI. A DELETE patch is held to the same
rule, since it would not clear those fields either.

**How "set" is detected.** A declared field counts when it is non-null —
`deleted`, a primitive, when it is `true`; none of the others has a
deserialisation default, and every in-process caller builds its patch from an
empty `DocumentDescriptor`. A key the model does not declare at all (the
review's `resourceVisibility`) was dropped by the REST mapper's
`FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES=false`, so `DocumentDescriptor` gained a
`@JsonAnySetter` that records such keys — names only, capped at 100, never
serialised or stored, values still discarded — exposed as `unmodeledFields()`.
Stored descriptors with legacy fields load exactly as before. Undeclared keys
are echoed quoted, sanitised, shortened to 64 characters and at most ten
(`N more` for the rest). `callerLevel` and `ownerName` are read-only, and Jackson
hands such a property to the any-setter rather than dropping it; they are
skipped there, so a client echoing them is not refused for it.

**Caller audit.** Every PATCH of the descriptor store sends name and/or
description only: the Manager's `updateDescriptor` (nine call sites, the operator marker among them), MCP
`create_agent` / `update_agent`, `AgentSetupService`, and
`src/main/docker/seed-demo-agent.sh`. The Chat UI sends none, and the Platform
Operator's prompt says nothing about this body — its write canary renames the
operator, a name-only patch — so neither the prompt nor the allow-list changes.
`updateDescriptor`'s parameter type also allowed a `resources` key no caller
used and the server never had; it is gone. Unknown keys elsewhere in EDDI are
unaffected: the recording is on this model only, and only this endpoint reads it.

**Files:**
[`DocumentDescriptor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/descriptors/model/DocumentDescriptor.java),
[`RestDocumentDescriptorStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/descriptors/rest/RestDocumentDescriptorStore.java),
[`IRestDocumentDescriptorStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/descriptors/IRestDocumentDescriptorStore.java),
[`RestDocumentDescriptorStoreTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/descriptors/rest/RestDocumentDescriptorStoreTest.java),
[`descriptors.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/api/descriptors.ts),
[`developer-quickstart.md`](../developer-quickstart.md)

## 🐛 fix(openai-compat): the /v1 shared key works with OIDC on (2026-11-09)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/v1-shared-key-under-oidc`)

### What changed and why

Security review finding B7. With `quarkus.oidc.tenant-enabled=true` (for example `docker-compose.auth.yml`) and the default `eddi.openai-compat.http-policy=permit`, every `/v1` request carrying the shared key (`Authorization: Bearer sk-…`) got `401` with a `WWW-Authenticate` challenge from the OIDC layer, and `OpenAiAuthFilter` never ran. Requests without an `Authorization` header did reach the filter. Open WebUI cannot mint per-user OIDC tokens, so Keycloak + Open WebUI, the main production setup, could not work, although [the docs](../open-webui-integration.md) said it did.

**Cause.** `/v1/*` has its own `permit` permission so the key can reach the filter, but Quarkus authenticates proactively. Read from the Quarkus 3.40.1.1 sources (the version `pom.xml` pins): `HttpSecurityRecorder.AuthenticationHandler` runs `HttpAuthenticator.attemptAuthentication` before any permission policy is consulted, and an `AuthenticationFailedException` from it goes to `DefaultAuthFailureHandler`, which sends the challenge and a `401` whatever the policy says. `OidcAuthenticationMechanism` hands the bearer value to its bearer mechanism, which cannot verify `sk-…` as a token and throws. Without a header it returns no identity instead, which is why only that case got through.

**Fix.** A new `OpenAiPermitModeAuthMechanism` (an `HttpAuthenticationMechanism` bean). `HttpSecurityConfiguration.getMechanisms` sorts mechanisms by descending priority, and `HttpAuthenticator.createSecurityIdentity` stops at the first one that returns an identity when inclusive authentication is off. The new mechanism ranks one above `quarkus.oidc.priority` (default 1001) and, for a `/v1` request in permit mode, returns the anonymous identity. That is the identity `HttpSecurityRecorder` assigns to a request that carried no credential at all. OIDC is never asked about the key, the `permit` policy admits the request, and `OpenAiAuthFilter` checks the key: the right key is served, a wrong one or a JWT gets the adapter's `401 invalid_api_key`. The mechanism verifies nothing and grants nothing; anyone can be anonymous by leaving the header out.

It fails closed. It returns no identity, and OIDC runs as before, unless all of these hold: the adapter is enabled; `eddi.openai-compat.http-policy` **and** the effective `quarkus.http.auth.permission.openai-compat.policy` are both `permit`; `quarkus.http.auth.inclusive` is off; the method is one the permission lists; and both path forms Quarkus matches permissions against (the router's `normalizedPath()` and `HttpSecurityUtils.normalizePath` of it) are `/v1` or below it. With `http-policy=authenticated` nothing changes: OIDC validates the JWT and the filter checks the role. No other path's authentication changes, and proactive authentication stays on.

`OpenAiStartupGuard` now warns at startup when OIDC is on, the adapter is in key mode, and the mechanism cannot be active (inclusive authentication, an overridden `/v1` permission, or an unknown `http-policy`). In those cases every shared-key request would still get the OIDC `401`.

### Design decisions

- **Not an OIDC tenant.** A `TenantConfigResolver` returning a disabled tenant for `/v1`, or a named disabled tenant chosen by a `TenantResolver`, would also stop the verification (`OidcAuthenticationMechanism.authenticate` returns no identity for a disabled tenant). But in quarkus-oidc either one changes how the *default* tenant starts. `TenantContextFactory.createTenantContext` silently disables a default tenant with no `auth-server-url` once a `TenantConfigResolver` bean or a named tenant exists, where that is a startup failure today. A named static tenant is also selected by `DefaultStaticTenantResolver` for any request path with a segment equal to its id.
- **Not a per-path `auth-mechanism`.** It would need a config value that switches between this mechanism and `bearer` with `http-policy`, and a mechanism that names a credential transport.
- **The key is not checked in the mechanism.** The filter already checks it, in constant time, and answers in the OpenAI error envelope. A second check would mean two places to keep in step.

### Tests

- `OpenAiPermitModeAuthMechanismTest`: activation (each fail-closed condition on its own), path and method matching including disagreeing normalizations, the anonymous identity, no challenge, priority above OIDC, a chain that reproduces `HttpAuthenticator`'s ordering and shows the OIDC mechanism is never called for a `/v1` key request, and a pin on the shipped `openai-compat` permission in `application.properties`.
- `OpenAiStartupGuardTest`: the new warning condition.
- The Keycloak Auth E2E tier (`ui/manager/docker-compose.integration-keycloak.yml`) now enables the adapter with a shared key. [`e2e/auth/openai-compat.spec.ts`](../../ui/manager/e2e/auth/openai-compat.spec.ts) covers: the key is served on `/v1/models`; a full chat completion runs end to end; a wrong key, no key and a Keycloak JWT each get the adapter's `401 invalid_api_key` with no challenge; the key on a non-`/v1` path still gets OIDC's `401`. These are the regression tests against a real OIDC provider.

**Files:** [`OpenAiPermitModeAuthMechanism.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/openai/OpenAiPermitModeAuthMechanism.java), [`OpenAiStartupGuard.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/openai/OpenAiStartupGuard.java), [`OpenAiAuthFilter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/openai/OpenAiAuthFilter.java) (Javadoc), [`application.properties`](../../src/main/resources/application.properties) (comments), [`open-webui-integration.md`](../open-webui-integration.md), the two test classes, the E2E spec and compose file.

The Platform Operator needs no change: neither its prompt nor its allow-list covers the `/v1` adapter, which is deployment configuration.

## ♻️ refactor(llm): one AbandonableStreamCall for both streaming skeletons (2026-11-08)

**Repo:** EDDI (`refactor/abandonable-stream-call`)

### What changed and why

The legacy streaming path (`StreamingLegacyChatExecutor`) and the tool loop's streaming bridge (`ToolLoopStreamingChatModel`) each carried their own copy of the same concurrency skeleton: a latch per call, an `abandoned` gate, a lock that makes recording, forwarding and abandoning mutually exclusive, and the await with a timeout. The legacy copy was marked "KEEP IN SYNC" with the other, which is the only thing that kept a concurrency fix in one from missing the other.

- **New `AbandonableStreamCall`** — the one implementation. A fresh instance per call is the provider's `StreamingChatResponseHandler`; `await(seconds)` abandons the call before it returns on a timeout or an interrupt, `abandon()` covers a retried error, `forwardedText()` reads under the lock, and `runUnlessAbandoned` covers the legacy path's single-chunk fallback emit. Both callers keep their own policy: what an interrupt means (legacy salvages or throws, the tool loop throws), the timeout metric, the separator between tool-loop rounds, and the log wording.
- **The below-backstop `timeout` warning is no longer static state.** Its warn-once key set moved into an instance helper owned by the executor (one executor per `LlmTask`, shared with the cascade, so still once per process in production), and the two test-only static hooks (`shortTimeoutWarningCount`, `resetShortTimeoutWarnings`) are gone — the tests count the WARN lines instead.
- **`resolveTimeoutSeconds` got its Javadoc back.** The precedence rules for `timeout` against `streamingTimeoutSeconds` sat above a second Javadoc block and documented nothing. The static method is now the pure resolution (the cascade's step margin uses it and logs nothing); requests go through the instance method `backstopSeconds`, which also warns. `LlmTask`'s tool-loop bridge uses `backstopSeconds`, so that path still warns as before.

Behaviour is unchanged: the same text reaches the sink and memory, the same exceptions, metadata, metrics and log lines.

**Files:** [`AbandonableStreamCall.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/AbandonableStreamCall.java), [`StreamingLegacyChatExecutor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/StreamingLegacyChatExecutor.java), [`ToolLoopStreamingChatModel.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopStreamingChatModel.java), [`LlmTask.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/LlmTask.java), [`AbandonableStreamCallTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/AbandonableStreamCallTest.java), [`StreamingLegacyChatExecutorTimeoutTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/StreamingLegacyChatExecutorTimeoutTest.java)

### Design decisions

- **A handler object, not a template method.** The two callers differ in everything after the await (retry and salvage versus a thrown exception the tool loop's retry classifies), so the shared part is the call's state and its gate, and each caller keeps its control flow.
- **`abandoned` is a plain field under the lock.** Both copies only ever read and wrote the `AtomicBoolean` inside the lock, so the atomic added nothing.
- **Not unified here:** the timeout counter is still incremented from the executor's injected `MeterRegistry` on one path and from `Metrics.globalRegistry` on the other. Moving the tool loop onto the injected registry changes which registry a deployment sees the `tool_loop` series in, which is a behaviour change for a separate PR.

## 🧪 refactor(llm): chat/streaming parity test for every provider builder (2026-11-08)

**Repo:** EDDI (`refactor/builder-option-appliers`)

Every provider builder configures two langchain4j clients, a synchronous one and a streaming one, and the two builder types
share no supertype, so each builder applied its parameters twice, in two method bodies that differed only in the retry
setting. Nothing failed when one of them was edited and the other was not: only Anthropic had a test comparing the two, and
the whole builder package was excluded from the coverage gate.

### What changed and why

- **`ProviderChatStreamingParityTest`** builds both clients of every provider (OpenAI, the eight OpenAI-compatible
  presets, Anthropic, Mistral, Ollama, Gemini, Azure OpenAI and Bedrock) from parameter maps that together set every key the
  builder recognises, and asserts that the two report equal `defaultRequestParameters()` and send the same first request:
  URL, headers, JSON body without the streaming switch, and client timeouts. A key added to `recognisedParameters()`
  without a value in the test fails it, so the check cannot silently fall behind. Bedrock's AWS client cannot be intercepted
  offline and is compared on its request parameters only.
- **Streaming test seams** for OpenAI, Mistral and Ollama (`buildStreaming(…, HttpClientBuilder)`), matching the ones
  Anthropic and Gemini already had, and an `HttpClientProvider` seam for both Azure clients. Production passes the same
  clients as before.
- **`RecordingHttpClient`** records the connect and read timeouts the provider client configures, so the timeout is compared
  too.
- **JaCoCo** now excludes only the two builders whose clients cannot be built offline (Vertex: GCP credentials; OCI:
  `~/.oci/config`), instead of the whole package on the stale ground that it had no logic and needed live keys. The package
  holds the parameter catalog, the value parsing and the bounded HTTP client, all unit-tested.
- The OpenAI and Azure Javadoc no longer claims the two clients "cannot drift"; the test is what checks it.

**Files:** [`ProviderChatStreamingParityTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/ProviderChatStreamingParityTest.java),
[`RecordingHttpClient.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/RecordingHttpClient.java),
[`OpenAILanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/OpenAILanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`MistralAiLanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/MistralAiLanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`OllamaLanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/OllamaLanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`AzureOpenAiLanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/AzureOpenAiLanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`pom.xml`](../../pom.xml)

## ♻️ refactor(llm): provider request parameters built once for both clients (2026-11-08)

**Repo:** EDDI (`refactor/builder-option-appliers`)

With the parity test in place, the request-level settings of each provider builder are now built once, into langchain4j's
own request-parameter type for that provider, and handed to the synchronous and the streaming client through
`defaultRequestParameters(...)`. langchain4j starts every request from those, so the two clients cannot send different
sampling, token, stop, seed, caching or thinking settings any more; what each client builder still receives on its own is
the short list of options that are not part of a request (endpoint, credentials, timeout, logging, headers) or that the
library only accepts there.

### What changed and why

- **OpenAI** (and through it the OpenAI-compatible presets): `OpenAiChatRequestParameters` carries model, sampling,
  token limits, stop, seed, logit bias, reasoning effort, service tier, parallel tool calls, store, metadata, user, prompt
  cache key and the custom body fields. `strictTools`, `strictJsonSchema` and the `json_object` response format stay on
  the client builders.
- **Anthropic**: `AnthropicChatRequestParameters` carries model, `maxTokens`, sampling, stop, thinking type and budget,
  the thinking switches, the cache flags and TTL, `disableParallelToolUse` and `userId`. `thinkingDisplay`, `strictTools`,
  version, beta and the custom body fields stay on the client builders, which are the only place langchain4j takes them.
- **Mistral**: `MistralAiChatRequestParameters` carries everything except `strictJsonSchema` and the connection options.
- **Ollama**: the two hand-kept `applyTo` twins are gone; the parameters are read once into a `Settings` record whose
  `OllamaChatRequestParameters` carries the sampling and context options, `think` and `truncate`.
- **Gemini**: the two `applyExtras` twins are replaced by one `read` into a `Settings` record, so the thinking-config and
  media-resolution parsing (and their warnings) is written once instead of twice. Model, sampling, token limit,
  stop and the cached-content name travel as `GoogleAiGeminiChatRequestParameters`; the rest (seed, thinking config,
  tools, safety settings, media resolution) are options only the client builders take.
- **Azure OpenAI**: Azure has no request-parameter type of its own, so the common sampling settings travel as
  `DefaultChatRequestParameters` and the Azure-specific ones stay on the client builders.
- **Bedrock** is unchanged: it already built one `BedrockChatRequestParameters` for both clients.

No configuration key, default or wire field changes. The requests both clients send, and the models' default request
parameters, were compared field by field before and after the change for every provider, with a full and a minimal
parameter map, and are identical.

**Files:** [`OpenAILanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/OpenAILanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`AnthropicLanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/AnthropicLanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`MistralAiLanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/MistralAiLanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`OllamaLanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/OllamaLanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`GeminiLanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/GeminiLanguageModelBuilder.java),
[`AzureOpenAiLanguageModelBuilder.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/builder/AzureOpenAiLanguageModelBuilder.java)

## 🐛 fix(configs): one endpoint-parameter rule; brand SVG refuses CSS escapes (2026-11-08)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/endpoint-params-brand-svg`)

### What changed and why

**One answer to "does this parameter name an endpoint?"** Five places asked it, each with its own list, and the lists had drifted. The LLM registry knew Vertex's `apiEndpoint`; the embedding check knew `apiBase`, `api_base` and `apiUrl`; the export merge (`ScrubbedSecrets`) knew neither `apiEndpoint` nor `apiBase`. The merge's list matters most: it decides whether a target's hidden literal credential is put back into a synced config, and it withholds the credential when the config's destination changed. A synced config that changed only `apiEndpoint` or `apiBase` therefore had the target's literal key restored next to an endpoint the editor chose. No shipped builder reads either key together with an `apiKey` today (Vertex uses ADC), so this was latent, but the next builder that does would have opened it.

[`EndpointParameters.isEndpointKey`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/utils/EndpointParameters.java) is now the one rule: case-insensitive, any key ending in `url`, plus `uri`, `endpoint`, `apiEndpoint`, `apiBase`, `api_base`, `host` and `hostname`. The rule is the union of the old lists. `ScrubbedSecrets`, `ChatModelRegistry` (the `allowedHosts` check), `EmbeddingParameters` (refusing an endpoint the provider ignores) and `EmbeddingModelFactory` (its `allowedHosts` check) and `LlmTask` (which parameters a conversation summary on a different provider may not inherit) all use it.

Behaviour changes, all in the refusing direction:
- **Agent sync / upgrade:** a change to any of these keys now keeps the `${vault:REDACTED}` placeholder instead of restoring the target's literal credential.
- **LLM `allowedHosts`:** every endpoint parameter that is set is checked against a referenced secret's list. That covers `apiBase`, `hostname`, `uri` and any `…Url`, not only the six keys listed before.
- **Embedding parameters:** an endpoint parameter the provider does not honour is refused under more spellings (`apiEndpoint`, `hostname`, `uri`, `proxyUrl`, …), and with a restricted secret it is reported as an `allowedHosts` refusal first, as the factory's comment already said.
- **Conversation summary on another provider:** every endpoint parameter of the task is withheld from the summarizer, not only `baseUrl`, `endpoint` and `apiEndpoint`, so a summary provider that reads, say, `apiBase` never sends its own key to the task provider's host.

**Brand SVGs refuse backslashes.** [`BrandAssetValidator`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/branding/BrandAssetValidator.java) checked `url(` and `@import` as literal text. CSS decodes escapes, so `u\72l(http://…)` and `@\69mport "…"` load external resources while matching neither check. Any backslash now refuses the SVG. The check covers the whole document, not only `<style>` and `style="…"`, because every presentation attribute (`fill`, `filter`, `mask`, …) is parsed as CSS too. Finding where an element really ends would also need a parser that agrees with the browser's (CDATA, end tags). `image-set()` is refused as well, since it loads an image from a plain string without `url()`. An editor that writes a Windows path into its metadata (Inkscape's `inkscape:export-filename`) now trips the check, and the refusal message says to export a plain or optimized SVG.

**Upgrade note:** the images in `eddi.branding.assets-dir` go through the same checks at startup, and a refused file fails startup. A seeded SVG containing a backslash (or `image-set(`) that started before this change now stops the deployment from starting, with an error naming the file; re-export it as a plain or optimized SVG before upgrading. An SVG already uploaded through the API is not re-checked.

**Docs:** [White-labeling](../white-labeling.md), [Agent sync](../agent-sync-guide.md) and [Secrets vault](../secrets-vault.md) list the new rules.

### Tests

`EndpointParametersTest`; `ScrubbedSecretsDestinationTest` (every endpoint spelling is a destination; an ordinary parameter is not); `ChatModelRegistryAllowedHostsTest` (`apiBase`, `hostname` and `proxyUrl` are checked); `EmbeddingParametersBranchTest` (`apiEndpoint` on Vertex, `hostname` on Bedrock); `LlmTaskSummaryCredentialIsolationTest` (every endpoint spelling stays with the task's provider); `VaultAllowedHostsCoverageTest` (an unsupported endpoint key is judged by `allowedHosts`); `BrandAssetValidatorTest` (escaped `url(`/`@import` in `<style>`, CDATA, `style` and presentation attributes; `image-set()`; editor metadata refused with the way out).
