## ✨ feat(ui): readable tool calls and edit-on-approve for gated calls in every chat (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/chats-670-tool-display-and-hitl`)

### What changed and why

Every chat surface and approval card named tools by their identifier (`getAgentDescriptors`,
`add_issue_comment`) and showed gated-call arguments as a JSON blob, and none of them used what 6.7
sends with a live tool call: the SSE `tool_call` event now carries the designer's `toolDisplay` label
(`displayName`) and up to three allow-listed values (`display`), and a `tool_result` event follows each
call. Amending a gated call's arguments meant retyping the whole object.

- **One helper per app.** [`tool-labels.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/tool-labels.ts) (Manager) and
  [`tool-labels.ts`](../../ui/packages/core/src/tool-labels.ts) (Chat UI; since moved into `@eddi/core`) turn an identifier into words
  (`getAgentDescriptors` → "Get agent descriptors", `fetchHTTPStatus` → "Fetch HTTP status") and leave
  text with spaces (a designer label) as written. The Manager's also parses the `tool_call` payload and
  builds label/value rows from tool arguments, reusing the lossless parse of `hitl-format-args.ts`.
- **Live status lines** (Manager chat page and drawer, Platform Operator, Chat UI): "Using Get agent
  descriptors…", or the designer's phrase on its own ("Creating an action: Renew the contract…").
  `tool_result` stops the spinner of the call that finished (Manager) or returns the widget to
  "Thinking…" (Chat UI). Whether a status line is an identifier or a designer phrase is decided where
  the `tool_call` event is parsed and carried as `{ text, phrase }` — it is never re-derived from the
  string's shape, so a one-word `toolDisplay` label ("Searching") is not wrapped in "Using …" and a
  prefixed MCP name (`github.search-code`) is still put into words. An event without `displayName`
  or `display` (pre-6.7 backend) is an identifier, as before.
- **Approval card** (`approval-banner.tsx`, every surface that decides: conversation page, Approvals
  inbox, Platform Operator): each gated call shows its name in words with the exact identifier beside
  it, and its arguments as label/value rows by default, with JSON and Raw one click away (a generated
  tool's nested `requestBody` string is expanded into rows). The confirmation dialog, the decision
  receipt, the pause notice and the Approvals inbox list name tools in words; the inbox search matches
  both forms.
- **Edit-on-approve for gated calls**: "Amend arguments" now opens pre-filled with the call's arguments
  pretty-printed as sent (nested strings are not expanded, so a string parameter keeps its type). A copy
  that differs only in whitespace is a plain approval. An amendment that still contains `<REDACTED>` is
  refused before submit: the REST resume runs `amendedArguments` verbatim (only Slack merges against the
  raw arguments), so the marker would otherwise become the value.
- **Group transcripts**: "using {tool}…" names the tool in words.
- **Platform Operator prompt** (revision 60 → 61): `toolDisplay`, httpcall `argumentSchema` /
  `unknownFields`, and `answerMode: "tool"` were missing; an admin asking how to make tool calls read
  well, or why a call was refused before approval, now gets an answer. No allow-list or guard change.
- **Mocks**: `conv-toolcall-1` serves a TOOL_CALL pause shaped like `buildToolCallPauseDetails` (a
  generated tool with a JSON-string body and a redacted secret, plus an MCP tool) for MSW mode.
- Chat UI `AGENTS.md`: the backend-contract notes on `tool_call` and the event list are brought up to 6.7.

### Per-surface feature table

| Surface | Tool names in words | toolDisplay label | tool_result | Args as fields | Edit gated call (pre-filled) | Edit rule-pause answer | Reason codes | Approval context |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Manager chat page / drawer | new | new | new | new (activity details) | via conversation page | via conversation page | via conversation page | via conversation page |
| Conversation page + Approvals inbox (ApprovalCard) | new | n/a | n/a | new | new (was blank) | already | already | already |
| Platform Operator chat | new | new | new | new | new (was blank) | n/a (tool gate) | shown, not sent (see below) | n/a |
| Group / Workforce transcripts | new | event carries name only | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | already | already |
| Chat UI widget | new | new | new | n/a (no arguments on the wire) | n/a (read-only by design) | n/a | n/a | n/a |

### Not in this change

- **Group task and synthesis edits** (`taskDecisions[].editedResult`, `decision.editedAnswer` on a
  SYNTHESIS phase) stay REST/MCP: the group `approval-status` exposes neither whether
  `review.allowResultEdits` is on nor the results to edit, so the Manager cannot offer it honestly.
- `member_tool_call` (group streams) carries only the tool name, so group lines cannot use `toolDisplay`.
- The Chat UI remains read-only on a pause (no approve/edit for end users).
- The operator chat's `onDecide` wrapper drops the banner's `review` argument, so a reason code picked
  on an operator approval card is not sent. Left as is: the operator's resume path is security-reviewed
  and this change stays UI-only there.

## 🐛 fix(cluster): presence age on this node's clock; drain key and duplicate cleanup (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/cluster-presence-clock`)

### What changed and why

**Presence ages are measured on this node's own clock (OPS-1).** The presence watch stamped each
record with the NATS server's write time and expired it against this node's wall clock after the
30 s bucket TTL. A node whose clock ran about 20 s ahead therefore dropped every member between two
rewrites, and `members()`, `currentMembers()` and the RPC peer list came back empty. Four staleness
checks compared another node's `updatedAt` with this node's clock against two presence intervals
(20 s): the drain `LAST_NODE` guard, the drained node's own check of a drain request over RPC, the
overview's `STALE` card, and the watcher's `node.stale` entry. Skew above about 10 s was enough to
refuse a legitimate drain or to report a live node as stale.

`ClusterPresence` now records, for each record, the revision it saw and when it saw that revision on
its monotonic clock. Expiry uses that time, and the new `heartbeatAgeMillis(nodeId)` reports it.
The four checks use that age. A record that was already in the bucket when this node started looking
(the watch's initial values, the first bucket read, or a read more than one TTL after the previous
one) was written at an unknown moment, so it has no age until it is rewritten. The drain guards count
such a node as not serving, so they refuse rather than drain the last live node. The overview shows
it `LIVE` with the age its own timestamp gives, and the watcher does not report it. `updatedAt`
stays in the record and in the overview as `lastHeartbeat`, for display only. `node.stale` is still
keyed by `updatedAt`, so every node's report of one stale record collapses into one entry.

**The drain key of a decision being applied survives the node's own key sync (OPS-9).** `drain()`
opens `gate.drain`, writes `drain.<node>` and then asks the node over RPC. If the node's scheduled
`syncOwnDrainKey` ran in between, it saw a key for a node that was not draining yet and deleted it.
The node then refused the request as "not recorded", and the administrator got `409 NODE_FAILED`. The
sync now reads the key's revision first, then skips the delete while the drain gate is held, and
deletes only at that revision. A key written after the read is either covered by a gate the check
sees or carries a newer revision, and the conditional delete then fails. The drain flag is read
again just before the delete, so a drain applied on the node while the sync ran — the gate already
closed again after the reply — keeps its key too. A leftover key from a drain that a restart ended
is still removed on the next sync.

**Dead code (OPS-18).** `ClusterPresence.duplicateDetail()` had no caller; the same detail is in the
ERROR log. `KvLeaseManager.snapshot(prefix, max)` listed `max * 2 + 16` entries before filtering by
prefix, so a real prefix could miss matching leases. Its only production caller passed `""`, so it is
now `snapshot(max)`. `heartbeatAll()` is kept: nine tests use it.

**Tests (OPS-20, the presence part).** Skewed-clock tests for presence expiry and heartbeat age, the
drain guards, the overview and the watcher. The tests of the drain-key sync include one where the
key is rewritten between the sync's read and its delete.

**Files:** [`ClusterPresence.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ClusterPresence.java),
[`ClusterAdminService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/admin/ClusterAdminService.java),
[`ClusterWatcher.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/admin/ClusterWatcher.java),
[`KvLeaseManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/lease/KvLeaseManager.java),
[`coordinator-admin.md`](../coordinator-admin.md)

## ✨ feat(workforce): search my conversations, tool cards, and the tool notice before Edit and Regenerate (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/conversation-search-tool-ui` (`feat/workforce-tier1-ux` with `feat/conversation-search-tool-events` merged in)

The rest of phase 7 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §5, T1.3, T1.6 and T1.9), on the backend's B3 (`GET /conversationstore/conversations/search`) and B6 (the `tool_result` stream event). Frontend only.

### What changed and why

- **Search my conversations (T1.3).** A search box at the top of the Workforce sidebar's conversations ([`conversation-search.tsx`](../../ui/workforce/src/components/workforce/conversation-search.tsx)): a combobox whose results replace the date-grouped list while it holds text, **↑ ↓** and **Enter** to open, **Esc** to clear. A line under it says it searches **titles only** (tied to the box with `aria-describedby`), and a polite status says how many it found once a search settles, or that none matched, or that it failed (with Retry). [`use-conversation-search.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/hooks/use-conversation-search.ts) holds the request discipline: the text normalized as the backend stores titles and capped at `MAX_CONVERSATION_SEARCH_LENGTH` (also the box's `maxLength`), a 300 ms debounce, one `AbortController` per request (a new text aborts the old request, and a late answer is dropped), and **More results** until a page comes back empty — only an empty page ends the list, since the server drops unreadable rows from a page after paging, so a short page is not the end. A page whose matches were all left out here (member conversations) reads on by itself rather than saying that nothing matches. `searchConversations` takes an `AbortSignal` now (it uses the client's authenticated fetch; `api.get` takes no signal). Member conversations of a team session are left out, as in the list. The **⌘K quick switcher** adds the title matches it has not already shown, below the local ones, with the same "titles only" line.
- **No Manager surface.** The Manager's conversation pages list every user's conversations for an administrator, and its chat history one agent's; an owner-only title search fits neither, so search is Workforce only.
- **Tool activity as cards (T1.9).** A new entry, [`@eddi/conversation/tool-activity`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/tool-activity/index.ts): `ToolActivityLog` files each `tool_result` under its conversation and step (`beginTurn` from `onTurnStarting` empties the turn's step and every later, undone one; `clearStep` after a rerun, which has no stream; at most 50 calls per turn, the rest counted; 20 conversations). `ToolActivityCards` shows a turn's calls as one disclosure under its **question** — collapsed, "Tools used: 2 · 1 failed"; open, a card per call with the tool, whether it worked, and the summary. **The summary is a React text child, never markdown or HTML**: a test feeds it `<img onerror>`, `<b>`, `**…**` and a link and finds no element. The Manager/Workforce chat (`ChatPanel`, through `chatToolActivity` in `use-chat.ts`) and the member thread wire `@eddi/core/tool-events` into their controllers.
- **Live only.** The detailed snapshot holds the raw tool trace under `langchain:trace:*`, which the server redacts (`ToolTraceRedaction`) only on the way out through the stream and `task_complete`. Restoring cards from it would show unredacted tool results, so nothing is restored: a reload, a conversation opened later, or a turn answered without streaming shows no cards. A rerun (REST, no stream) clears its step's cards rather than leave the first run's.
- **The tool notice (T1.6).** `ChatPanel`'s `renderTurnNotice` now defaults to `ToolTurnNotice`: beside **Edit** and **Regenerate** of a last turn that ran tools, an inline warning names them ("Ran send_email. Editing takes back the reply, not what the tools did." / "… What they did stays, and a new reply may run them again."), tied to the button with `aria-describedby`. A host's own `renderTurnNotice` still replaces it. After an Edit of such a turn, the same point is made once more as a toast, since the undo takes the warning off the screen with the turn. **An inline warning, not a confirm step:** the effects already happened, so a dialog cannot prevent anything; an Edit only puts the text back in the composer, and nothing runs again until the user sends; the warning is visible before the click (and before **↑**), where a confirm on every edit of every tool turn would train people to click through it.
- **The widget does not grow.** It is at 169.4 of 170 KB gz and shows no tool activity, so it imports neither the new entry nor `@eddi/core/tool-events`; a test reads `ui/chat/src` and fails if it ever does. Lazy-loading the cards into the widget was rejected: even the loader and the listener would cost bytes it does not have, for a surface the embedding contract never promised. `docs/chat-ui-embedding.md` is unchanged.
- **Strings.** `toolActivity.*` (10 keys) in the package's own locale files (`withToolActivityLocales`, merged by `setupAppI18n`), `Workforce.search.*` (12) and `Workforce.switcher.searchFailed` in Workforce's, all in the eleven locales. The tool strings are their own bundle rather than `conversation.*`: that namespace's English is bundled into the widget.
- **Platform Operator.** The prompt now says how Workforce and the Manager's chat show `tool_result` (cards, the notice, live only, none in the widget), which an admin asked about a missing card would need. No allow-list change. `operator-revision.json` stays at **56** (set by the merge, unreleased) with the new fingerprint.
- **Docs.** [`workforce-guide.md`](../workforce-guide.md) (searching, what the agent's tools did, the warning on Edit and Regenerate, the keyboard table, two administrator notes), `ui/manager/AGENTS.md`, `ui/workforce/AGENTS.md`, the `@eddi/conversation` README.
- **Test fix.** The quick switcher's "filters … opens a chat with an agent" test read an agent row before the sessions loaded and filled the 30 rows; it now reaches the agent by name.

### Design decisions

- Titles only, as the backend decided (B3); the UI says so at every place it offers the search.
- Any non-empty page means "maybe more"; only an empty page ends the list. The backend drops rows from a page after reading it (a match whose memory is gone), so a short page is not the end — treating it as one hid later matches. The cost is one empty extra request at the end of a list.
- Cards go under the turn's question, so they read in the order things happened: the question, the tools it set off, the reply.

### Not done

- Restoring cards after a reload: needs a client-readable, redacted trace on the snapshot (a backend item).
- Tool cards for group discussions: the backend sends no `member_tool_result` yet.
- Searching outputs: the backend's follow-up (privacy review first).

## 🐛 fix(conversations): the chat panel resumes only the caller's own conversation (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/conversations-mine-flag`)

### What changed and why

The Manager's chat panel opens an agent by resuming the newest `READY` or `AWAITING_HUMAN` conversation
the listing returns. An administrator's listing holds **every user's** conversations, so an admin who
opened an agent an end user had talked to more recently landed in that user's conversation, and the
next message ran in it as that user: their tools, property writes and long-term memory. The admin
passes the per-conversation ownership check, so nothing stopped it. The operator's "restore my last
investigation" had the same shape: a fresh tab restored another admin's newest operator conversation,
pending approval card included, and its History list showed and opened other admins' investigations.

The Manager cannot filter this by itself: a conversation's owner is the principal name the backend
resolved, which the Manager does not reliably know. So the listing gained a flag:

- **`GET /conversationstore/conversations?mine=true`** lists only the caller's own conversations, for
  every caller: an administrator, and an agent's reviewer (who otherwise also sees that agent's
  conversations). The owner is pushed into the descriptor query as an exact match on `userId`, so a
  pre-v5.1.6 conversation that records no owner on its descriptor is left out, and the per-row check
  enforces the same, as it stays the authority over the query. The "search matches nothing, list
  everything" fallback is decided within the same set. Without a named caller, or with authorization
  disabled (a conversation then records the userId the client passed, not the principal, even when a
  token names the caller), there is no owner and the flag changes nothing. The default is `false`: existing clients are unchanged.
- **Manager:** the chat panel's resume and its history list (they share one cache entry, so they must
  ask the same question), the operator's restore and its History list all ask for `mine`. Browsing
  everybody's conversations stays on the Conversations page.
- **Operator polling:** the 1.5 s poll while a resumed turn settles slept through a helper that added an
  abort listener per call and never removed it, about 200 on one signal over the five-minute ceiling.
  It is now `lib/abortable-sleep.ts`, which removes its listener when the timer fires.
- **Operator History** sorts with `conversationRecency`, whose comment already claimed the list shared
  it; the list carried its own copy of the expression.

**Files:** [`IRestConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/rest/IRestConversationStore.java),
[`RestConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/rest/RestConversationStore.java),
[`conversations.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/api/conversations.ts),
[`use-chat.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/hooks/use-chat.ts),
[`use-operator-chat.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/hooks/use-operator-chat.ts),
[`use-conversations.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/hooks/use-conversations.ts),
[`operator-history.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/components/operator/operator-history.tsx),
[`abortable-sleep.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/abortable-sleep.ts),
[`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md#the-conversation-store)

### Design decisions

- A server-side flag, not a client filter: only the backend knows the principal name it stamps as owner.
- Exact owner match, no "or unowned" alternative: a legacy conversation without a recorded owner is not
  "mine" to resume. Same semantics as the open "search my conversations" endpoint (#1307).
- The old eight-argument Java signature stays as a `default` overload (`mine=false`), so the MCP tool and
  the existing tests are unchanged.

## ✨ feat(parser): correct phrase search, match confidence, partial phrases and an embedding fallback (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/inputparser-improvements`)

### What changed and why

A critical re-read of the input parser (`modules/nlp`) found defects that made it silently miss inputs its
dictionaries covered, plus measurement it computed and threw away. Each defect below was reproduced
before it was fixed, and each fix was mutation-checked (re-introducing the defect fails its test).

**Defects fixed**

- **The combination search skipped readings.** `IterationCounter` capped every token's match index by the
  *last* token's match count, so a token with more readings than the last one never had its alternatives
  tried (`[1,0]` produced 1 of 2 combinations, `[2,0,0]` 1 of 3) while out-of-range plans were generated
  and discarded. A typo in the first word of a phrase, whose phrase reading was a correction's second
  candidate, therefore never matched the phrase. Replaced by a complete best-first enumerator (increasing
  total rank, then lexicographic), linear per step, allocation-free; `Permutation` is gone.
- **A complete phrase match found after the first combination was appended behind worse solutions**, and
  the pipeline takes the first one. It now goes to the front.
- **Partial phrase matching never worked**: `lookForPartlyMatch` compared phrase words with *found* words
  (different classes, never equal) and mixed up the two lists' indexes. Rewritten, and opt-in — see below.
- **Quick replies were not normalized** like the input they are compared with: under the punctuation
  normalizer a click on `Yes, please!` arrived as `Yes , please !` and matched nothing.
- **Generated quick-reply expressions were ASCII-only** (`[^a-zA-Z0-9 ]`): `Größe` became `gre`, Cyrillic,
  Arabic or CJK quick replies an empty expression. Letters and digits of every script are kept now.
- **Phrases of a dictionary in another language were candidates** — phrases are matched by word value, so
  a `de` phrase could match in an `en` conversation once the English dictionaries knew its words.
- **A phrase assembled from typo corrections reported accuracy 1.0**; it is now as accurate as its least
  accurate word and counts as corrected.
- **The `/parser` endpoint skipped the normalizers**, so it answered for an input the pipeline never parses.
- `won't` normalized to "would not" (now "will not"); typographic apostrophes (`don’t`, as phones type it)
  were never expanded; 20 common contractions added.
- The email dictionary rejected top-level domains longer than four letters (`.technology`, `.online`).
- `toLowerCase()` without a locale in the nlp module (Turkish-locale JVMs).

**Performance** (none measured as a regression, all remove work per input token):
`RegularDictionary.lookupTerm` returned one match per phrase containing a word plus a 0.9 copy of every
exact hit (now one per distinct word, from an index built once) and checked case on a parallel stream over
all keys; `Phrase.getWords()` re-split and re-allocated on every call (now cached); duplicate matches of
one token are dropped before enumeration; `RawSolution` has content equality, so the solution list no
longer fills with copies; without phrases, `parse` stops at the chosen reading plus `maxAlternatives`
(`parseAllReadings` keeps the full list for the `/parser` endpoint); the Damerau–Levenshtein correction
applies its length bound before lower-casing each dictionary word.

**New, all opt-in or diagnostic**

- `expressions:confidence` (accuracy of the least accurate contributing match), `expressions:inputTruncated`
  (input over `maxInputTokens`), and accuracy/corrected markers in `expressions:matches`.
- `minMatchingAccuracy` (default 0): matches below it contribute no expressions — "do not act on a guess".
- `maxAlternatives` (default 0, max 10): other readings as `expressions:alternatives`.
- `matchPartialPhrases` (default off) + `minPartialPhraseCoverage` (0.5): a fragment yields its phrase with
  accuracy = coverage; equally covered ambiguous fragments yield nothing.
- `semanticFallback`: when the dictionaries understood nothing, the input is compared by embedding
  similarity with example sentences (explicit, and by default the dictionaries' own phrases) and the best
  example at or above `minScore` (0.8) supplies its expressions. Reuses `EmbeddingModelFactory` (vault
  secrets, host allow-list, SSRF policy, cache). Examples are embedded once on first use; a provider failure
  is "no match" with a 60 s back-off; a malformed block fails the deployment. Metric
  `eddi.parser.semantic.fallback{outcome}`.
- **Vault grant enforcement now covers parser configurations**: `VaultGrantChecker` reads parser documents
  and scans every step's inline `config`/`extensions`, so a `${vault:…}` key in a parser's
  `semanticFallback` — stored or inline — is checked against the agent's grant like an LLM's.
- From review: a quick-reply phrase with the same text as a configured phrase now yields the quick
  reply's expressions (single quick-reply words already won); `UnrestrictedSecretsCheck` scans each
  string value with the linear reference scanner instead of one regex over the serialized document,
  where an unterminated `${vault:` in one field hid the reference in the next; the new counter is
  documented in [`metrics.md`](../metrics.md) and charted on the full-metrics dashboard ("Input Parser" row).
- From the second review (after merging main): a variable-store failure during the grant check's in-place
  `${vars:…}` expansion escaped to the catch-all that answers "no violations" — it now fails closed through
  the per-variable path; the semantic fallback reads the same token-limited input and the same effective
  language (default `en`) as the dictionaries, and an example with a language never matches a turn without
  one; `minMatchingAccuracy` takes part in choosing a token's reading (qualifying readings first, and
  readings of different accuracy are no longer collapsed); a complete phrase reading no longer cuts off the
  readings asked for by `maxAlternatives` / `parseAllReadings`; an ambiguous phrase fragment no longer
  suppresses unambiguous fragments elsewhere; the phrase index uses the same case-insensitive equivalence as
  the word map (Turkish dotted/dotless i), and the Damerau–Levenshtein length pre-check allows for lower-case
  expansion (`İ`).
- From the third review: a phrase or phrase fragment whose final accuracy is below `minMatchingAccuracy` is no
  longer taken (it outranked a qualifying word reading and then yielded nothing); the semantic fallback
  re-embeds its examples when the factory hands out a new model (changed variable or secret), so input and
  examples share one embedding space; an unsupported `embeddingProvider` fails the deployment; a parser that
  replaces an earlier result on the same step (`appendExpressions: false`) clears its confidence,
  alternatives and matches.
- From the fourth review: every occurrence of a phrase or fragment is judged against the threshold — a
  below-threshold first occurrence no longer hides a qualifying one later in the input.
- From the fifth review: the ambiguity check for fragments sees every qualifying fragment of every phrase,
  so a phrase's better fragment elsewhere no longer hides that its fragment at one position is contested.

**Files:** [`InputParser.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/nlp/internal/InputParser.java),
[`IterationCounter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/nlp/internal/matches/IterationCounter.java),
[`InputParserTask.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/nlp/InputParserTask.java),
[`SemanticFallback.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/nlp/semantic/SemanticFallback.java),
[`RegularDictionary.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/nlp/extensions/dictionaries/RegularDictionary.java),
[`VaultGrantChecker.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/VaultGrantChecker.java),
[`semantic-parser.md`](../semantic-parser.md); Platform Operator prompt (revision 63).

### Behaviour changes for existing agents

Agents that relied on a defect parse differently: inputs whose phrase needed a non-first reading of an
early token now match the phrase; a complete phrase match now wins over an earlier partial reading;
quick replies with punctuation now match under the punctuation normalizer; `won't` expands to "will
not". The pinned enumeration order in `IterationCounterTest` was an artifact of the old scheme and was
replaced by the documented best-first order.

### What's next

- The Manager's parser editor has no form fields for the new keys yet (they work in the JSON tab).
- The `/parser` endpoint takes no language and does not consult the semantic fallback.

## 🐛 fix(hitl): crash recovery also recovers interrupted group discussions (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/group-crash-recovery`)

### What changed and why

A group discussion whose node died mid-run stayed `IN_PROGRESS` (or `SYNTHESIZING`) for good. Its leg lives only in the JVM that ran it, the startup crash recovery deliberately skipped running group discussions, and `continue` and `followup` both require `COMPLETED`, so nothing but a manual cancel could end it. The sharpest case was a crash right after an approve: the approve CAS clears the pause bookmark in the same write that moves the discussion to `IN_PROGRESS`, so there was no pause left to restore either. Only a standing team's run had a backstop, the cadence claim TTL, whose default is 24 hours.

- **`HitlCrashRecoveryObserver`** — the single-node startup pass now scans `IN_PROGRESS` and `SYNTHESIZING` group discussions and cancels each one last written before the JVM started, through `IGroupConversationService.cancelDiscussion` (the ordinary cancel: state CAS, timeout schedule removed). It is gated on `eddi.hitl.crash-recovery.recover-in-progress`, like the regular IN_PROGRESS recovery, and the WARN summary counts the cancelled discussions. The first start after the upgrade also cancels every discussion that was already stuck this way. A running-state cancel releases no ephemeral agents (no leg is left to); they go when the discussion is closed or deleted. The leader-elected cluster sweep (`runClusterRecovery`) is unchanged and does not cancel groups.
- **`GroupHitlCoordinator.resumeDiscussion`** — the approve resume now stamps `lastModified`, as the human-turn and tie-break resumes already did. Without it, a discussion paused before a restart and approved after it would carry the pre-restart timestamp into its new, live leg, and the recovery pass could cancel that leg.
- **Docs** — [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md#crash-recovery) (crash recovery list, overlapping-JVM caveat), a new "Restarts" section in [`group-conversations.md`](../group-conversations.md#restarts), and the `recover-in-progress` row of [`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md).

### Design decisions

- **Process start, not a timestamp taken in the observer.** The cut-off is the JVM start time (`RuntimeMXBean.getStartTime()`): nothing this process runs can have been written before it, whatever ran before the `StartupEvent` observer.
- **Cancel, not restore.** A resumed group leg keeps no bookmark, so there is no pause to put back; cancelling ends in a state the user can act on, and a standing team's next fire returns its pulled backlog tasks to `PENDING`.
- **Reuse `cancelDiscussion`** rather than a raw state write, so the transition is the CAS-guarded one a manual cancel makes.
- **Single node only.** In cluster mode a group discussion holds no conversation lease that could prove its leg dead; a cluster variant would need its own staleness rule and is left as a follow-up.

**Files:** [`HitlCrashRecoveryObserver.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/HitlCrashRecoveryObserver.java), [`GroupHitlCoordinator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/GroupHitlCoordinator.java), [`HitlCrashRecoveryObserverTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/HitlCrashRecoveryObserverTest.java), [`GroupHitlCoordinatorTest.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/GroupHitlCoordinatorTest.java)

## 🐛 fix(groups): streaming continue binds the caller; MCP and templates enforce REST's checks (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/group-rest-mcp-parity`)

### What changed and why

Three places where one surface of the group API did less than its sibling.

- **A streamed continuation ran without its caller.**
  `POST /groups/{groupId}/conversations/{id}/continue/stream` hands the round to
  `RestGroupConversation`'s own virtual-thread executor. Nothing carried the caller
  across, and on that thread the request scope is gone, so the member turns'
  `captureOrCurrent()` found nobody: every `${caller:userId}` and `${caller:token}`
  failed closed on this path alone. The caller is now captured on the request
  thread and bound around the work with `CallerIdentityContext.withIdentity`, the
  way `startAndDiscussAsync` already does for a streamed start. `CallerIdentityContext` is a new constructor
  parameter of `RestGroupConversation`.
- **The MCP discussion tools skipped the question ceilings.** REST re-checks by
  hand that a question is present and at most 50,000 characters, and that `userId`
  and `targetAgentId` are at most 256, because a question is fanned out to every
  member in every phase at the group owner's cost. An MCP tool calls the service
  in-process, where no Bean Validation constraint runs, so a caller with the
  CONVERSE role (`eddi-viewer` included) could send a multi-megabyte question, or
  start a discussion about nothing. The check now lives in `GroupQuestionLimits`,
  next to `GroupAttachmentLimits`, and `discuss_with_group`,
  `start_group_discussion`, `followup_with_member` and `continue_group_discussion`
  call it before any read, access check or service call, answering
  `{"error": …, "errorCode": "BAD_REQUEST"}`. REST calls the same class, so both
  surfaces refuse the same input with the same text. The constants stay on
  `IRestGroupConversation`.
- **Creating a group from a template turned an access refusal into a 500.**
  `POST /groupstore/templates/{id}/instantiate` saves through
  `IRestAgentGroupStore.createGroup`, which throws `ForbiddenException` for an
  assigned agent the caller may not use. The generic `catch (Exception)` logged it
  as an error with a stack trace and answered `500`. A `ForbiddenException` or a
  JAX-RS `WebApplicationException` is now rethrown, as `RestTeamHandoffs` and
  `RestGroupWorkspace` do, so the caller gets the `403` (or the store's own status).
  The MCP side had the same fault: `create_group_from_template`, and every other
  group tool that ends in `McpGroupTools.failure` (`create_group`, `update_group`
  and the rest), answered the store's `ForbiddenException` with an `INTERNAL`
  reference and a logged stack trace. `failure` now answers it with
  `"errorCode": "FORBIDDEN"` and the guard's text, through the shared
  `McpToolUtils.accessRefusal` that `toolFailure` already uses.

The Javadoc on `budgetRefused` said the team-budget refusal "carries no
caller-supplied id". It does carry the group id: the stored one, already resolved
by the group read that precedes admission, and needed by the administrator `PUT`
path in the exhausted message. The comment now says that.

### Design decisions

- **Bind at the dispatch, not in the service.** Moving the async dispatch into
  `GroupConversationService`, or running the SSE method inline on a virtual
  thread, would also fix it, but would change when the SSE response is committed.
  Capturing before `submit` is the established pattern and a two-line change.
- **One check class, not a public static on the REST resource.** The MCP tools
  are in another package; making `RestGroupConversation.rejectionReason` public
  would have the MCP layer depend on a JAX-RS resource. `GroupQuestionLimits`
  reads the ceilings from `IRestGroupConversation`, so nothing is duplicated.
- **List limits not clamped.** The plan also suggested clamping `limit` in
  `list_groups` and `list_group_conversations`. REST takes the same unbounded
  `limit`, and both are bounded by the storage layer's `MAX_RESULT_LIMIT` (10,000),
  so this is not a check REST makes and MCP skips. Left for a change that bounds
  both surfaces together.

**Files:** [`RestGroupConversation.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestGroupConversation.java),
[`GroupQuestionLimits.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/GroupQuestionLimits.java),
[`McpGroupTools.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpGroupTools.java),
[`RestGroupTemplates.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/rest/RestGroupTemplates.java),
[`group-conversations.md`](../group-conversations.md); tests in `RestGroupConversationExtendedTest`,
`McpGroupToolsTest`, `RestGroupTemplatesTest`, and the constructor update in the other five
`RestGroupConversation*Test` classes.

## 🐛 fix(manager): per-slot agent creation in the group wizard cannot overwrite or orphan agents (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/group-wizard-slot-create`)

### What was wrong

"Create Agent" on a member card of the Group Setup Wizard provisions and deploys
that agent while the person keeps working. Its write-back rebuilt the whole
member list from the render in which the button was clicked, by index. So with
two cards created at once, whichever finished second set the other card back to
"not created" (its agent id gone), and the final Create then provisioned and
deployed a second agent for that card. The first stayed deployed with nothing
pointing at it. An edit to another card made while one was provisioning was
reverted, and removing a card above a provisioning one shifted the indexes, so
the new agent was written into the wrong card. The moderator's write-back spread
the moderator as it was at the click, reverting edits made meanwhile.

Three more ways in: the final Create stayed enabled while a card was still
provisioning (provisioning that card a second time); a card's Remove and type
toggle stayed enabled while its own agent was being created; and during the
final Create, Back and the step indicator still worked, so the person could edit
members that the Create then overwrote with its own click-time copy. While a
card was provisioning they worked too, and picking a preset on the first step
replaced every card, so the new agent had no card left to be written back to.

### What changed

- [`group-wizard.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/pages/group-wizard.tsx): every member
  slot carries a stable client-side `id` (a module counter, not
  `crypto.randomUUID()`, which is missing outside a secure context; it is never
  sent, since the save maps slots to `GroupMember` field by field). The wizard's
  `update` also accepts a function of the current state, and every member and
  moderator write in the Members step goes through it, keyed by id. Cards are
  keyed by id. The final Create is disabled (and `handleCreate` returns) while
  any card is provisioning; a card's Remove and type toggle, and the moderator's
  Remove, are disabled while that agent is being created; Back and the step
  buttons are disabled during the final Create and while any card or the
  moderator is provisioning. The per-slot buttons get
  `gw-create-agent-<n>`, `gw-create-moderator`, `gw-add-moderator`, `gw-remove-moderator`, `gw-moderator-prompt`, `member-created-<n>` and `moderator-created` test ids (the tests select by them, not by translated copy).
- [`create-group-dialog.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/components/groups/create-group-dialog.tsx):
  removed the `selectedTemplate` state, whose value was never read.
- New [`group-wizard-slot-create.test.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/pages/__tests__/group-wizard-slot-create.test.tsx):
  seven cases (two cards at once, an edit and a removal during provisioning, the
  locked controls, navigation locked while a card provisions, a moderator edit
  across a failed create, navigation locked during the final Create). Each fails
  on the previous code.

### Design decisions

- **Functional updates over a ref to the latest state.** A ref would also fix the
  stale read, but writes would still race each other between renders; a
  `setState` updater always applies to the state React holds.
- **Lock navigation during the final Create rather than make its write-back
  id-keyed too.** The review step renders no editors, so once Back and the steps
  are locked nothing can change the members under it, and its retry-safety logic
  stays as it is.
- **Lock navigation while a card provisions rather than only the preset step.**
  Only the preset step can replace the cards, but it is two Back clicks away;
  one lock on Back and the steps, held for the few seconds a create takes, is
  simpler than guarding each preset button.

## 🔧 fix(llm): an `ai.labs.apicalls` step is offered to the LLM as tools, and neither API-call step name is "legacy" (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/httpcalls-step-alias-tools`)

### Why

The API-call step has two names. `eddi://ai.labs.httpcalls` is the task's own id
(`ApiCallsTask.ID`), and `V6RenameMigration` keeps that step type on purpose.
`eddi://ai.labs.apicalls` is an alias that `ApiCallsModule` registers for the same task.
`LlmModule` registers `ai.labs.langchain` for `ai.labs.llm` the same way. Both names run
in the pipeline.

Tool discovery did not treat them alike. `WorkflowTraversal.discoverConfigs` compared the
step type string literally, so an agent whose workflow typed its API-call step
`eddi://ai.labs.apicalls` ran that step but never offered its calls to the LLM as tools.
Nothing logged it. The same literal match hid a `langchain`-typed step from
`TurnBudgetValidator`.

The Manager made it look deliberate: its pipeline view labelled the canonical
`ai.labs.httpcalls` "API Calls (legacy)" and the alias "API Calls", which is the wrong way
round.

### What changed

- `WorkflowTraversal`: there is a new `STEP_TYPE_ALIASES` map (`apicalls` → `httpcalls`,
  `langchain` → `llm`, matching the bootstrap modules' alias registrations) and a
  `canonicalStepType` helper. Discovery compares canonical names on both sides and keys its
  cache by the canonical name, so asking for an alias and asking for its target share one
  traversal. This covers HTTP-call tools, MCP tools, RAG and the turn budget check, since
  they all go through it.
- Manager `extensions.ts`: both names are labelled "API Calls", and the canonical name sorts
  first. The `extensionTypes.httpcallsLegacy` key is removed from all 11 locales.

### Behaviour change for existing agents

An existing agent whose workflow types its API-call step `eddi://ai.labs.apicalls` now has those calls
offered to the model as tools, because `enableHttpCallTools` defaults to `true`; before this change they
ran in the pipeline but were never exposed. Set `enableHttpCallTools: false` in the agent's `langchain.json`
(or remove the calls you do not want exposed from the step's httpcalls configuration) to keep the old behaviour. The upgrade
guide ([Changes after 6.7.0](../upgrading-from-6.5.md#changes-after-670)) says the same.

### Tests

`WorkflowTraversalTest` has a parameterised test that discovers a step typed with either
name when asked for the other, plus the `langchain` → `llm` case, and a test that an alias
never matches a different type. Mutation check: putting the literal comparison back fails
all 3 alias cases.

### Next

The Workforce Active Tools panel (#1294) mirrors the backend's old exact match and leaves
out `apicalls`-typed steps. Once this lands it should list them too.

---

## 🐛 fix(import): a refused merge update fails the import instead of creating a duplicate (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/import-merge-refusals`)

### What changed and why

- **A store that refuses a merge update now fails the import.** `RestImportService.updateTracked` returned `null` on any non-200 answer, and every caller then created the resource instead. Only two stores answer a refusal rather than throwing — `RestDictionaryStore.updateRegularDictionary` (a regular expression that does not compile) and `RestApiCallsStore.updateApiCalls` (batch settings over the deployment ceiling) — and for both the fallback went through `createResourceDirect`, which runs neither check: the refused document was stored as a second resource with the archive's origin id, and the workflow was pointed at the copy. `updateTracked` now throws a `WebApplicationException` carrying the store's own response (its `400` and body reach the client), or a `500` for no answer or a non-error status; the import's existing rollback undoes what the merge had already written, and a `WARN` line names the refused resource.
- **The dead fallbacks are gone.** Every other store updates through `RestVersionInfo.update`, which answers 200 or throws, so the `updated != null ? updated : create…` branches for agents, workflows, parsers, behavior, LLM, property, output, MCP calls and RAG (with its second `applyStorageRulesToNewRag`) could never run. The snippet merge no longer "falls through to create" a second snippet of the name it had just matched; a refused snippet update costs that snippet, like any snippet failure.
- **Live sync takes only a resource id as `sourceAgentId`.** `RemoteApiResourceSource.readAgent` concatenated the id into the request path, and resolving that against the source URL normalised `../` away — an id like `../../backup/export/x` read another path on the source with the caller's token. `previewSync`, `executeSync` and both batch endpoints refuse anything `RestUtilities.isValidId` rejects with a `400` (a failed entry in a batch) before a request is sent, and `readAgent` encodes the id as one path segment, as `exportAgentArchive` already did.
- The Javadocs of `RestApiCallsStore`, `RestLlmStore`, `RestMcpCallsStore` and `RedactionMarkerGuard` that described the fallback as intended are rewritten.

### Docs

[`import-export-an-agent.md`](../import-export-an-agent.md) (when a merge fails part-way) and [`agent-sync-guide.md`](../agent-sync-guide.md) (the `sourceAgentId` parameter).

### Tests

`RestImportServiceMergeSharedExtensionTest` (refused dictionary and httpcalls updates fail the merge with the store's answer, roll back, and create nothing; a non-error or missing answer is a `500`), `RestImportServiceSnippetResilienceTest` (a refused snippet update creates no duplicate and does not fail the agent), `RestImportServiceSyncCoverageTest` (a crafted `sourceAgentId` is a `400` on preview, sync and both batches, with no request sent), `RemoteApiResourceSourceTest` (the id never leaves `/agentstore/agents/`). Each failed on `main` and was mutation-checked.

**Files:** [`RestImportService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/RestImportService.java), [`RemoteApiResourceSource.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/backup/impl/RemoteApiResourceSource.java)

## 🐛 fix(ingestion): fetchable crawl URLs, bounded markdown prefixes, at-block hiding rules (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/ingestion-html-and-crawl`)

### What changed and why

Three ingestion defects from the whole-codebase review.

- **A link with a raw space was never fetched.** jsoup resolves `href="my doc.html"` without encoding the space.
  `CrawlUrls.canonicalize` already encoded it for the document id, but the crawler fetched the raw `abs:href`, and
  `SafeHttpPageFetcher`'s `URI.create` refused it. The page became an inconclusive error on every run, which also
  kept deletion reconciliation from ever running for the source. The new `CrawlUrls.fetchable` strips the fragment
  and encodes through the same tolerant parser (an already valid URL comes back byte-identical); `WebCrawler` uses
  it for links and for sitemap URLs, including for the `MAX_LINK_LENGTH` bound and the frontier cost. A URL that
  still does not parse is skipped instead of queued to fail.
- **Nested blockquotes and lists cost the square of their depth.** A blockquote renders into its own buffer and
  copies every line out behind `> `; a list item nested through another element (`li > div > ul`) is re-indented
  by every enclosing item. Neither budget of the walk counts those prefixes, so a 240 KB mail quoted ninety levels
  deep allocated 340 MB. `HtmlToMarkdownConverter` now caps the stack of such prefixing containers at
  `MAX_PREFIXED_NESTING = 8` per conversion (counted on the per-conversion `Walk`, so the converter stays
  stateless); deeper ones are written in place without their marker. Output up to eight levels is unchanged.
- **A hiding rule inside `@media screen` evaded the mail hidden-content pass.** `HiddenStylesheetRules` skipped
  every at-block, so `<style>@media screen{.x{display:none}}</style>` hid text from every reader and still sent it
  to the model. Blocks that hold on every screen — `@layer`, `@supports`, and `@media` with no query, `all`,
  `screen`, `only screen` or `not <other type>` — are now entered and their rules read like top-level ones.
  Entering continues the same forward scan (the block's closing brace is skipped like a stray one), so nesting
  costs no recursion and the pass stays linear. Viewport and device queries (`(max-width:600px)`,
  `prefers-color-scheme`), `print`, `@container` and the like stay skipped, because responsive mail hides one copy
  there and the other copy everywhere else.

### Design decisions

- The crawl fix encodes the URL to fetch rather than fetching the canonical id: the canonical form lowercases the
  host, drops tracking parameters and folds `index.html`, and the server should get the URL the page linked to.
- The markdown cap bounds stacking instead of rewriting the output model to stream prefixes; eight levels keep
  every real document intact.
- At-blocks are entered in place instead of collected onto a work list and re-read, which is simpler and needs
  no depth cap. A feature query that holds everywhere (`(min-width:0)`) is still skipped; the class Javadoc and
  [`security.md`](../security.md) say so.

**Files:** [`CrawlUrls.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/crawl/CrawlUrls.java),
[`WebCrawler.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/crawl/WebCrawler.java),
[`HtmlToMarkdownConverter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/HtmlToMarkdownConverter.java),
[`HiddenStylesheetRules.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/HiddenStylesheetRules.java),
[`security.md`](../security.md), and their tests.

## 🐛 fix(managed-conversations): one claim path for the user-conversation mapping (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/managed-conversation-claims`)

### What changed and why

A managed conversation is one conversation per intent and user, held by a `UserConversation` mapping
with a unique (intent, userId) key. Two requests that both need a new conversation (none yet, or the
current one has ended) each start one and each try to store the mapping. The REST endpoints and the MCP
`chat_managed` tool handled that race differently, and both badly:

- **REST, ended conversation** (`RestAgentManagement`): the replace deleted the mapping unconditionally.
  A slower request deleted the fresh mapping a faster one had just stored, so the two clients went on
  talking to two different conversations; or its insert collided and the request failed with an opaque
  `500`.
- **REST, no mapping yet**: the loser re-read the winner, but left its own started conversation running
  with nothing pointing at it.
- **MCP `chat_managed`** (`McpConversationTools`): a lost insert fell into the generic catch and answered
  "Failed to chat via managed agent", again leaving the started conversation running. A store failure on
  the mapping read was swallowed as "no mapping", so a database hiccup started a second conversation and
  orphaned the user's real one. An agent that was not deployed yet answered a generic failure where REST
  answers `503` with `Retry-After`. And it always took the trigger's first deployment, where REST picks
  one at random.

Now there is one claim path. `IUserConversationStore.createOrAdopt` stores the candidate mapping or, on
a duplicate key, returns the mapping that won (a store error if that one is gone again by the time it is
read). Both callers use it, end the conversation they started whenever it is not the one returned
(best effort, logged on failure), and replace an ended mapping with `deleteUserConversationIfMatches`, so a
delete only removes the mapping while it still names the ended conversation. This is the pattern the
Slack, webhook and email channels already followed. MCP now lets a mapping-read failure surface, answers a
not-ready agent with `"errorCode": "UNAVAILABLE"`, and picks a random deployment like REST.

`RestAgentManagement` also takes `SecurityIdentity` and `OwnershipValidator` through its constructor.
The `{userId}` ownership check used to run only `if (ownershipValidator != null)`, a guard that turned
itself off when its dependency was missing, and the three test classes set both fields by reflection
(two of them never set the validator, so they ran with the check off). The check is now unconditional and
the tests pass both through the constructor.

**Behaviour changes:** a REST request that loses the replace race is served on the winner's conversation
instead of failing with `500`; `chat_managed` answers the same race with the winner's reply, a not-ready
agent with `UNAVAILABLE`, and spreads new conversations across a trigger's deployments. A conversation a
request started but could not claim is ended (MCP records `system:lost-create-race` as who ended it).
This holds on both stores, whether the claim fails with a checked `ResourceStoreException` (how PostgreSQL
reports a failed read or write) or with an unchecked driver exception (how MongoDB, the default backend,
reports a timeout or a write error other than a duplicate key).

**Files:** [`IUserConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/triggermanagement/IUserConversationStore.java),
[`RestAgentManagement.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestAgentManagement.java),
[`McpConversationTools.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/mcp/McpConversationTools.java),
[`managed-agents.md`](../managed-agents.md), [`mcp-server.md`](../mcp-server.md)

**Not changed:** `EmailInboundProcessor` and `OpenAiConversationBridge` keep their own copies of the
adopt-and-end logic; moving them onto `createOrAdopt` is a follow-up.

## Manager: dialog bodies no longer touch the dialog border (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`ui/manager`) · **Branch:** `fix/manager-dialog-body-padding`

**What changed.** The "This agent is not granted its vault keys" dialog showed its
title bar with padding but its body flush against the dialog's left and right border:
the key notice box, the "Add … to the grant?" question, the "allow every agent"
checkbox and the footer buttons all sat on the edge.

**Root cause.** `AccessibleDialog` (`ui/manager/src/components/ui/accessible-dialog.tsx`)
pads only its own header (`p-5`); the content slot is rendered edge to edge on purpose,
so full-bleed bodies (the version diff editor, toolbar strips) can reach the border. Every
caller is expected to pad its own content, and 13 of the 18 call sites do (`p-5`, a few
`p-6`). Five did not:

- `ui/manager/src/components/secrets/grant-required-dialog.tsx` (the reported dialog)
- `ui/manager/src/components/feedback/ratings-table.tsx` (internal-note dialog)
- `ui/manager/src/components/quotas/team-budget-dialogs.tsx` (adjust-cap and transfer dialogs)
- `ui/manager/src/pages/user-conversations.tsx` (Create Binding dialog)

Each now wraps its body in `p-5`, matching the header. The convention is written into
the `children` doc comment of `AccessibleDialog`.

**Design decision.** Fixed at the call sites, not in the primitive: padding the content
slot by default would double-pad the 13 dialogs that already pad themselves and break the
full-bleed ones. The shared `AlertDialog` pads its whole content (`p-6`) and was fine; the
hand-rolled modals and sheets were audited and each pads its own sections. The Chat UI has
one inline confirm strip, which is padded.

## fix(manager): every workflow step opens in the Agent Studio, and a denser pipeline list (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/studio-step-editors-and-polish`)

### Why

Selecting an `eddi://ai.labs.httpcalls` step in the Agent Studio showed "Editor not available
for this extension type". That is the API-call task's *canonical* step type
(`ApiCallsTask.ID = "ai.labs.httpcalls"`; `ai.labs.apicalls` is the alias), so an agent with
thirty API-call steps had thirty uneditable stages. The same happened to steps still typed
`ai.labs.langchain` (registered by `LlmModule` as an alias of `ai.labs.llm`).

**Root cause:** the Studio resolved step → editor through its own partial type table
(`EXTENSION_TO_SLUG` in `editor-registry.tsx`), while the workflow page resolved the same step
by the store segment of its config URI (`workflow-step-links.ts`, with a third copy of the
store table) and the add-extension dialog and orphans page used a fourth
(`EXTENSION_TO_RESOURCE_SLUG`). The Studio's copy lacked both aliases.

### What changed

- **One resolution path.** `getResourceSlugForStep` in
  [`ui/manager/src/lib/api/extensions.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/api/extensions.ts) decides by
  the config URI's store segment (`apicallstore` → `apicalls`, through `RESOURCE_TYPES` and the new
  `getResourceTypeByStore`), and only falls back to the step type for a URI without that shape.
  The Studio uses it; `EXTENSION_TO_SLUG` is gone; `workflow-step-links.ts` reads the same store
  table instead of its own copy. `ai.labs.langchain` was added to the type tables.
- **Store-less steps get an informative state instead of a warning.** Templating explains what it
  does; a parser with an embedded config shows that config read-only; a step of a store-backed
  type with no URI says to attach a resource; an unknown type shows its stored config read-only.
  Each links to the workflow page (`/manage/workflowview/:id?version=N`), where steps are attached
  and inline configs are edited.
- **Pipeline list (`pipeline-railroad.tsx`).** Compact rows with the pipeline position, the
  resource's descriptor NAME (read per resource through the descriptor store, shared cache key
  with the parser editor), the id in the tooltip; consecutive steps of one type are grouped under
  one header with a count; the connector lines and per-card gaps are gone; the type label now
  comes from the shared `extensionTypes.*` labels, so it matches the workflow page.
- **Studio chrome.** Pipeline header with a step count and proper padding, larger workflow
  switcher, wider default pipeline column (288px), editor panel padded `px-6 py-5`, the shared
  `EmptyState` for "select a stage", the chat toggle as a `Button` with an `aria-label`.
- **i18n.** New `studio.inlineConfig` and `studio.stepInfo.*` keys in all 11 locales; the unused
  `studio.type.*`, `studio.noConfig` and `studio.unsupportedType` keys were removed.

### Step types and how the Studio handles them

| Step type (backend registration) | Handling |
| --- | --- |
| `ai.labs.httpcalls` (ApiCallsTask.ID), `ai.labs.apicalls` (alias) | API Calls editor (`apicallstore`) |
| `ai.labs.behavior` (RulesEvaluationTask.ID), `ai.labs.rules` (alias) | Rules editor |
| `ai.labs.llm`, `ai.labs.langchain` (alias) | LLM editor |
| `ai.labs.property` | Property Setter editor |
| `ai.labs.output` | Output editor |
| `ai.labs.mcpcalls` | MCP Calls editor |
| `ai.labs.rag` | RAG editor |
| `ai.labs.parser` with a URI / with an embedded config | Parser editor / read-only inline config + link to the workflow page |
| `ai.labs.templating` | Explanation (no config of its own) + link |
| anything else | Read-only stored config + link |

### Decisions

- The URI's store wins over the step type: it names the store the task really reads, whatever
  spelling the workflow carries. Not editing a parser's inline config in the Studio itself — that
  needs a workflow-document save, which the Studio's cascade does not do; the workflow page does.
- The "API Calls (legacy)" label for `ai.labs.httpcalls` is untouched (being decided separately);
  the pipeline list now shows it, as the workflow page already did.

### Verification

`npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run i18n:check` clean; Studio, editor-registry,
extensions, workflow, i18n and page suites pass. Mutation check: removing the store lookup from
`getResourceSlugForStep` fails the httpcalls/store tests. Checked in the browser against the MSW
mock with a 20-step pipeline.

## test(operator): CI activates the Platform Operator against a real backend (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`test/operator-activation-e2e`)

### Why

6.7.0 shipped with Platform Operator activation broken: `POST /administration/agents/setup-api` refuses a
`systemPrompt` over `AgentSetupService.MAX_SYSTEM_PROMPT_LENGTH` (100,000), and the operator prompt had grown to
~110,000 characters, so every fresh activation and every upgrade answered `System prompt is too long (109661
characters, maximum 100000) (HTTP 400)`. Every operator test until now ran on MSW, and none ever sent the real
prompt to the real endpoint. This entry adds the test that would have caught it, and the cap fix it needs to pass
(the separate cap-fix PR, #1290, was closed unmerged).

### What changed

- **New Playwright tier `operator`** (`ui/manager/e2e/operator/operator-activation.operator.spec.ts`,
  `npm run test:e2e:operator`). It activates the operator through the Manager the way an administrator does, then
  checks every stage of the real path: the spec pre-flight (`findMissingEndpoints`, `findUnusableEndpoints`), the
  platform base URL prefilled from `GET /administration/operator/self-url` and recorded as `apiBaseUrl`,
  provisioning through `setup-api` with the real prompt and allow-list, deploy, `verifyGateInstalled` and the gate
  dry run (`operator-gate-verified`), the read canary (no `operator-canary-warning`), the write canary (its
  `canary-result` report must say `pass`), and a chat turn whose tool call must come back with data.
- **A deterministic model.** `ui/manager/e2e/operator/llm-stub.mjs` is a dependency-free OpenAI-compatible server
  (streaming and non-streaming chat completions, `/models`). It answers "list the agents" with a
  `readAgentDescriptors` call, the write canary's prompt with exactly the tool and id it names, and a tool result
  with `OPERATOR_E2E_TOOL_OK` — or `OPERATOR_E2E_TOOL_FAILED` for an `{"error": …}`, a 4xx/5xx or a
  `NOT_EXECUTED` schema refusal. Arguments are filled from each tool's own JSON schema. No paid provider, no key.
- **`ui/manager/docker-compose.integration-operator.yml`**, an overlay on `docker-compose.integration.yml` that adds
  the stub as `llm-stub` (`node:26.13.0-alpine`, the script mounted read-only); `npm run infra:up:operator` /
  `infra:down:operator`. The `ui` project ignores `e2e/operator/`, and `eslint.config.js` exempts it from the
  "import `test` from ./fixtures" rule, as it does the other real-backend tiers. The tier runs with no retries: a
  retry would meet the operator the first attempt activated and report that instead of the real failure.
- **CI job `Operator Activation E2E`** (`e2e-operator` in `ci.yml`), on the image built from the commit like
  `Auth E2E`, whenever `code` changed — so every PR touching the backend or `ui/`. It feeds `E2E Gate` and is a
  `needs` of `docker`, so a commit whose operator cannot be activated is never published. `BuildQualityGatesTest`'s
  "docker needs everything the gate grades" check covers the new job without a change.
- Docs listing the CI jobs: root `AGENTS.md` (Key Files), `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `ui/manager/CONTRIBUTING.md`,
  `ui/manager/AGENTS.md` (Platform Operator), `docs/release-versioning.md` (the `docker` needs list) and the ship-pr
  skill.

### Verified locally

- Against the released `labsai/eddi:6.7.0` image and the Manager it bundles: **fails** with
  `Operator activation failed: System prompt is too long (109661 characters, maximum 100000) (HTTP 400)`.
- Against an image built with the cap raised to 250,000, with this branch's Manager: **passes** (about 18 s).
- Mutation checks against that image, each failing by name: an allow-list entry the spec lacks (`This deployment
  does not expose 1 endpoint(s) …`); `EDDI_SELF_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9` (the read canary's "could not connect to
  the platform API at http://127.0.0.1:9"); and provisioning without `hitlConfig` (`The approval gate could not be
  verified … hitlConfig is absent`).

### The setup-api prompt cap

- **`AgentSetupService.MAX_SYSTEM_PROMPT_LENGTH` 100,000 → 250,000.** The cap bounds a `systemPrompt` that reaches
  `setup` / `setup-api`, including one an LLM writes through `create_sub_agent` (storage growth, log noise); it is
  not a prompt-cost control. The operator prompt the Manager sends is ~110,000 characters for `read_write`, and
  grows every release; 250,000 gives it room and still bounds an LLM-authored prompt. Raising the cap rather than
  trimming reviewed safety and tool instructions keeps the prompt unchanged (no revision bump for it); shortening
  the prompt is still worth doing, since it is sent on every operator turn.
- **The Manager mirrors the cap** as `SETUP_MAX_SYSTEM_PROMPT_LENGTH` in `ui/manager/src/lib/api/agent-setup.ts`.
  The new backend `SetupPromptCapMirrorTest` reads that file and fails when the two values differ.
- **New `ui/manager/src/lib/operator/__tests__/operator-prompt-size.test.ts`** builds the exact prompt
  `provisionOperator` sends, for both scopes (`read_write` grants every endpoint, so every optional section is in)
  and the longest brand name the validator allows, and fails above 80% of the cap. The headroom is the point: the
  guard should fire on a feature branch, a release before the cap would.

### Fixed on the way

The first green run surfaced three defects in what activation provisions. The tier now asserts each of them.

- **The operator prompt failed its template render on every turn (revision 60 → 61, landing as 63).** The backend renders
  `systemMessage` with Qute, and the forEach entry in the prompt's apicalls section was written
  `forEach {pathToTargetArray (…, required), iterationObjectName …}`. Qute read that as an expression and could not
  parse it. Every operator turn logged `ERROR … Template processing failed for LLM parameter 'systemMessage'` and
  sent the raw prompt, and every deploy reported `TEMPLATE_DOES_NOT_PARSE`. The line now lists the fields in prose.
  With that fixed, the render still failed on the next one: `eddi_email_outbound_total{outcome: sent, …}` reads as
  the namespace `outcome:` (`No namespace resolver found for [outcome]`), which a parse alone does not catch. It is now
  written with the label in parentheses. Both scopes now parse and render. The revision bump is what tells existing
  deployments to upgrade.
- **The compatibility check reported a false `TEMPLATE_DOES_NOT_PARSE`.** `LlmTask` wraps `${vault:…}`-style mentions
  in raw sections before rendering an LLM parameter (`escapeConfigReferenceMentions`), but `AgentCompatibilityLint`
  parsed the raw text, so a prompt that only *mentions* the syntax (the operator's tells the model to store keys as
  `${vault:key-name}`) was reported on every deploy. New `CompatibilityRules.scanLlmTaskTemplates` checks a task's
  `parameters` as they render. The rest of the task is still checked raw, because it renders without that escape.
  `escapeConfigReferenceMentions` is now public for it.
- **Optional query parameters were required tool arguments.** `McpApiToolBuilder` marked every query parameter
  required, defaults included, so `readAgentDescriptors` made the model invent `filter`, `index`, `limit`,
  `ownership` and `space`. A query parameter is now required only when the spec says so. New
  `Request.optionalQueryParams` (null by default, so stored configs are unchanged) lists the others, and
  `ApiCallExecutor.buildRequest` leaves one off the request when its value renders empty instead of sending
  `?limit=`. The approval preview, the request fingerprint and the execution all go through `buildRequest`, so
  request pinning sees the same request that is sent. Side effect: an endpoint that needs a query value its spec does
  not mark required (`duplicateRag`'s `version`) now answers the model with its own 400 instead of being forced.
  A path parameter stays required even when an optional query parameter of the same name shares its tool variable
  (review finding; regression test `pathParamSharingNameWithOptionalQueryStaysRequired`).
- **A repeated operationId dropped a tool.** `GET /groupstore/groups/{groupId}/lessons` and `…/outcomes` were both
  `list`. Tools register by name, so assembly logged `Tool name collision: http tool 'list'` and the operator never
  received one of two endpoints it had been granted. The two now have their own operationIds (`listGroupLessons`,
  `listGroupOutcomes`). `McpApiToolBuilder.uniqueToolName` also keeps names unique for any spec: the first operation
  keeps its operationId and a repeat takes its method-and-path slug, so a third-party spec can no longer lose a tool
  this way.

[HTTP Calls → Argument schema](../httpcalls.md#argument-schema-for-llm-tools) and its field table now describe the
requiredness rule, `request.optionalQueryParams` and the renaming of a repeated operationId.

Unit tests: `AgentCompatibilityLintTest` (vault mention not reported, a real error beside one still reported),
`McpApiToolBuilderArgumentSchemaTest` (requiredness, `optionalQueryParams`, repeated operationIds, suffix fallback),
`ApiCallExecutorTest` (an empty optional parameter is not sent, a supplied one and a non-optional empty one are).
Mutation-checked: reverting each of the four fixes fails its test. The E2E checks the same things from outside: one
distinct tool per allow-listed endpoint reached the model (read from the stub's `GET /__e2e/tools`, published on
loopback port 8089), `readAgentDescriptors` has no required arguments, the operator's deployment status carries
no `TEMPLATE_DOES_NOT_PARSE`, and no request reached the model with the prompt unrendered. The stub counts system
prompts that still hold `{#if}` section tags: when its render fails, `LlmTask` only logs the error and sends the raw
text. Restoring the `{outcome: …}` line fails that check with 5 unrendered prompts.

### Next

- Making `Operator Activation E2E` (or `E2E Gate`) a required status check is a repository-settings decision; only
  `Build & Test` and `CodeQL Analysis` are required today.
- The revision bump collided as expected: `main` reached 62 meanwhile, so the merge of `main` into this branch
  (2026-11-14) re-bumped to **63** with the fingerprint of the merged prompt, which still parses and renders in both
  scopes.
- **Follow-up, not in this PR:** the prompt still holds literal braces that Qute evaluates as variables and the
  runtime renders as empty — `{input}`, `{output}`, `{id}`, `{risk}`, `{cause}`, `{context}`, metric label sets such
  as `{reason="caller_timed_out"}` and `{provider,outcome}` (16 in the read_write scope). They do not fail the render,
  so the model simply never sees that text. Escaping the prompt's literal braces at build time, with a test that
  renders it under the runtime engine's rules, is the fix.

## 🐛 fix(llm-tools): the tool-result guardrail screens the whole result before truncation; the summarizer honours a `${vars:...}` provider type (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/tool-result-screening`)

### What changed and why

**The guardrail ran on the cut text, so everything past the cut was ungoverned (LLM-3).**
`ToolLoopRunner` redacted secrets, truncated, and only then ran the tool-result
guardrail. With `truncationStrategy: paginate` the whole remainder went into
`PaginatedResponseStore` unscreened, and pages 2..N came back through
`fetch_tool_response_page`, a built-in tool. Under the documented narrowing
`directiveAppliesToSources: ["mcp", "a2a", "http"]` a built-in result is not
screened for directives, so an instruction an MCP server placed after the first
page reached the model unredacted, in an envelope that said `builtin`. The
`summarize` strategy likewise sent the unscreened text to its summarizer model.
The default configuration (an empty source list means every source) was not
affected.

`ToolResultGuardrail.inspect` is split in two. `screen` runs the directive rule
on the whole result and applies `warn`/`redact`/`block`; `mark` adds the
provenance envelope. The runner now screens first, truncates the screened text,
and marks what is left, so stored pages and summarizer input are governed text
under the tool's real source. A summary is screened once more, because it is new
text a model wrote from third-party content. Both methods keep the
degrade-to-allow behaviour.

Behaviour changes: `block` now also fires on a directive in the part a
`headTail` or `truncate` cut drops. The `tool_result` trace entry shows the
screened text (without the envelope). `eddi_guardrail_toolresult_count_total`
counts a `summarize` summary as a second screening.

**The summarizer ignored global variables in the provider type (LLM-11).**
`ToolResponseTruncator` passed `task.getType()` unresolved to
`ChatModelRegistry.getOrCreate`, so a task typed `${vars:provider}` failed to
build a summarizer and `summarize` silently became `truncate`. The type is now
resolved through `GlobalVariableResolver`, as `LlmTask` resolves it. Parameters
need no such step: the registry resolves `${vars:...}` and vault references in
them itself, while a Qute expression in a parameter stays unrendered (now noted in
the class Javadoc). `PaginatedResponseStore` moved into the same constructor, which
removed a null-fallback branch for a field CDI always fills and the reflection
the tests used to set it.

### Design decisions

- **Screen before truncation and mark after, rather than screening each page as
  it is fetched.** Per-page screening would need the original source carried
  through the page store and would still miss a directive split across a page
  boundary. Screening once, whole, covers every strategy at once.
- **Re-screen only the summary.** The cut strategies only remove text from an
  already-screened result, so scanning their output again would cost a second
  regex pass and a second metric count on the default path for nothing.
- **Resolve the type in the truncator**, the one place that builds the summarizer
  model, rather than in the runner.

**Files:** [`ToolResultGuardrail.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/ToolResultGuardrail.java), [`ToolLoopRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java), [`ToolResponseTruncator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolResponseTruncator.java), tests in `ToolLoopRunnerGuardrailWiringTest`, `ToolResultGuardrailTest`, `ToolResponseTruncatorTest`, `ToolResponseTruncatorExtendedTest`, `HelperModelCallRetryTest`; docs [`langchain.md`](../langchain.md), [`mcp-client.md`](../mcp-client.md), [`metrics.md`](../metrics.md).
