## ✨ feat(llm): traceable rolling summaries — cited turns, verbatim numbers, a deterministic check (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/traceable-summaries`, stacked on `feat/user-memory-contested`)

### What changed and why

The rolling conversation summary was prose with no link back to the turns it condensed: `conversationRecall` could
drill back, but nothing told the model where to look, and nothing caught a number the summarizer rounded or invented.
`conversationSummary.citeSources: true` makes it extractive and checkable. See
[LLM Configuration → Traceable summaries](../langchain.md#traceable-summaries-citesources).

- **Prompt** — with `citeSources` the summarizer must end every point with the turns it came from (`[turns 4-7, 9]`,
  numbered as the summarizer input and `conversationRecall` number them: 0-based conversation steps), copy numbers
  verbatim and write "unclear in source" instead of guessing. The rules are appended to a custom `summarizationPrompt`
  too.
- **Check** — [`SummaryCitations`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/SummaryCitations.java), no model:
  every citation inside the summarized range, every bullet cited, every number verbatim in the cited turns' screened
  text. Citation indices, unbracketed `turn N` references and list markers are not numbers. A citation of a turn
  before the previous boundary is checked against the previous summary's text — the only source of those turns the
  summarizer saw.
- **Policy** — `onValidationFailure`: `keep` (default), `retry_once` (same screened content, spend-checked first, tokens
  added to the first call's so the step prices both), `fallback_to_previous` (previous summary and boundary stay).
  Counted on `eddi.summary.citation.checks{source,outcome}`, charted on the Full Metrics dashboard.
- **Recall** — `conversationRecall` takes a citation as it stands: `[turns 4-7, 9]` returns every turn it names, each
  once, clamped and capped at `maxRecallTurns`. A plain query behaves as before.
- **Wiring** — `LlmTask.resolveEffectiveSummaryConfig` copies the two new fields (it rebuilds the config when the
  provider or model is inherited, and would otherwise have switched the feature off silently).

### Design decisions

- Off by default; while off the prompt is byte-identical and the check never runs. `keep` is the default policy because
  a fallback retries on every turn and pays for it.
- Numbers match by exact text: `1,000` ≠ `1.000` ≠ `1000`, `10%` ≠ `10`, `-5` ≠ `5`. Strict on purpose — the prompt asks
  for verbatim copies, and a reformatted number is exactly what the check exists to catch.
- The check is line-scoped (a bullet plus its continuation lines), not sentence-scoped: deterministic and cheap, and a
  model that puts one citation at the end of its bullet is what the prompt asks for.

### What's next

Dream consolidation gets the same check and records the ids of the entries a consolidated memory came from (entry
below).

---

## ✨ feat(memory): traceable Dream consolidation — cited originals and sourceEntryIds (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/traceable-summaries`, stacked on `feat/user-memory-contested`)

### What changed and why

A Dream consolidation replaces its originals and then deletes them, so a number the model rounded or invented replaced
the user's fact for good, and nothing recorded what a consolidated memory was made of. `dream.citeSources: true` applies
the rolling summary's check to every consolidation group. See
[User Memory → Traceable consolidation](../user-memory.md#traceable-consolidation-citesources).

- **Model** — [`UserMemoryEntry`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/properties/model/UserMemoryEntry.java) gains
  `sourceEntryIds` (never null, `[]` by default; the 15-argument constructor stays). Every ordinary write clears it —
  a value stated anew is not derived from those sources.
- **Stores** — MongoDB and PostgreSQL write it on every path `assessment` is written on (upsert, `insertIfAbsent`,
  `upsertIfOwnedBy`, `replaceIfUnchanged`, the mappers) and the flat `mergeProperties` clears it. PostgreSQL adds
  `source_entry_ids JSONB DEFAULT '[]'` with `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`; a MongoDB document without the field reads as
  `[]`. Covered by `DatastoreParityTest` on both backends. The GDPR export carries it (the record is serialised whole).
- **Dream** — with `citeSources` the request numbers the originals (`"ref": 0..n-1`, not their ObjectIds), every
  consolidated entry must carry `"sources"`, and `SummaryCitations.checkConsolidatedEntry` checks it (refs exist, every
  number of the value verbatim in the cited originals' key and value). A separate parser reads the `sources` array; the
  plain parser and request are untouched while the flag is off. Duplicate keys merge their sources.
- **Policy** — `dream.onValidationFailure`, default `fallback_to_previous` (the group is skipped, originals kept);
  `retry_once` is bounded by `maxCostPerRun`, the legacy call cap and the tenant budget and costed like any call;
  `keep` writes anyway (an entry left without valid refs records the whole group). Counted on
  `eddi.summary.citation.checks{source="dream"}`.

### Design decisions

- The Dream default differs from the conversation default (`keep`) on purpose: a rolling summary is a derived view over
  a conversation that is kept in full; a consolidation deletes what it read.
- `sourceEntryIds` points at deleted documents (except an original the consolidation overwrote in place). That is what
  recording provenance can mean without changing what consolidation deletes; the ids identify the facts in logs,
  backups and exports.
- A retry that throws skips the group but does not abort the phase: the group's first call succeeded, so it is no
  configuration fault.
- After merging #1295's `asStatement()` (every store value write is stored `ACCEPTED`): `asStatement()` keeps
  `sourceEntryIds`, because provenance is not a verdict and Dream's consolidation write goes through those same store
  methods. The REST boundary (`UserMemoryWriteRules.withDefaults`) clears it, so a client echoing a consolidated
  entry's ids onto an edited value does not keep them. MCP and every other writer build their entries without sources.
  `DatastoreParityTest` pins both halves on MongoDB and PostgreSQL.

### Review follow-up (2026-11-12)

- **Citation failures no longer log content.** The WARN lines for a failed citation check (conversation summary and
  Dream), a failed retry and an unparseable or unserialisable Dream answer now carry ids, counts, the policy and the
  exception class only. The problem list, which quotes the summary text and memory keys, and the parser's message,
  which quotes the model's answer, moved to a DEBUG line (sanitised, capped).
- **`conversationRecall` and out-of-range citations.** A cited turn past the summarized turns used to be clamped onto
  the last summarized turn, so `[turn 40]` returned turn 4 as if it were the source. A citation wholly outside the
  summarized turns, or a bracket that is not a list of turn numbers, now names no turn: the tool says so and logs one
  DEBUG line. A range that only partly overlaps is still cut at the last summarized turn.

### Review follow-up (2026-11-15)

- **Dream checks each cited object before merging duplicate keys.** The citation check used to run on
  `mergeDuplicateKeys`' output, whose sources are the union of every object sharing a key. An object citing nothing,
  or citing an original that does not hold its number, borrowed a sibling's sources and passed, and the originals were
  then deleted. The check now reads the parsed answer object by object, on the first call and on the `retry_once` call
  alike; the merge still runs afterwards and still records the union of the (now individually valid) sources.
- **A cut turn is checked against the text that was sent.** When a single turn is longer than `maxCharsPerUpdate`, the
  check kept that many characters of the turn's raw text. The request itself is shorter: the "Turn N — User:" labels
  and the cut notice take part of the budget, so a number just past the sent prefix could back a summary the
  summarizer could not have read. The check now keeps exactly the user and agent text inside the sent prefix.
- **A `retry_once` summary call is spend-checked with the first call's cost.** The retry's admission ran before the
  first summary call's cost was recorded, with `0.0` pending, so a $1 ceiling with $0.90 recorded admitted a retry after
  a $0.20 first call. `ConversationSummarizer`'s admission callback now takes the update's unrecorded token usage
  (empty before the first call, the first call's usage before the retry), and `LlmTask` prices it with the same
  `ModelPrices` it records the summary spend with and passes it as `pendingUsd`. The cost is only checked there; both
  calls are still recorded once, together, after the update.
- **An empty first answer gets the citation policy.** With `citeSources` on, an empty summarizer answer returned early,
  before the check: `retry_once` never made its corrective call and no outcome was counted. It now goes through the
  check, which fails it ("the summary is empty"), and the configured policy applies. `retry_once` retries,
  `fallback_to_previous` falls back, and `keep`, having nothing to keep, falls back rather than replace the previous
  summary with nothing. Each case is counted. With `citeSources` off the early return is unchanged.

---

## 🐛 fix(turns): queued-turn guard on the one-read path, busy skips and inert abandoned outcomes (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/turn-runner-guards`)

### What changed and why

Four holes in how a queued turn starts and how an abandoned one ends, all in `ConversationStepRunner`
plus one in the SSE stream.

- **A conversation deleted while its turn was queued (RT-1).** Both stores answer a missing document
  with no state and no revision. The queued-say guard skipped only `AWAITING_HUMAN`, `IN_PROGRESS` and
  `ENDED`, so a turn queued during a delete or a GDPR erasure ran on its request-time snapshot: model and
  tool calls, and `storePropertiesPermanently` wrote the erased user's long-term properties back after
  the erasure's memory sweep. Only the final persist failed. Such a turn is now skipped and reported as
  `ENDED`: REST answers `410`, a stream ends with `done`, and the clients already start a new conversation
  on `ENDED`.
- **A store failure at that guard (RT-2).** The state/revision read ran outside any `try`. A pool timeout
  there escaped the task, the coordinator dead-lettered the turn, and its caller never heard back (REST
  waited for its `408`, a stream got no terminal event). The same held for a runtime that refused the
  body (`RejectedExecutionException`). Both now answer **busy** (`409` + `Retry-After`, `done` with
  `IN_PROGRESS` on a stream; the input was not consumed), the contract the fence branch already used for a
  store failure. The three busy paths share one `skipAsBusy`.
- **The late outcome of a turn the watchdog abandoned (RT-5).** After a watchdog timeout the coordinator
  starts the next turn while a body that swallowed its interrupt keeps running. When it finally ended, its
  callbacks settled it as a cancelled turn, keyed only by conversation id: on one node that stamped
  `EXECUTION_INTERRUPTED` over the `READY` the next turn had committed (and made that turn's pause commit
  miss); in cluster mode its expired lease put a lease-lost record in the map the next turn drains, so the
  next turn was dead-lettered as lease-lost after it had answered `200`. Once the dispatcher has given up,
  the outcome now only refreshes the state cache. The same holds for a persist the completion callback
  started just before the watchdog gave up and that failed afterwards: a fence or revision-guard refusal is
  no longer recorded for the next turn to drain (which dead-lettered that turn), and a store failure no
  longer stamps `ERROR` over the next turn's state. The comments that said a node runs at most one turn of a
  conversation at a time were wrong and are corrected.
- **A stream whose turn hung past the watchdog (RT-2).** REST has its `408`; a stream had nothing and was
  kept open by the keep-alive for as long as the body hung. Once the turn has written its first event, a
  stream that then carries none of its events for `systemRuntime.agentTimeoutInSeconds` plus one
  keep-alive interval is ended with an `error` event, code `turn_timeout`. A live turn cannot reach that
  (the watchdog bounds it), a queued one has written nothing yet, and with the keep-alive off the backstop
  is off too.

`ConversationServiceQueuedTurnTest` now stubs `getStateAndRevision`, the path both production stores
take; before, every queued-turn test ran on the legacy two-read fallback. One test keeps the fallback
covered.

**Files:** [`ConversationStepRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationStepRunner.java),
[`RestAgentEngineStreaming.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestAgentEngineStreaming.java),
[`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md), [`hitl.md`](../hitl.md) (the resume stream's
`turn_timeout`), [`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md) (the stream's `turn_timeout` code); tests in `ConversationServiceQueuedTurnTest`,
`ConversationStepRunnerLeaseTest`, `ConversationStepRunnerAbandonedTurnTest` (new) and
`RestAgentEngineStreamingKeepAliveTest`.

## 🐛 fix(llm-tools): per-turn tool budgets live on the conversation step, forgetFact included (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/turn-scoped-tool-budgets`)

### What changed and why

Two LLM-tool budgets were counted in a field of the tool object: `maxWritesPerTurn` in `UserMemoryTool`
and `maxDelegationsPerTask` in `ConverseWithAgentTool`. A turn builds its tools more than once — each
step of an agent-mode model cascade runs its own tool assembly, and so does the continuation of a turn
resumed after a HITL tool approval — and every assembly starts a new tool object at zero. A three-step
escalating cascade could therefore write three times `maxWritesPerTurn`, and a resumed turn got a fresh
budget, while [`user-memory.md`](../user-memory.md) promised a limit "per conversation turn".

The new `TurnCounter` keeps the count on the conversation's current step instead, where
`hitl:tool_pause_count` already lives for the same reason. `ContextualToolsProvider` hands the memory
tool `tools:memory_writes`, and `DynamicAgentToolsProvider` hands the delegation tool
`tools:delegations:<taskId>` (`default` for a task without an id), so the delegation cap stays per LLM
task as documented, now across every assembly of that task in the turn. The next turn's step starts at
zero again. Two consequences follow from counting on the step: the LLM tasks of one turn share
`maxWritesPerTurn` (it was per task execution), and a rerun of the turn, which re-executes the same step,
starts from what its first run spent — the writes and delegations that run made are not undone either.
Neither key has a public prefix, so the counts stay out of the simple client snapshot; `returnDetailed=true` shows them as plain integers, like `hitl:tool_pause_count`. The key is read by exact
match: the step's `getLatestData` is a prefix lookup and would let `tools:delegations:a`
read the count of `tools:delegations:ab`. A tool built without a conversation (direct construction)
still counts on its own instance.

`forgetFact` was not covered by any budget, so one injected "forget everything about the user" could
delete every memory the agent owns in a single turn. A successful deletion now counts against
`maxWritesPerTurn` like a stored fact, and is refused with the same "Maximum writes per turn" message
once the budget is spent. A key that matches nothing and a refused deletion do not count. The refusal
text now says "saved or forgotten", since it can answer either tool.

**Files:** [`TurnCounter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/TurnCounter.java),
[`UserMemoryTool.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/UserMemoryTool.java),
[`ConverseWithAgentTool.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/ConverseWithAgentTool.java),
[`ContextualToolsProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ContextualToolsProvider.java),
[`DynamicAgentToolsProvider.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/DynamicAgentToolsProvider.java),
[`user-memory.md`](../user-memory.md)

## ✨ feat(events): the per-user event stream, `GET /me/events` (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/user-event-stream` (from `feat/conversation-search-tool-ui`)

Phase 8 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §6, **B12**), the backend for T2.3 (approvals in the flow) and T2.8 (long jobs with a notification when done). A server-sent event stream that tells a signed-in user's tabs about things that concern them, plus a framework-free client in `@eddi/core/user-events`. No UI yet: the next step wires the notifications.

### What changed and why

- **`GET /me/events`** ([`IRestUserEvents`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/userevents/IRestUserEvents.java), [`RestUserEvents`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/userevents/RestUserEvents.java)): a `ready` frame, then one named event per thing — `approval.pending`, `approval.decided`, `team.started`, `team.waiting_for_human`, `team.ended`, `job.finished`, `job.run_completed`, `job.failed`, `notification.created` — and a keep-alive comment every 20 s. Any role may open it; an anonymous caller gets `401` with authorization on (`@RolesAllowed` first, an explicit check second).
- **Ids and kinds, never content** ([`UserEvent`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/userevents/UserEvent.java)): conversation, agent, group, discussion, schedule and notification ids, plus short enum states. No message text, title, prompt, pause reason, tool argument or display name; the client fetches details through the access-checked endpoints. Tests serialize every group state with a question, an answer, a pause reason and a rendered prompt set, and find none of them on the wire.
- **Scoping at fan-out, per connection** ([`UserEventHub`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/userevents/UserEventHub.java), [`UserEventAudience`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/userevents/UserEventAudience.java)): each event carries an audience read from the authoritative record (the conversation descriptor's owner, the discussion's owner and `HUMAN` roster, the notification's recipient), and each connection is checked against it with what its own request established — principal, `eddi-admin` / `eddi-approver`, team subjects — plus, for a paused 1:1 conversation, a fresh `ResourceAccessGuard.checkPrincipalUse` on the agent (the rule `HitlAccessGuard.requireConversationResumeAccess` applies to an owner's resume). A check that cannot answer delivers nothing. Approvers and admins get approval events by role, as [Who May Decide](../hitl.md#who-may-decide) says.
- **Sources.** `approval.pending` (1:1) from `HitlPauseCommittedEvent`; `approval.decided` (both surfaces) from `HitlDecisionRecorded`; group gates and `team.*` from `GroupConversationService.executeDiscussion` (start of a fresh leg, and the end of every leg, reading the state the leg left behind) and the cancel of a paused discussion in `GroupHitlCoordinator`; `job.finished` from `ConversationContinuations.armIfDecided` when a continuation turn arms no successor (state `done`, or the refusal reason); `job.run_completed` / `job.failed` from `ScheduleFireExecutor` (the owner read from the conversation descriptor, never the schedule); `notification.created` from `WorkspaceNotifications.deliver`, `UPDATE_AVAILABLE` to every admin.
- **Cluster.** [`UserEventPublisher`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/userevents/UserEventPublisher.java) delivers locally and publishes `ClusterEvent.USER_EVENT` through `IClusterEventBus.publishTransient` (new): core NATS on `eddi.<prefix>.tevt.>` with `eddi.messaging.type=nats`, a no-op in-memory; each receiving node scopes the event against its own connections. Principals travel as `KvKeys.sha256` hashes, as the other user-keyed cluster events do. A kind a node does not know is dropped. **Not on the JetStream stream** that carries cache invalidations (review finding): there a burst of user events evicted invalidations from the stream's 100,000-message cap sooner, during an outage filled the shared outbox (`eddi.cluster.events.outbox-size`) until its overflow forced `cache.resync-all` on reconnect, and replayed hints up to an hour late. A transient event is never stored, queued or replayed, carries no stream sequence (so it is never a gap), and is dropped while disconnected (`eddi_cluster_events_transient_dropped_total{type}`; `…_transient_published_total{type}` otherwise). [`JetStreamEventBus`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/events/JetStreamEventBus.java) subscribes the transient wildcard on every (re)connect and hands it to the same handlers.
- **Review fixes to the sources.** `job.run_completed` is not raised for a scheduled run whose turn ended `AWAITING_HUMAN` (its `approval.pending` says so; the run is not over). A continuation whose next schedule could not be written now raises `job.finished` with state `schedule_failed` — the job stops there as surely as at its limit. A nested discussion (`depth > 0`) raises neither `team.started` nor `team.ended`: it is a step of its parent, whose owner would otherwise hear each sub-team start and end.
- **Bounds.** Per-user (`8`) and per-node (`10000`) connection caps answering `429` with `Retry-After`. Before refusing, the caller's own streams whose client already went away are swept under the registration lock (only that caller's bucket, never a global scan), so a reload or a flaky network is not refused by its own dead tabs, and two concurrent opens at one below a cap cannot both pass it; one fan-out thread behind a bounded queue (dropped and counted when full); a per-connection buffer of unacknowledged frames (`64`), past which the stream is closed as a slow consumer; streams closed at token expiry and after `max-connection-age` (1 h) so captured roles are re-read; a vanished client noticed at the next heartbeat. Seven `eddi.user-events.*` properties in `application.properties` and [configuration-reference.md](../configuration-reference.md).
- **Authorization off:** every caller is owner and admin, as on every other endpoint, so all streams share one anonymous bucket and get every event. That bucket is exempt from the per-user cap, which there would cap every caller of the deployment together; the per-node cap still bounds it.
- **Metrics:** `eddi_user_events_connections`, `eddi_user_events_delivered_total{kind}`, `eddi_user_events_dropped_total{reason}`, `eddi_user_events_rejected_total{reason}` — [metrics.md](../metrics.md#user-event-stream-metrics), a **User event stream** row on the Full Metrics Reference dashboard, and the Kubernetes copies (`scripts/sync-monitoring-assets.py`).
- **Frontend.** [`@eddi/core/user-events`](../../ui/packages/core/src/user-events.ts), its own `exports` entry, not re-exported from the root: `getUserEventStream` (one per tab), typed events, `on(kind | "*")`, `onStatus` (`open` means refetch), `fetch` through the app's `HttpClient` (bearer token and one refresh on `401`), reconnect with exponential backoff and jitter, stop on `401`/`403`/`404`, at least the server's `retryAfterSeconds` on `429`. A test fails if `ui/chat/src` ever imports it. MSW: [`user-event-handlers.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/test/mocks/user-event-handlers.ts) holds the stream open after `ready` and writes whatever `pushMockUserEvent` sends; `GET /me/events` is in `openapi-operations.json`.
- **Docs.** New [user-events.md](../user-events.md) (in `SUMMARY.md`); [rest-api-reference.md](../rest-api-reference.md), [hitl.md](../hitl.md), [group-conversations.md](../group-conversations.md) and [long-jobs.md](../long-jobs.md) link to it.
- **Platform Operator.** The prompt explains the stream (kinds, audiences, limits, cluster behaviour, metrics, the audit-ledger dependency of `approval.decided`). No allow-list change: a stream is no tool. `operator-revision.json` → **57**.
- **Platform Operator, caps.** The prompt also says the per-user cap does not apply to the shared anonymous bucket with authorization off (only the node cap does); revision stays 57, fingerprint updated.

### Design decisions

- **Hints, no replay.** `Last-Event-ID` is not supported: a replay would need a per-user history shared across pods. The client refetches on every (re)connect instead, so a missed event is never lost in effect.
- **The audience is data; the decision is the receiver's.** The raising node names who may receive an event; every node, its own included, checks its connections against that with their own captured identity.
- **The end of a group leg reads the persisted state** instead of listening to each branch's listener call, so a new way of ending a discussion cannot forget to tell anyone.
- **`approval.decided` rides `HitlDecisionRecorded`**, which carries the owner and covers both surfaces, cancels and timeouts; the price is that it needs the audit ledger (on by default), which the docs and the operator say.
- **Refuse at the cap, not evict the oldest:** eviction would let two misbehaving tabs knock each other off in a loop; a refused tab backs off, and a closed tab's slot frees as soon as another stream is opened at the cap (otherwise at the next heartbeat).

### Not done

- The UI (T2.3 / T2.8 notifications) — the next step.
- Web push.
- No `*IT`: the cross-pod path is covered through the bus abstraction (two hubs joined by a fake bus with a JSON round trip), not against a live NATS.

---

## fix(nlp): a chat message with stray parentheses no longer fails the turn (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/user-input-expression-depth`)

A production deployment on 6.6.0 failed three turns in five minutes when users pasted long text containing list
markers and unbalanced parentheses (`a)`, `(It`, `3))`). The log showed `Error while parsing Expression` repeated, then
`ExpressionLimitExceededException: Expressions nest 33 levels deep; at most 32 are accepted`, and the user received
the generic fallback answer.

**Root cause:** every token no dictionary knew became `unknown(<token>)` (`Unknown`), and `InputParserTask` stores the
expressions of a turn as one string that rule matching, the property setter and the append merge parse again. The
token text went into that string verbatim, so each `(` in the message became an opening bracket in expression syntax.
Opening brackets from different tokens add up across the whole message; past 32 the depth bound added in 6.6 refused
the string and the exception failed the lifecycle. Before the bound the same input produced silently wrong nesting.
`main` still had it.

**Fix:** `Unknown` now builds `unknown(<token>)` from the token without `(`, `)` and `,`, the three characters the
notation gives meaning to; a token that is only such characters becomes a bare `unknown`. The word itself (`getValue()`,
match details, the conversation input) is untouched, and the other dictionaries that build expressions from runtime
text only accept digits, e-mail addresses or epoch times, which cannot carry those characters. The malformed-expression
log in `ExpressionProvider` is a WARN instead of an ERROR.

**Decision:** `eddi.expressions.max-depth` and `eddi.expressions.max-length` are unchanged and still refuse
configuration-authored and client-supplied (`context` type `expressions`) nesting; user chat text is data and is
sanitised where it becomes an expression, not by loosening the guard.

**Tests:** `UserInputExpressionRoundTripTest` runs prose with many unbalanced parentheses through the parser, the
stored expression string and the re-parse (failed with the 33-level error before the fix), checks that dictionary words
still match, and that authored deep nesting is still refused.

---

## 🛠️ fix(manager): Active Tools in the Workforce agent panel shows what the LLM really gets (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/workforce-active-tools-display`)

### Why

The Workforce chat's Agent Details panel listed an LLM task's `tools` as raw
`eddi://ai.labs.apicalls/apicallstore/apicalls/<id>?version=1` URIs, one badge each. The badges
overflowed the 320 px panel (horizontal scrollbar), an agent with sixteen tool sets produced a
wall of near-identical badges, and nothing said which tool set was which.

Worse, it was the wrong list. The backend never resolves `task.tools`
(`LlmConfiguration.Task#tools`: "a non-empty list only switches the task into agent mode").
HTTP and MCP tools are discovered from the `eddi://ai.labs.httpcalls` / `eddi://ai.labs.mcpcalls`
steps of **every** workflow of the agent (`HttpCallToolsProvider`, `McpToolsProvider`,
`WorkflowTraversal.discoverConfigs`), gated by `enableHttpCallTools` / `enableMcpCallTools`,
which default to **on**. The panel showed the HTTP/MCP chips only when those flags were
explicitly `true`, and showed a built-in whitelist even when `enableBuiltInTools` was off, in
which case the backend ignores it.

### What changed

- `ui/manager/src/lib/agent-tool-refs.ts` (new): `collectWorkflowToolRefs` reads tool sets from
  the agent's workflows the way the backend does. It matches the step type exactly (a step typed
  with the `eddi://ai.labs.apicalls` alias is not offered as a tool by the backend, so it is not
  listed), respects the two switches with default-on semantics, and deduplicates.
- `ui/manager/src/components/workforce/active-tools-list.tsx` (new): the Active Tools section.
  - Each tool set is shown by its **descriptor name** (one cached `getDescriptor` read, short-id
    fallback), with an HTTP/MCP icon, its version, the full URI in the tooltip and a link to its
    editor.
  - The first four are shown; the rest sit behind "Show N more".
  - Built-in tools appear only when `enableBuiltInTools` is on: the whitelist as chips, or one
    "All built-in tools" chip when there is no whitelist.
  - There is a Memory chip and a total count.
  - A loading state shows while the workflows load.
- `agent-details-panel.tsx`:
  - It fetches every workflow of the agent, sharing the existing query key so the first workflow
    comes from cache.
  - It uses the new component and no longer repeats capabilities in the tool list.
  - It clips horizontal overflow, and the model badge truncates.
- Six new `Workforce.agentEditor.tools*` keys in all 11 locales.

### Tests

`active-tools-list.test.tsx` has 9 tests:

- collection across workflows, with dedupe;
- the `apicalls` alias is ignored;
- the per-category switches;
- `task.tools` is not listed;
- name resolution, link and collapse;
- the id fallback on a 404 descriptor;
- built-ins gated by `enableBuiltInTools`;
- the loading and empty states.

Mutation check: when the HTTP switch is flipped from default-on to default-off, 2 tests fail.

### Follow-ups found on the way (not in this PR)

- Pipeline labels: `extensions.ts` labels the canonical `eddi://ai.labs.httpcalls` step type
  "API Calls (legacy)" and the alias "API Calls". It is the other way round: `ApiCallsTask.ID`
  is `ai.labs.httpcalls`, and `V6RenameMigration` deliberately keeps that step type.
- Backend: a workflow step typed `eddi://ai.labs.apicalls` runs, but its calls are never offered
  to the LLM as tools, because `WorkflowTraversal` matches the type string exactly.
  `docs/architecture.md` shows that alias in an example.

## ✨ feat(workforce): approvals in the flow and long-job notices on the user event stream (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/workforce-approvals-job-notices` (from `feat/user-event-stream`)

Phase 8 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §5 Tier 2): **T2.3** (approvals in the flow) and **T2.8** (long jobs with a notification when done), the first UI on the per-user event stream B12 ([user-events.md](../user-events.md)).

### What changed and why

- **One stream per tab, in both signed-in apps.** [`UserEventBridge`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/components/layout/user-event-bridge.tsx), mounted by the Manager's `AppLayout` and Workforce's `WorkforceLayout`, starts the tab's stream once someone is signed in and stops it on sign-out. Invalidations are coalesced per query key (`coalesce-invalidations.ts`): the first runs at once, further ones within 500 ms collapse into one trailing refetch, so a burst of events costs at most one refetch per key per half second and an isolated event is not delayed. Every event invalidates the queries it concerns ([`staleQueryKeys`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/user-event-stream.ts)); every (re)connect refetches them all, since the stream has no replay. The transport is `ApiClient.authorizedFetch`, but the error stays `@eddi/core`'s `ApiError`, which is how the client tells a refusal (401/403/404) it must not retry. `useUserEvent`, `useUserEventStatus` and `useEventDrivenInterval` ([`use-user-events.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/hooks/use-user-events.ts)) are how components listen.
- **Manager:** the approvals inbox, the sidebar badge, the chat's pause notice and the notification bell now update the moment an approval is decided, a pause lands or a notification arrives, instead of at the next 5–10 s poll. Its polls are unchanged.
- **Workforce — what waits on a human (T2.3).** [`ApprovalsIndicator`](../../ui/workforce/src/components/workforce/approvals-indicator.tsx) in the topbar, on every viewport: a count of the person's own paused chats and team sessions and, for an approver or admin, the pauses they may decide; a panel listing each with what its timeout policy does and when (`pausedAt + approvalTimeout`), jumping to the paused turn in Workforce or, for a review, to the Manager's approvals inbox. Data from the existing `GET /agents/pending-approvals` and `GET /groups/pending-approvals` ([`use-workforce-approvals.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/hooks/use-workforce-approvals.ts), [`workforce-approvals.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/lib/workforce-approvals.ts)), on the Manager's query keys, polled every 30 s only while the stream is not open. The sidebar marks a paused chat or team session.
- **By when.** The read-only [`PauseNotice`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/components/hitl/pause-notice.tsx) (the chat in both apps, the Workforce member thread) now says when and how a finite timeout policy decides on its own.
- **Workforce — notices (T2.8, and "told when it continues" of T2.3).** [`WorkforceNotices`](../../ui/workforce/src/components/workforce/workforce-notices.tsx) toasts a finished or stopped long job (turn limit, cost ceiling), a failed scheduled run (and its recovery; see below), a team discussion that ended, a team waiting for *your* turn (`yourTurn: true` only) and a decision on *your own* pause, with "Open" to the conversation or team session — except the one already on screen. [`noticeFor`](../../ui/workforce/src/lib/workforce-notices.ts) picks a generic sentence; events carry no content. Toasts use sonner's polite live region and never take focus.
- **Recurring jobs stay quiet (review fix).** A schedule's every fire raised a toast (and, opted in, an OS notification) — a 60 s heartbeat meant one a minute — and each retry of a failing fire raised another. [`NoticeGate`](../../ui/workforce/src/lib/workforce-notices.ts), one per tab: a routine `job.run_completed` is not shown, only the success that ends a failure streak; `job.failed` is shown once per `scheduleId` until that schedule succeeds again (at most 500 remembered); an event more than 2 minutes old by its `ts` (delivered late) is not shown. No new strings.
- **Known limit, documented in [`use-workforce-approvals.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/hooks/use-workforce-approvals.ts):** an approver or admin gets everyone's pauses capped at 200, so on a busy deployment their own pause can fall outside the page — then missing from "mine" and from the "your conversation continues" check. The endpoints have no owner filter yet.
- **Browser notifications, opt-in.** [`use-browser-notices.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/hooks/use-browser-notices.ts): off by default; turned on from the indicator's panel, the only place the permission prompt is ever requested; sent only while the tab is hidden.
- **i18n:** 3 `hitl.*` keys in `@eddi/app-kit` and 38 `Workforce.approvals.*` / `Workforce.notices.*` keys in Workforce, translated into all 11 locales.
- **Platform Operator:** the prompt says how the apps use the stream, what Workforce shows and why a user may see no notices. `operator-revision.json` → **58**.
- **Docs:** [user-events.md](../user-events.md) ("In the Manager and Workforce"), [`ui/workforce/AGENTS.md`](../../ui/workforce/AGENTS.md).

### Design decisions

- **Refetch, never render the event.** An event says only that something changed; the lists come from the access-checked endpoints, so the UI cannot show something the stream should not have carried.
- **No new endpoint.** "Who must approve" is shown as the person's own pause versus one they may review, which is what the pending-approvals summaries can say; naming the reviewer would need a backend field.
- **A hand-rolled disclosure panel, not the Radix dropdown,** in the topbar: the topbar is in Workforce's first load, and a menu role would also be wrong for a list of links plus a setting.
- **Toast only what is news.** No toast for `approval.pending` (the count says it, and it is usually what the person just did), `team.started` (they started it) or an approver's queue changing.

### Not done

- **Deciding in place in Workforce** (T2.3's "an approver decides in place"): reviews link to the Manager's inbox; the Operator-style inline approval card is a follow-up.
- **Progress for a running long job** (T2.8's "shown as progress"): the stream has no in-progress event for continuations, only `job.finished` / `job.failed`; a backend event would be needed.
- The pause notice's "Review" link still goes to the Manager's conversation page, which an `eddi-user` cannot open.

---

## ✨ feat(ui): "What I remember about you", composer modes, and the `/chat/embed.js` launcher (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/workforce-memory-composer-embed` (on `feat/conversation-search-tool-ui`)

Phase 8 (Tier 2) of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §5): T2.4, T2.5 and T2.9. Frontend, plus one literal JAX-RS path for the launcher. T2.1/T2.2/T2.6/T2.7 and the per-user event stream (B12) are other branches.

### What changed and why

- **"What I remember about you" (T2.4).** A Workforce page at `/workforce/memory` ([`workforce-memory.tsx`](../../ui/workforce/src/pages/workforce/workforce-memory.tsx)), linked as **My memory** from the sidebar footer and from the ⌘K switcher. It lists the signed-in person's own persistent memory, newest first, with the agent that saved each entry (named from the catalog, else its id; a legacy entry without one says so) and its visibility in plain words (*Private*, *Team* with the team's name, *All agents*). It has a search box, **Forget** for one entry, and **Forget everything** behind a confirm that names the consequence: every agent and team, gone from new conversations, cannot be undone, conversations kept. It uses the existing REST API only (`GET /usermemorystore/memories/{userId}`, `DELETE /entry/{id}`, `DELETE /{userId}`), with the token's `preferred_username` as the user id. The backend's `OwnershipValidator` answers 403 for anyone else's, so the page can only show a person their own entries. The backend lets a person delete any of their entries, shared ones included, so the page offers that and says that a shared entry goes for every agent. The reserved `_gdpr_` rows cannot be deleted (400 on the single delete, kept by delete-all), so they are counted and named in a note, not listed. A stored value is a text child, never markdown or HTML. Row shaping is pure, in [`memory-entries.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/lib/memory-entries.ts). The user-memory API and hooks moved from the Manager to `@eddi/app-kit` with `git mv`, and the Manager's admin tab now imports them from there. The MSW handlers already covered the three endpoints.
- **Composer modes (T2.5).** [`composer-mode.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/controller/composer-mode.ts) in `@eddi/conversation` defines the composer's three modes once: the field an agent asked for (`inputField`) wins over the user's lock (secret mode), and the lock wins over text. Only a `password` field (or one with no `subType`, in any case) is a secret. `email` and `text` are ordinary turns of that type. Quick replies show beside text and a requested field, and are hidden under the lock. The widget's `Composer`/`SecretInput` and `@eddi/app-kit`'s `ChatPanel`/`SecretInputField` (Manager, Workforce, test drawer) all follow it. Fixes that came with it:
  - **A secret was echoed in the clear.** The text draft and the masked value were one state in both composers, so turning the lock off showed the typed secret in the textarea. They are now separate: leaving secret mode drops the masked value, the text draft typed before survives the trip, and a secret the server refused (409) returns to the masked field.
  - **The app-kit field was wrong.** It masked and vaulted every `subType`, so an email address was sent as a secret. It also trimmed passwords. It now sends what the shared rule says, and gained the widget's **Type a message instead**.
- **The embed launcher (T2.9).** `<script src="https://eddi.example.com/chat/embed.js" data-agent-id="…" defer>` draws a floating button that opens the `/chat` widget in a panel ([`launcher.ts`](../../ui/chat/src/embed/launcher.ts)). It is framework-free and imports nothing from the widget. It is its own Vite build (`vite.embed.config.ts` → `dist/scripts/js/chat-ui-embed.js`), which the existing Maven `chat-ui*.js` copy, exclude and clean patterns already cover. EDDI serves it from a literal path, `RestHtmlChatResource#fetchEmbedScript` (beside the SPA catch-all, which would otherwise answer with HTML under `nosniff`), as JavaScript with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=3600`, and answers 404 when the jar has no UI.
  - **Settings.** `data-` attributes set the agent id, environment, position, colour (the widget's colour rule), label (a built-in "Chat" per widget locale), open-by-default (not on a phone), language, theme and a token provider.
  - **Token hand-off.** It relays the widget's existing protocol. `tokenOrigin` is set only when the host gives a provider, since the widget would otherwise wait five seconds for a token. A message is answered only from its own iframe's window and EDDI's origin, and the token is posted to that exact origin.
  - **Isolation.** The button and panel live in a shadow root. The launcher never touches cookies or storage and uses no `innerHTML` or `eval`.
  - **Accessibility.** The button is a labelled `<button>` with `aria-expanded`. The panel is a labelled dialog: focus moves to it, Tab is kept inside, Esc or the close button closes it, and focus returns to the button. On a phone the panel is full-screen with safe areas, and there is no animation under reduced motion.
  - **Budget.** `check-bundle-budget.mjs` learned `scripts` entries, and the launcher has its own budget of 5 KB.
- **Strings.** `Workforce.memory.*` (36 keys) in Workforce's eleven locales. T2.5 adds none: the app-kit field reuses `conversation.input.typeInstead`. The launcher's default label and close label are a small table in the script, in the widget's eleven locales.
- **Platform Operator.** The prompt now covers the launcher (what to set, the two settings it fails without: `eddi.chat.frame-ancestors` and the host page's CSP), "My memory" (what a user may delete, the `_gdpr_` rows), and the `inputField` rule. Revision **58** (57 is taken by sibling branches), fingerprint `9f251b17d4cf3b8a`. No allow-list or guard change.
- **Docs.** [`chat-ui-embedding.md`](../chat-ui-embedding.md) section 6, "The launcher": the snippet, the attribute table, authentication, the host page's CSP (`script-src`, `frame-src`, inline style in the shadow root, no SRI), and accessibility. Also [`user-memory.md`](../user-memory.md) ("What the user sees"), [`workforce-guide.md`](../workforce-guide.md) (the composer's modes, "What your agents remember about you"), [`agent-config-authoring.md`](../agent-config-authoring.md) (`inputField`), and the Chat UI and Workforce AGENTS and `@eddi/conversation` README.

### Bundle sizes (gzip -9, KB)

| Entry | Before | After | Budget |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `chat.html` (widget first load) | 169.4 (169,379 B) | 169.5 (169,543 B) | 170 |
| `scripts/js/chat-ui-embed.js` (new) | — | 3.6 (3,647 B) | 5 |
| `workforce.html` | 255.0 | 256.5 | 280 |
| `manage.html` / `welcome.html` | 611.8 | 612.8 | 620 |

No existing budget was raised. Workforce grew 1.5 KB. About 1 KB is the entry chunk (the sidebar link and its icon, the switcher item). The rest is Rolldown splitting the shared `groups` chunk differently (`sse` 45.3 KB + `groups` 35.3 KB, before one 80 KB chunk), now that `SecretInputField` imports `@eddi/conversation`'s main entry. The memory page itself is a lazy route. The widget's growth is the composer split and the shared mode resolver. It is within the budget but not offset (see the decision log).

### Design decisions

- **Search in the browser over what the page shows**, not `GET /{userId}/search`. That endpoint matches keys and string values only (newest 200), while the person thinks in agent and team names, which only the page knows.
- **Forget one has no confirm. Forget everything does.** One entry is one row, and the toast names it. "Everything" is irreversible across every agent, so its dialog says so in full.
- **The launcher's dialog takes focus on open** (Esc works at once, Tab reaches the close button and the conversation). Inside the conversation, Esc keeps the widget's meaning, Stop. A widget-to-host "escape" message would need the widget to know its parent's origin even without a token provider, which costs widget bytes the budget does not have.

### Not done

- Esc from inside the conversation closing the launcher panel (see above).
- Editing a memory entry: the REST upsert is open to the owner, but what an end user may rewrite (an agent's private note, a team's entry) needs a product decision first.
- An E2E spec for `/workforce/memory`: the MSW tier runs with no signed-in user (the auth context has no `user`), and the page correctly asks for nothing then. The Vitest suite covers the flows with a signed-in context.

## ✨ feat(workforce): teams beside chats, @mention hand-off, artifact versions, turn notes (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/workforce-teams-mention-artifacts` (on `feat/conversation-search-tool-ui`)

Part of phase 8 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §5, Tier 2): T2.1, T2.2, T2.6 and T2.7. Frontend only; no backend change. T2.4 (memory), T2.5 (composer modes), T2.9 (embed launcher) and B12 (the per-user event stream) are other branches.

### T2.1 — Teams beside chats

**Already there (phases 2–6):** a team's board streams the discussion live (phase rail, member turns, task board), shows the structured decision (`DecisionRecordCard`: winner, tally, ballots, minority report), the HUMAN member's turn with its prompt and timeout (`HumanTurnBanner`, `POST …/human-input`), the approval pause linked to the Manager, Stop, follow-ups, history; the sidebar lists team sessions; the catalog lists the teams an `eddi-user` may use.

**Added:**

- **Home starts a team session.** Home's picker lists the catalog's teams under the agents (`<optgroup>`s). With a team picked, sending opens its board with the question in **router state, never the URL** ([`workforce-team-start.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/lib/workforce-team-start.ts)), and the board starts a new session with it once (guarded by identity, dropped from the history entry so a reload cannot start it again, ignored when it names another team).
- **Templates.** The preset templates (`/groupstore/templates`, `instantiate`) answer `eddi-editor` and `eddi-admin` only (`IRestGroupTemplates`), and creating a team is authoring — the wizard already offers them to authors. For an `eddi-user`, the listed teams are where a session starts; the guide says so.
- **The decision in plain words.** `decisionSummary` ([`decision-summary.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/team/decision-summary.ts)) turns the record's typed fields into one or two sentences — "The team voted, and “Ship it” won. Members who disagreed: 2." — the decision card's first line, and the board announces it politely when it arrives. Never an agreement's `winner` (a proposal id). Worded without plural forms, so each locale needs one string.
- **Whose turn.** `HumanTurnBanner` takes `viewerIsMember`: "It's your turn, Gregor" for the member, "Gregor's turn — only Gregor or an administrator can answer" for anyone else. The board compares the signed-in username or e-mail with `pendingHumanInput.memberId` only for the wording; the form stays, because the client cannot know which claim the server reads as the principal, and the server decides. A refused submission (`403`) is explained in those words. The banner is a labelled region; the board's status line announces the turn.

### T2.2 — @mention hand-off: the design decision

**No backend support exists for this.** EDDI's one hand-off concept, [cross-team handoff](../team-handoff.md), is an *agent* filing a *task* into a standing team's backlog under two-sided author consent — not a person moving their own question from one conversation to another, and it records nothing in the origin conversation.

So the client-side version, said plainly: typing **@** in a Workforce chat opens a combobox over the catalog's agents and teams (`GET /catalog/*`, so only listed entries the caller may USE; never the agent store, never the chat's own agent). Picking one shows "Hand over to …" above the box; sending **starts a new chat with that agent, or a new team session, with the question as its first message** — through the existing router-state start paths — instead of sending it to this chat's agent. A marker in the origin chat says where it went and links to it; the new conversation shows where it came from and links back.

The hand-off is **recorded in this browser only** ([`workforce-handoffs.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/workforce-handoffs.ts): keyed by principal, at most 100, the question cut to 140 characters, removed at sign-out like the pinned responses). The marker says so in visible text, the guide says so. **Why that is the honest version:** the target agent or team receives an ordinary first message, which is exactly what happened on the server; a marker written *into* the transcript would claim a server-side record that does not exist, and faking one as a message would put client text into the agent's conversation and its context. The store re-renders readers on a write in the same tab and, through the `storage` event, on a change or clear made in another tab (a stale list there would also have overwritten the other tab's record on its next write). Recording it properly is a backend item (a `handoffFrom` on the descriptor, readable by its owner), listed below.

- `ChatPanel` gets two optional props the Manager does not pass: `mention` (the textarea becomes a `role="combobox"` with `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, `aria-activedescendant` over a `listbox`; ↑ ↓, Enter or Tab to choose, Esc closes only the popup — a running turn's Esc-to-stop does not fire — Backspace in an empty box drops the choice; a polite status says how many suggestions and the choice) and `transcriptMarkers` (host lines after a step's last message, at the top for none, at the end when the step was undone). The pure parts are [`lib/chat-mention.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/chat-mention.ts).
- `ChatPanel` now takes a second initial message while mounted (by identity), so a hand-off from one chat to another agent on the same page starts, and reports the conversation it started (`onInitialConversationStarted`) so the record is linked. Files are not handed over; secret mode has no mentions.

### T2.6 — Shared artifacts with versions

**Already there:** `ArtifactsPanel` in the transcript header (current content, a revision count). **Added:** an artifacts button on the board (shown when the session has any, with the count) opens a side panel — the same non-modal slide-over as the Team and Sessions panels, full width on a phone, loaded lazily ([`artifacts-drawer.tsx`](../../ui/workforce/src/components/workforce/artifacts-drawer.tsx)). Inside, `ArtifactVersions` ([`artifact-versions.tsx`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/team/artifact-versions.tsx)): the list, one artifact opened, a **Version** select over the current content plus every revision its `history` keeps (`ARTIFACT_HISTORY_CAP` = 10), each with editor and time; an earlier version says so and offers the current one; "versions before vN are no longer kept" when the history dropped some. No request: the versions arrive with the status read, which the board polls while a discussion runs. Focus goes to the panel's close button once loaded and back to the trigger on Escape or close. One rendering policy for every artifact view, `ArtifactContent` ([`artifact-content.tsx`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/team/artifact-content.tsx)): MARKDOWN through the one renderer with raw HTML escaped, JSON pretty-printed, TEXT plain — `ArtifactsPanel` now uses it too. **Not built:** a diff between versions (a line diff over 256 KB documents needs its own bounded algorithm; switching covers reading), and editing (no endpoint lets a person write an artifact).

### T2.7 — Cascades and guardrails in plain words

What the backend exposes per streamed turn, and nothing more: `cascade_escalation {fromStep, toStep, confidence, threshold, reason, durationMs}`, `llm_retry {reason, attempt}` (in the SSE union since phase 7) and `guardrail_triggered {type, phase, action}`. A new [`turn-notes.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/tool-activity/turn-notes.ts) in `@eddi/conversation/tool-activity` reads the first two (`reason` only), files them per conversation and step like the tool cards (`TurnNotesLog`), and `TurnNotes` renders a sentence per reason — "Another model was tried because the first answer wasn't confident enough." (`low_confidence`), took too long (`timeout`), failed (`error`, `retryable_error`), couldn't be used (`invalid_output`); "The model was asked again because its first reply was empty / was cut off / was stopped by the provider's content filter / wasn't in the expected format / the conversation was too long for it". The guardrail notices the controller already keeps on a reply now read "A safety check removed part of the answer (pii-redaction)." instead of "Guardrail (output): pii-redaction redacted" (`GuardrailNote`; three `chat-message.test.tsx` assertions changed with the wording, the seven `chat.guardrail*` keys they used were removed). Wired into `ChatPanel` (Manager and Workforce chat) under each turn's last message, and into Workforce's member thread. Nothing renders when the stream said nothing.

**Gaps, documented:** a cascade stopped early by `maxCostPerRun` or `maxTotalDurationMs` sends no event, so users are told nothing; the model's name and cost are not shown to end users (the widget's escalation indicator is unchanged); like the tool cards, notes are live only (the stored trace is not client-safe); a turn answered without streaming has none; group member turns stream no such events.

### Bundles, i18n, operator

- First load, gzip: Workforce 256.7 → 256.7 KB (budget 280), Manager 615.3 → 615.3 KB (620), both measured on the base commit and on this branch, the widget 169.4 → 169.4 KB (170): it imports none of this. No budget raised.
- 70 new keys in the eleven locales (turn notes in the tool-activity bundle, team strings in the team bundle, `chat.mention.*` in app-kit's, `Workforce.handoff.*` / board / home in Workforce's).
- **Platform Operator:** the prompt explains the turn notes and their gaps, the browser-only hand-off (not cross-team handoff), Home's teams, the plain decision, the human-turn wording, the artifacts panel, and that templates need an author role. No allow-list change. Revision **57**.
- Docs: [`workforce-guide.md`](../workforce-guide.md) (Home, why an answer came the way it did, @ hand-off, keyboard, a team, administrator notes), [`group-conversations.md`](../group-conversations.md) (humans in Workforce, artifacts in Workforce), `ui/workforce/AGENTS.md`, `ui/manager/AGENTS.md`, the `@eddi/conversation` README.

### Follow-ups

- A server-side hand-off record (the origin on the new conversation's descriptor, owner-readable) so the marker survives devices.
- A `member_tool_result` / member cascade events for group turns; a client-readable redacted trace so tool cards and turn notes survive a reload.
- An event when a cascade's cost or duration ceiling ends escalation.
- A bounded line diff between artifact versions.

## 🔒 fix(workspaces): legacy resources grant at most EDIT; settings load once and revalidate (2026-11-06)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/workspace-legacy-access`)

### What changed and why

- **A resource that predates ownership no longer makes every caller its owner.**
  `DescriptorAccess.effectiveLevel` gave `OWN` to any caller for an unowned descriptor
  under `legacy-visibility=shared`. `OWN` gates delete, share, revoke, visibility and
  move, so any user could publish a colleague's pre-upgrade agent to anonymous callers,
  or move it into `user:<them>` — after which it was owned, and everyone else lost
  access. With enforcement off, the same answer let any user read the grant list of a
  legacy resource. The legacy contribution is now `EDIT`: everyone can still read,
  use, edit and deploy pre-existing resources, so an upgrade hides nothing, but the
  `OWN` acts are admin-only until an owner is assigned with
  `PUT /descriptorstore/descriptors/{id}/shares/owner`. Explicit grants on a legacy
  resource still count in full. It is the rule a resource with no descriptor at all
  already followed — an absent record must not grant authority — though that case,
  in `ResourceAccessGuard.requireLegacyFallback`, stops lower still, at read and use. See
  [Resources that predate ownership](../workspaces.md#resources-that-predate-ownership).
- **The workspace settings cache no longer makes access checks wait for the store.**
  `WorkspaceSettings` re-read the stored settings under a lock when its 5-second cache
  expired, so every request whose access check asked `admitsLegacy()` queued behind a
  database read with no timeout — at every expiry, and for the whole of an outage. It is
  now stale-while-revalidate: one caller re-reads, everyone else is served the last
  values. Each read is numbered when it starts and each adopted write when it lands, and
  the higher number is what stays served, so neither a write adopted meanwhile nor the
  settings page's forced re-read is undone by an older read that answers later. Only the
  very first read, with nothing to serve, is still waited for. No executor is involved.
- **A corrupt stored `legacy-visibility` is validated once, at load.** It used to be
  re-validated, and logged at `WARN`, on every access check. It is now treated as unset
  when read, logged once per distinct bad value, and `GET /workspaces/settings` reports
  it as `DEFAULT` — the default that actually applies.
- **`PUT /workspaces/settings` stamps `updatedAt` to the millisecond.** Mongo keeps
  milliseconds and Postgres microseconds, so the value this instance adopted differed
  from what a replica read back.
- **New `WorkspaceSettingsStoreParityTest`** runs the Mongo and Postgres settings stores
  through the same cases: unknown tenant, full round trip, unset fields read back as
  null, writing null clears a stored value (how `PUT` unsets a setting), tenant
  isolation, timestamp precision. Neither store had a test before.

### Behaviour change for administrators of upgraded deployments

With `eddi.workspaces.enabled=true`, a non-admin can no longer delete, share, publish,
move or read the grants of a resource created before ownership was recorded; the
Manager stops offering those actions on it (it reports `callerLevel: EDIT`). Assign an
owner to each such resource you want someone other than an administrator to manage.
Nothing changes for administrators, or while enforcement is off apart from the grant
list.

**Files:** [`DescriptorAccess.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/DescriptorAccess.java),
[`WorkspaceSettings.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/WorkspaceSettings.java),
[`RestWorkspaces.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/security/spaces/rest/RestWorkspaces.java),
[`workspaces.md`](../workspaces.md); tests in `DescriptorAccessTest`, `ResourceAccessGuardTest`,
`RuntimeWorkspaceSettingsTest` and the new `WorkspaceSettingsStoreParityTest`.

## 🐛 fix(compat): no false TEMPLATE_DOES_NOT_PARSE for a prompt that mentions a vault reference (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/compat-template-parse-vault-prose`)

### What changed and why

Every Platform Operator deployment logged `[TEMPLATE_DOES_NOT_PARSE] llm task '…'.parameters.systemMessage: … empty
expression found {vault:…}, so every render of it fails`, while the agent answered correctly. The warning was a false
positive: at runtime `LlmTask.runTemplateEngineOnParams` first wraps `{vault:…}`, `{vars:…}`, `{connection:…}` and
`{caller:…}` mentions in Qute raw sections (`escapeConfigReferenceMentions`), so a prompt that only documents the syntax
renders fine. The deploy-time lint parsed the unescaped text.

- [`CompatibilityRules.scanLlmTaskTemplates`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/compat/CompatibilityRules.java)
  applies the same escaping before parsing, and only to the task's own `parameters` — the strings the runtime escapes.
  Cascade step parameters, API-call, property and output templates are rendered without it and still warn.
  `AgentCompatibilityLint` uses it for LLM tasks; `LlmTask.escapeConfigReferenceMentions` is now public for this.
- A genuinely broken template next to a mention still warns. No Manager change, so no operator revision bump.

## The team kind of conversation — frontend unification phase 6 (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/conversation-operator-team` (stacked on `feat/workforce-app`, PR #1285)

Phase 6 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md`, §3.2, §7): group discussions become the **team kind** of `@eddi/conversation`, the same controller and components in the Manager and in Workforce, and Workforce's two group transcript renderers become one. The **operator kind** did not move in this phase (below).

**What changed**

- **`@eddi/core/groups`** ([`groups.ts`](../../ui/packages/core/src/groups.ts)) — the discussion contract: every type a stored or live group discussion is made of (`TranscriptEntry`, `GroupConversation`, the decision, tasks, proposals, artifacts), the SSE events and payloads, the group constants and the display fallbacks for styles and entry types, moved out of `@eddi/app-kit`'s `lib/api/groups.ts`, which re-exports the module so no import changed. The group *configuration* type, its normaliser and the calls stay in app-kit (they reference app-only modules). The four HITL types a stored discussion carries about its pause moved to `core/hitl.ts`. Not re-exported from core's main entry, so the Chat UI never resolves it.
- **`GroupDiscussion`** ([`controller/group-discussion.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/controller/group-discussion.ts)) — the framework-free controller of one group's discussion: the state a run builds, `applyGroupStreamEvent` (the reducer every SSE event goes through), and start, approve-and-resume, continue a round, detach, Stop (the deferred Stop, 409 as already ended), reset and `whenAccepted`. A plain store (`subscribe`, `getSnapshot`) over a `GroupTransport`, with an optional host store. It is the code of `@eddi/app-kit`'s `use-group-discussion-stream.ts`, which keeps its whole API and now binds the controller (a transport over the apps' client, one controller per group writing its slot of `useGroupStreamStore`). New framework-free tests: `group-discussion.test.ts` (10). Every existing stream, board and workspace test passes unmodified.
- **`@eddi/conversation/team`** — the team components moved from `@eddi/app-kit` with `git mv`: `TaskBoard`, `DecisionRecordCard`, `StructuredTurnCard`, `StructuredEntryBody`, `NegotiationLedger`, `ArtifactsPanel`, `DiscussionInsights`, `HumanTurnBanner`, the overview (`PhaseRail`, `MemberRoster`, `ParticipationMatrix`, `InteractionMap`, `BidBoard`, `DiscussionOverview`, the view toggle) and the entry readers (`group-utils`, `group-entry-body`, `group-payloads`, `group-decision`, `discussion-styles`, `use-discussion-digest`, ISO durations). Router-free. Their strings (152 keys, nested `groups.*` as before) moved to the package's [`team/i18n/locales`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/team/i18n/locales/en.json) in all eleven locales, merged by `withTeamLocales` from `setupAppI18n`; the translation-quality suite merges them too. `Badge` and the display helpers (`hashColor`, `getInitials`, `formatUsd`, `formatDuration`) moved to `@eddi/ui` (app-kit's `lib/utils` re-exports the helpers). `ConversationMarkdown` moved to `@eddi/conversation/i18next`.
- **One transcript in Workforce.** The history viewer (`conversation-viewer.tsx`) renders a stored discussion through the board's transcript (`board-transcript.tsx`, read-only and opened at the top) instead of its own copies of the question, member, synthesis, error and skipped cards and the phase separator. History now looks like the board, with its pins and "Ask more".
  Two Workforce coverage assertions named copy of the deleted cards ("Final Synthesized Answer", "No content") and now assert the board transcript's synthesis card and "No response generated"; the wave-3 parity test no longer looks for an icon map in the viewer (it has none) and checks that it renders through the board transcript. No other test assertion changed; the moved components' tests changed their import path only.
- **Links** in the September and October archives and one fragment pointed at the files that moved; they were repointed (link targets only).
- **`npm run i18n:check`** counts every `i18n/locales` directory of a package, not only `src/i18n/locales`.
- **Lockfile** — `@eddi/conversation` depends on `@eddi/ui`, `i18next` and `react-i18next` (already in the tree); the lockfile was regenerated in `node:26.13.0`.

**Not done in this phase, and why**

- **The Operator still runs its own turn loop** (`use-operator-chat.ts`). Moving it onto `AgentConversation` needs controller work first: an approval policy hook (resume with per-call decisions and request pinning, poll until the resumed turn settles, the 409 / `awaiting_approval` race that back-fills the ask, executed calls reconstructed from the detailed log). The 2,600 lines of operator store tests pin those call sequences, and the approval UI is security-reviewed. Nothing under `ui/manager/src/lib/operator`, in `lib/api/updates.ts` or `lib/hitl-tool-approvals.ts` changed, so the Stryker scope and the HITL guard tests are untouched. The Platform Operator's prompt and allow-list are unchanged (no operator-visible behaviour moved).
- **The Manager's group transcript** (`discussion-transcript.tsx`, `agent-response-card.tsx`) still renders its own entry cards, around the approval card and editor tools. Moving it onto the shared transcript changes how the Manager's workspace looks and its tests' markup, and is the next step, together with moving `board-transcript.tsx` and the advisor card into `@eddi/conversation/team` behind slots (pins and "Ask more" as `messageActions`).

## ✨ feat(conversations): search my conversations; tool_result on the stream (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/conversation-search-tool-events`, stacked on `feat/conversation-operator-team`)

The backend half of phase 7 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md`, rows B3 and B6; T1.3, T1.6 and T1.9 build on them).

### What changed and why

**B3: search my conversations.**

- `GET /conversationstore/conversations/search?q=&index=&limit=` returns the caller's own conversations whose **title** contains `q`, newest first, as the same descriptors the listing returns. It is paged like the listing: `index` is a page of results, and `index × limit` above 10,000 is a `400`. A page can come back short, because a match whose conversation memory is gone is left out.
- **Owner scope.** The caller's conversations only, for every caller, an administrator included. This is "my conversations"; an administrator who needs everybody's uses the listing's `filter`. The owner goes into the query as an exact `userId` match (`QueryFilter.exact`), so another user's descriptor is never read, and a row-level check repeats it. Because the owner is exact, a page from the store is a page of results, and the listing's owner-scan budget and back-fill are not needed. An anonymous caller with authorization on gets an empty list without a query. With authorization disabled every conversation is searched, as the listing then shows every conversation. A pre-v5.1.6 descriptor that records no owner is not searched; such descriptors predate titles.
- **Matching.** A new `IResourceFilter.ContainsIgnoreCase` is the case-insensitive twin of `Contains`: an escaped regex with the `i` option on MongoDB, an escaped `ILIKE … ESCAPE '\'` on PostgreSQL. Either way `q` is literal text. `q` is normalized the way titles are stored (`ConversationTitles.requireValidSearchQuery`: whitespace collapsed, control and bidi characters dropped) and must be 1 to 100 characters. No new index: the exact owner predicate, which uses the existing `userId` index, narrows the rows first.
- **Privacy.** Only titles are searched. A title never comes from a secret turn (B2 derives it after the turn-end scrub), it lives on the descriptor, and GDPR erasure deletes every descriptor of the user (`GdprComplianceServiceTest.deleteUserData_erasesConversationTitlesWithTheirDescriptors`). The search keeps no index of its own and drops a match without memory, so nothing it finds outlives an erasure. `RestConversationStoreSearchTest` pins title-only matching, the owner scope, the dropped foreign and orphan rows, normalization and paging.
- **Outputs are not searched (follow-up).** They live in the conversation memory documents, not the descriptors, so searching them needs its own index on both backends. They also hold model and tool text that the turn-end scrub does not cover: it removes secret input and secret context, not what a tool echoed. So searching them needs a redaction decision as well as an index, and that was not clearly safe to do here.
- Surfaces: `searchConversations` in the app-kit API layer, an MSW handler, the line in `openapi-operations.json` (added by hand, as for earlier endpoints: no instance was running to refresh it), and [`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md#searching-my-conversations) and [`gdpr-compliance.md`](../gdpr-compliance.md).

**B6: `tool_result` on the SSE stream.**

- After every tool call it announced with `tool_call`, a streamed turn now sends `tool_result {tool, ok, summary}`. `ok` is true when the tool ran and `ToolOutcomeClassifier` reads its result as a success: an HTTP error status, an `error` field or a `NOT_EXECUTED`/`DENIED` status make it false, and so does a refusal (budget, quota, turn deadline). `summary` is the start of the result or of the refusal, at most 200 characters.
- **The same redaction as `toolTrace`.** The redaction `LifecycleManager` applied to the `task_complete` trace moved into `ToolTraceRedaction` (`redactTrace`, `redactValue`), and both channels use it. `ToolTraceRedaction.summary` redacts the **whole** text before cutting it, so a credential that straddles the cut cannot leak its first characters past the redactor's minimum length. A test pins this.
- The event is built in `ToolLoopRunner.executeToolCall`, which now wraps the old body (`runToolCall`), from the trace entry this call wrote. That covers every exit, the mock, budget and quota refusals included, and the resume path of an approved call. No event is sent for a call cut off by a cancellation or an approval pause. A failing sink never breaks the tool loop. The event is plumbed through `ConversationEventSink`, `StreamingResponseHandler`, both `ConversationService` bridges and `RestAgentEngineStreaming` as a typed `ToolResultEvent`.
- It is additive: older clients ignore unknown events. **UI:** `SSEEventType` lists `tool_result`, and `llm_retry`, which the backend already sent. A new contract test in `stream-events.review.test.ts` reads the event names from `RestAgentEngineStreaming.java` and fails when the union and the Java disagree. The parser is in a separate entry, `@eddi/core/tool-events` (`parseToolResult`, `toolResultListener`), which plugs into `AgentConversation`'s `onStreamEvent`. The widget does not import it and its controller is unchanged, so the widget bundle, at its 170 KB budget, does not grow. A widget test pins that nothing of the payload reaches the page.
- No meter: the per-tool counters (`eddi_tool_calls_total` and friends) already count these calls, and the event adds no new outcome.
- Group discussions: not done. A member's turn is not streamed, and only its tool *name* reaches the group stream (`member_tool_call`). A `member_tool_result` needs its own observer, so it is left as a follow-up.

**Platform Operator.** The prompt explains the search (the caller's own conversations only, an admin's too; titles only; not one of its tools) and `tool_result`. The allow-list is unchanged. The search answers only the caller's own conversations, so it would not help the operator inspect anyone else's, and the operator already holds the listing. Revision 55.

### Design decisions

- Titles only, now. Outputs need an index and a redaction rule of their own (see above).
- Owner-exact for administrators as well: the endpoint is the end user's search box, not an admin tool.
- A substring match (escaped regex / `ILIKE`), not a MongoDB text index or a PostgreSQL `tsvector`. Titles are short. Users expect a substring to match ("rom" finds "Rome"), which word-stemmed full-text search does not do. The exact owner predicate keeps the scan to one user's rows, and the existing descriptor query path and both backends' escaping are reused without new schema.

### What's next

- Frontend: the Workforce sidebar search box (T1.3), and tool cards (T1.9) and the "this turn ran tools" notice on edit and regenerate (T1.6) built on `@eddi/core/tool-events`.
- Searching outputs, after the privacy review above. `member_tool_result` for group discussions.

**Files:** [`IRestConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/rest/IRestConversationStore.java), [`RestConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/rest/RestConversationStore.java), [`IResourceFilter.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/datastore/IResourceFilter.java), [`ConversationTitles.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationTitles.java), [`ToolTraceRedaction.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/lifecycle/ToolTraceRedaction.java), [`ToolLoopRunner.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolLoopRunner.java), [`RestAgentEngineStreaming.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/RestAgentEngineStreaming.java), [`tool-events.ts`](../../ui/packages/core/src/tool-events.ts); docs [`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md), [`gdpr-compliance.md`](../gdpr-compliance.md), [`README.md`](../README.md).
