## ✨ feat(conversations): titles, rename and pin; agent appearance and chat controls (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/conversation-titles-agent-appearance`)

The backend half of phase 4 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md`, rows B2 and B4; decisions D7 and D9): what a sidebar of conversations and a per-agent home screen need from the server.

### What changed and why

**B2 — conversation titles, rename, pin.**

- `ConversationDescriptor` gains `title`, `titleSource` (`AUTO`, `LLM`, `USER`) and `pinned`. Stored on both backends as plain descriptor fields; every listing that returns descriptors (`GET /conversationstore/conversations`, MCP `list_conversations`) now carries them. A descriptor written before reads untitled and unpinned.
- **Default title** (`ConversationTitles`, `ConversationTitleService`): the first user message that was not secret, whitespace collapsed, control and bidi characters dropped, cut at a word to `eddi.conversations.title-max-length` (default 80). It is written from the turn's completion callback on exactly the turn that holds that message — a walk over the in-memory steps on every other turn, one write on that one. **Never from a secret turn:** a turn is skipped when the client flagged `secretInput` (a secret `inputField` does), when a `scope: "secret"` property vaulted the input, when the input carries a secret context placeholder, or when an input guardrail withheld it. All of it is read after `Conversation`'s turn-end scrub, so the plaintext is never seen; the next usable message titles the conversation, and with none it stays untitled.
- **Rename and pin:** `PATCH /conversationstore/conversations/{conversationId}` with `{"title", "pinned"}` (either). Owner or `eddi-admin` only, through the same strict owner check as delete (`requireConversationOwnerStrict`; reviewers get nothing); `400` for an empty body or a blank or over-200-character title, `404` for a soft-deleted conversation.
- **A rename is never overwritten.** Title writes are one conditional update per backend — `IResourceStorage.setFieldsIf` (Mongo `updateOne` with the guard in the filter, PostgreSQL `data || ?::jsonb` with the guard in the `WHERE`) — with precedence `USER` > `LLM` > `AUTO`: `AUTO` lands only where there is no title, `LLM` only over `AUTO`, the owner's edit always. No read-modify-write, so a model title arriving after the owner renamed cannot clobber it. PostgreSQL builds the patch as a JSON tree rather than through the persistence mapper, which omits null map entries — a field set to `null` is stored as JSON null on both backends, as the contract says.
- **Model-written titles, opt-in** (`titleGeneration` on the agent, default off): after the first exchange a background virtual thread (at most 8 at once per node; beyond that the default title stays) asks the configured model through the shared `SummarizationService`, as Dream does. Refused by the unpriced-call policy and the tenant cost budget like other model calls; the priced spend is recorded on the tenant budget and reported to spend sinks as the new `SpendEvent.Kind.CONVERSATION_TITLE`, with conversation, agent, version and user. Counted on `eddi.conversation.titles{outcome}` (new Full Metrics dashboard panel).
- **GDPR:** the export's `ConversationExportEntry` carries `title` and `pinned` (live descriptor, else the archived copy of a soft-deleted conversation); erasure already deletes every version of each conversation's descriptor, which is where titles live — now pinned by a test.

**B4 — agent presentation.**

- `appearance {displayName, avatar, accentColor, starters[]}` and `conversationControls {undo, redo, attachments, rating, quickReplies, streaming, rawHtml}` on `AgentConfiguration`, all optional. Validated on every agent write path (REST, ZIP import, sync, setup) by `AgentPresentationValidator`: display name ≤ 80, no control characters; accent `#rgb`/`#rrggbb`; avatar an `https` URL without credentials or a `data:image/png|jpeg|gif|webp;base64` URI ≤ 64 KB (SVG and `http` refused — the browser renders it as `<img>`, EDDI never fetches it); ≤ 6 starters of ≤ 200 characters. `titleGeneration` is validated there too (provider and model required when enabled, `maxLength` 10–200).
- `GET /agents/{agentId}/profile` returns `appearance` (or `null`) and `conversationControls` resolved to booleans — absent switches `true`, **`rawHtml` `false` unless the agent sets it (D9)** — for the deployed version, read through `AgentConfigurationCache`.
- ZIP export/import and agent sync carry the blocks unchanged (they move agent JSON; the export's secret scrub keeps them — tested).
- Manager: a "Presentation & titles" section on the agent page (appearance, controls, model titles), keys in all 11 locales. Platform Operator: the prompt explains appearance, controls, the `rawHtml` default and titles; revision bumped. The `PATCH` is deliberately not in its allow-list — renaming is the conversation owner's act. `eddi-admin` skill mentions the fields. The section is disabled for a read-only viewer like every other section, and changing the title model's provider drops `apiKey` and `baseUrl` from its `parameters` (they belong to the old provider; a vault reference without `allowedHosts` would otherwise go to the new one).
- Chat UI: the review notice and the settled profile lookup now land in one commit (the flag was set a microtask after the notice, and the ref `handleSend` reads synced in a passive effect), so a quick reply clicked right after the notice appears is no longer dropped.

### Design decisions

- Title derivation runs in the completion callback, not as a lifecycle task: it is a session concern of the turn's outcome, and reading after the scrub is what makes "never from a secret turn" hold for every way a turn becomes secret.
- Controls are presentation only: hiding undo does not disable the endpoint; `rating: false` can hide but not enable ratings.
- `rawHtml` is reported now; the widget keeps rendering HTML until its own phase ships, so agents can opt in first (`docs/chat-ui-embedding.md` → "Upgrading").

### What's next

- **Catalog (#1273):** once both land, `IRestCatalog.CatalogAgent` should carry `appearance` (and probably `conversationControls`) — `AppearanceProfile.of(config.getAppearance())` from the same deployed version the catalog already resolves; the operator revision files of both PRs will conflict and need one more bump.
- **Frontend:** Workforce sidebar (titles, rename/pin via the `PATCH`, grouping by date), home with per-agent starters, and the widget honouring `conversationControls` (including `rawHtml`). A `pinned` filter on the listing would let a sidebar fetch pinned items regardless of paging; not added yet.

**Files:** [`ConversationTitles.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/ConversationTitles.java), [`ConversationTitleService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/ConversationTitleService.java), [`ConversationDescriptor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/descriptor/model/ConversationDescriptor.java), [`IResourceStorage.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/datastore/IResourceStorage.java), [`RestConversationStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/rest/RestConversationStore.java), [`AgentPresentationValidator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/agents/AgentPresentationValidator.java), [`IRestAgentProfile.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/api/IRestAgentProfile.java), [`GdprComplianceService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/gdpr/GdprComplianceService.java); docs [`agent-config-authoring.md`](../agent-config-authoring.md), [`chat-ui-embedding.md`](../chat-ui-embedding.md), [`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md), [`gdpr-compliance.md`](../gdpr-compliance.md), [`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md), [`metrics.md`](../metrics.md).

## 📝 docs(connections): Google Workspace recipe — Gmail and Drive as per-user LLM tools (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`docs/google-workspace-tools-recipe`)

### What changed and why

The Google Workspace plan's conversation-tools slice. It is configuration apart from one engine
addition, the `encoder:mimeHeader` template function that `createDraft` uses for the subject (see
below), so the recipe needs a build that includes it. Per-user Google access was the motivating case for connections, but no worked config
showed it end to end, and the prerequisites (verified identity, allowlisted credential endpoints,
Google's publishing-status rules) were scattered or missing.

- **Recipe** — [`docs/agent-configs/google-workspace-tools/`](../agent-configs/google-workspace-tools/),
  an importable agent in the ZIP layout: the `google-workspace` connection
  (`OAUTH2_AUTHORIZATION_CODE`, `PER_USER`, scopes `gmail.readonly` + `gmail.compose` +
  `drive.readonly`), a Gmail httpcalls config (`searchMail`, `readThread`, `createDraft`), a Drive one
  (`searchDrive`, `readDriveFile`), an LLM task with httpcall tools on and built-in tools off, and the
  setup wizard's catch-all rule. Every tool has an `argumentSchema` with `unknownFields: reject` and
  authenticates with `"Authorization": "${connection:google-workspace}"`.
- **[`google-workspace.md`](../google-workspace.md)** (new, in `SUMMARY.md` under Connections) —
  prerequisites (OIDC-verified users and which entry points are `SELF_ASSERTED`; connection settings
  with both Google origins on the credential-endpoint allowlist; the OAuth client; Linked accounts,
  including that only an admin can *start* a link from that page), Google's publishing-status table,
  installation, and the security notes below.
- **[`email-integration.md`](../email-integration.md#gmail-and-google-workspace)** — the Gmail note
  expanded: why an inbound Gmail integration is refused at save (authorization-code grants are
  `PER_USER`, a poll has no verified principal — `ConnectionResolver.requireMailLoginTarget`), that app
  passwords over `BASIC` are not blocked but untested, and what is planned (admin-linked `SERVICE`
  grant, domain-wide delegation).
- **`StrictBoundaryShippedConfigsTest`** now maps `.connection.json` to `ConnectionConfiguration`, so
  the recipe's connection — and any future fixture under `connections/` — is parsed strictly instead
  of being counted as skipped.
- Cross-links from [`connections.md`](../connections.md#see-also) and
  [`agent-config-authoring.md`](../agent-config-authoring.md#reference-implementation).

### Design decisions

- **No send tool; drafts only.** A model that reads mail and can send is an exfiltration channel for
  prompt injection in mail bodies. The page says how to gate a send tool behind
  `hitlConfig.toolApprovals`, and that `gmail.compose` already permits sending — the "no send" is the
  tool set's, not the token's.
- **CR/LF in `to`/`subject` is neutralised in the template, not rejected by the schema.** The argument
  schema check deliberately drops `pattern` (backtracking risk on model text), so a pattern forbidding
  `\r\n` would accept everything. Instead `to` has CR and LF removed with `.replace(…)` and `subject`
  goes through the new `encoder:mimeHeader`; the body is `base64Mime` after the header block.
- **`encoder:mimeHeader` (new, `EddiTemplateExtensions`).** The first version wrapped the whole subject
  in one `=?UTF-8?B?…?=` word, which breaks RFC 2047's 75-character word limit from 46 UTF-8 bytes up (24
  `é`); the review caught it. The function always B-encodes, ASCII included, so it keeps the CR/LF
  guarantee; Jakarta Mail's `MimeUtility.encodeText` was rejected because it leaves all-ASCII text,
  CR and LF included, unencoded. Words carry at most 39 bytes (64 characters framed, so `Subject: `
  plus the first word fits a 76-character line), split on code points and joined with CRLF SP.
  `EncoderExtensionsTest` round-trips them through `MimeUtility.decodeText`.
- **`StrictBoundaryShippedConfigsTest.shippedConnectionsImport`.** The strict sweep swallows malformed
  JSON and wrong value types, and the importer silently skips a connection it cannot read, leaving the
  agent's `${connection:…}` reference unresolved. Every shipped `*.connection.json` is now read as
  `RestImportService` reads it and passed through `ConnectionConfiguration.validate()`. Verified against qute-core 3.40.1.1: a raw CR/LF inside a Qute string literal is kept,
  and nested namespace calls work as `str:concat` arguments.
- **`readThread` returns Gmail's base64url body parts as-is** — templating has no decoder; documented
  as a limitation rather than worked around.

### What's next

A real `pattern` (or a narrower "no control characters" keyword) for tool arguments would let such
fields be refused rather than rewritten. Admin-linked `SERVICE` grants and domain-wide delegation for
Gmail as an inbound channel are later slices of the plan.

---

## ✨ feat(connections): signed-JWT credentials — OAUTH2_JWT_BEARER and private_key_jwt (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/connections-jwt-bearer`) — slice C1 of the Google Workspace plan

### What changed and why

A service account that proves itself with a private key instead of a client secret could not be
expressed as a connection: Google service accounts (RFC 7523 JWT bearer grant) and Entra apps
registered with a certificate (`private_key_jwt`) both need EDDI to sign an assertion.

- **`AuthType.OAUTH2_JWT_BEARER`** (new; `isOAuth()` true, new `isServiceAccount()` shared with
  `OAUTH2_CLIENT_CREDENTIALS`). `SERVICE` binding only. `oauth.tokenUrl`, `oauth.privateKey`
  (vault reference), optional `clientId` (issuer), `privateKeyId` (`kid`), `audience` (defaults to
  the token URL), `scopes`. The assertion is RS256 via the JDK `Signature` — no new dependency —
  with `iss`, `sub = iss`, `aud`, `scope`, `iat`, `nbf`, a unique `jti` and a five-minute `exp`.
- **`oauth.clientAuthMethod: "private_key_jwt"`** on `OAUTH2_CLIENT_CREDENTIALS` and
  `OAUTH2_AUTHORIZATION_CODE`: `privateKey` replaces `clientSecret`, and every token request
  (mint, code exchange, refresh) carries a freshly signed `client_assertion` (`aud` = token URL,
  unique `jti`). Optional `certificateThumbprint` (hex or base64url) in `x5t#S256` (default) or
  `x5t`, chosen by `certificateThumbprintHeader`; the digest length must match the header. The
  default stays `client_secret_basic`. The existing `clientAuthMethod` field was extended rather
  than a second `clientAuth` field added, so one field says how the client authenticates.
- **`SigningKey`** (new, `connections/oauth`) parses a PKCS#8 PEM or a whole Google service-account
  JSON key file from ONE vault entry (`private_key`, `client_email`, `private_key_id`; `token_uri`
  deliberately ignored). Refuses PKCS#1, encrypted keys, non-RSA keys and RSA below 2048 bits with
  fixed messages and no exception cause, because a JSON parser's message quotes the key.
  `toString()` is redacted. **`JwtAssertions`** (new) builds and signs both assertions.
- **`OAuthTokenClient.jwtBearer`**; client authentication factored into `authenticateClient`;
  the token-URL allowlist and https checks factored into `requireUsableTokenEndpoint` and run
  before anything is signed. **`OAuthTokenService`** mints JWT bearer grants through the same
  cache/single-flight as `client_credentials` and re-mints on expiry or `REFRESH_FAILED`;
  `OAuthConfig.clientCredentialReference(AuthType)` picks `privateKey` or `clientSecret` for the
  service and the authorization callback alike.
- **Validation** (`ConnectionConfiguration`): `privateKey` reference-only; a field the chosen flow
  would ignore (`clientSecret` beside a key, `authorizationUrl`/`extraAuthParams`/thumbprint on JWT
  bearer, `audience` outside it) is refused; identifiers are bounded literals.
  `RestConnectionStore` applies the vars→vault check to `oauth.privateKey`;
  `ConnectionReadRedactor` redacts a legacy literal `privateKey` for non-admins.
- **Manager**: the new auth type in the chooser, labels and badges; private-key, thumbprint and
  audience fields; and **connection templates as data** — JSON files under
  `ui/manager/src/lib/connection-presets/` (Google service account, Entra app with a certificate)
  offered by the New connection dialog. New i18n keys are translated for `de`; other locales carry
  the English text until translated.
- **Docs**: `docs/connections.md` — two new quick-start sections, model and redaction updates, a
  security rule on private keys.
- **Review follow-ups.**
  - `oauth.privateKeyId` is refused on a client-secret flow, which never builds an assertion. The
    refusal names the effective method, `client_secret_basic`, when the field is null.
  - `OAUTH2_JWT_BEARER` now refuses every `clientAuthMethod` except the stored default.
  - The Manager's `isThumbprint` reads a value exactly as `canonicalThumbprint` does: only colons and
    spaces removed, a 128-character cap.
  - A blank thumbprint is refused for `private_key_jwt` when the token URL is an Entra host. Entra
    identifies the key only by the `x5t` header. Providers that use `kid` keep it optional.
  - The two presets' labels and descriptions are translated in every locale. The JSON text is the
    fallback.
  - The private-key picker and the thumbprint field announce their hint and error.
  - `docs/connections.md` names the `openssl -fingerprint` digest, because the default is SHA-1.

### Decisions

- Assertion lifetime five minutes, not Google's one-hour maximum: it is presented once,
  immediately, and an intercepted one is replayable until it expires.
- A JSON key file is parsed when used, not at save: resolving a secret only to inspect it is a use
  of it. Save time checks only that `privateKey` is a vault reference.
- The Platform Operator was not changed: it has no connection tools or connection section (writes
  are admin-only by design), and its one mail line naming `OAUTH2_CLIENT_CREDENTIALS` stays
  accurate — a Google service account cannot log into a mailbox without domain-wide delegation.

### Next

C2 (domain-wide delegation: `sub` = a Workspace user) and C5 (keyless signing) build on
`JwtAssertions`.

---

## ✨ feat(outbound): a Vert.x engine for OutboundHttp, with a pre-send connected-peer check (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/outbound-vertx-engine`)

Phase 4 of [`planning/http-client-consolidation-plan.md`](../../planning/http-client-consolidation-plan.md), which this branch also adds — it was referenced by Phases 1 and 2 but never merged.

### What changed and why

On the JDK engine the facade can only re-check a target **after** the exchange, so a DNS-rebinding request has already been sent blind. Moving `httpcalls` (Phase 5) onto that engine would lose the pre-send check its own Vert.x client has today. This adds the engine Phase 5 needs.

- **`VertxOutboundEngine`** (`engine/httpclient/outbound`) — the core Vert.x `HttpClient` of the Quarkus `Vertx`, one client per profile (TLS and cleartext separately for `protocol=auto`). No redirects, no decompression, no Vert.x tracing (the facade writes and strips trace headers). The request target is built from the validated `URI`'s parts; a `URI` with no host is refused rather than re-parsed (security review H2).
- **`OutboundEngine.Hop.peerCheck`** — run on the address the connection reached, after it is assigned and before a byte is written. It runs on every request, so a pooled connection is judged each time it is handed out. A refused connection is closed, not just reset: resetting a request with nothing written yet hands an HTTP/1 connection back to the Vert.x pool, still open to the refused address until the keep-alive timeout, and the close is what evicts it. The JDK engine ignores it; the post-exchange recheck stays on both. Skipped through a forward proxy, whose peer is the proxy.
- **`ConnectedPeers`** — the address rules, once: `STRICT` refuses loopback/private/link-local/metadata peers, `METADATA_ONLY` metadata/link-local ones, `HOPS_ONLY` only on redirect hops. `HttpClientModule` parses peers through it.
- **`VertxBodyStream`** — the event-loop-to-blocking bridge: bounded queue (pause at 256 KiB, resume below 64 KiB), ordered end/failure, reset on early close, and a close that wakes a blocked reader.
- **Selection** — `eddi.http.outbound.engine` now takes `jdk` or `vertx`; new per-profile `eddi.http.outbound.<profile>.engine` and `…max-connections-per-host` (fails startup on a `jdk` profile). `apicalls` defaults to `vertx` — no caller sends through it until Phase 5, so nothing changes at runtime today. `ProfileRoutedEngine` dispatches; the Vert.x engine is built only when a profile uses it.
- **Metrics** — `eddi.http.outbound.requests` and `eddi.http.outbound.duration` gain an `engine` tag.
- **Docs** — `configuration-reference.md` (new keys), `security.md` (DNS rebinding table), `application.properties` comments.

### Tests

`VertxOutboundHttpServerTest` reruns the whole `OutboundHttpServerTest` suite on Vert.x; `VertxOutboundEngineTest` (loopback: refused peer receives zero bytes, host differential, cap mid-stream, deadline, proxy, pool-limit queuing); `VertxOutboundEnginePeerRefusalTest` (mocks: a refused connection is closed before the reset and nothing is sent, a second request on the same pooled connection is checked and refused again, an allowed peer's connection stays open); `VertxOutboundEnginePoolIT` (loopback: two refused requests to one origin, pool limit 1, open two connections, each closed by the client with zero bytes received); `VertxBodyStreamTest`; `OutboundEngineSelectionTest` (settings, and which peer check each hop carries).

### Design decisions

- The peer check is a callback on the hop, so the rules sit in the facade and an engine only says where it connected.
- A request with its own `hopValidator` gets no peer check on redirect hops: its validator decides what a hop may reach.
- Cleartext `auto` is HTTP/1.1 on Vert.x — neither JDK-style `h2c` upgrade nor prior-knowledge HTTP/2.

### What's next

Phase 5 (`httpcalls` onto the facade). It needs the previous hop's connected class and the allow-list in the peer check (plan §5 item 5), and pool gauges for Vert.x profiles.

## ⚡ feat(rag): pgvector v2 layout — HNSW index, indexed jsonb metadata, pooled connections, batched inserts (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/pgvector-scale-layout`)

Slice T2 (pgvector throughput) of the enterprise ingestion plan. `EmbeddingStoreFactory.buildPgVector`
built langchain4j's `PgVectorEmbeddingStore` from host/port/user/password: no vector index (every
retrieval an exact scan), metadata in a `json` column nothing can index (every `removeAll` by document
id and owning source a sequential scan), and a new physical connection for every operation. At ten
million chunks retrieval took seconds and each document replacement scanned the table.

### What changed

- **New `PgVectorSchema`** ([`PgVectorSchema.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/PgVectorSchema.java)) —
  the table layouts and EDDI's own DDL. A table EDDI creates gets the **v2 layout**: `metadata jsonb`,
  an HNSW index on the embedding (`vector_cosine_ops` — langchain4j orders by `<=>`), and b-tree
  expression indexes on `(metadata->>'documentId', metadata->>'sourceKey')` and
  `(metadata->>'sourceKey', metadata->>'runId')`. Index names are unqualified, lower-case and at most 63
  characters (a long table name is shortened with a digest).
- **New `EddiPgVectorEmbeddingStore`** ([`EddiPgVectorEmbeddingStore.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/EddiPgVectorEmbeddingStore.java)) —
  a subclass that overrides the protected `getConnection()` to `SET hnsw.ef_search` on every borrowed
  connection, and splits `addAll` into `insertBatchSize`-row batches.
- **New `PgVectorConnectionPools`** ([`PgVectorConnectionPools.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/PgVectorConnectionPools.java)) —
  Agroal pools (already on the classpath through `quarkus-agroal`), one per (host, port, database,
  user, password), keyed on a SHA-256 digest, bounded like the MongoClient cache, `minSize` 0 with a
  two-minute reap so an abandoned pool holds no connection. Closed by `clearCache()` and `@PreDestroy`.
  Max size: new property `eddi.rag.pgvector.pool.max-size` (default 10). The database name is
  URL-encoded into the JDBC URL so it cannot add driver properties.
- **`EmbeddingStoreFactory.buildPgVector`** now parses the new parameters, borrows from the pool,
  creates the `vector` extension only if it is missing (langchain4j ran `CREATE EXTENSION` on every
  connection, which needs CREATE privilege even when it is a no-op), inspects the table, picks the
  layout, builds through langchain4j's `datasourceBuilder()` and then creates the v2 indexes.
- New `storeParameters`: `layout` (`auto`|`v1`|`v2`), `vectorIndex` (`hnsw`|`none`), `hnswM` (16),
  `hnswEfConstruction` (64), `hnswEfSearch` (40), `insertBatchSize` (500). Range-checked; messages name
  the parameter, never the value.
- Docs: [`rag.md`](../rag.md#pgvector-at-scale) (new "pgvector at scale" section, parameter table,
  limits), [`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md#knowledge-bases-rag) (pool size).
- Tests: `PgVectorSchemaTest` (parameters, layout resolution, DDL, index names, JDBC URL, batching and
  ef_search against mocked JDBC); `PgVectorScaleLayoutIT` (pgvector/pgvector:pg16) — indexes exist, the
  store's own `removeAll` and search statements use them (plans captured with `auto_explain`,
  `enable_seqscan=off`), a pre-existing json table keeps working untouched, `layout: v1`, pool sharing.

### Design decisions

- **Layout chosen by the table, not by a default on the knowledge base.** `layout: auto` (the default)
  keeps an existing table in the layout its `metadata` column type says it has and creates a missing
  one in v2. A store-parameter default of `v2` would have applied to every existing knowledge base too
  (none carries the parameter), and EDDI would then have built an HNSW index over a production table
  from inside a request — blocking writes for as long as it takes. An explicit layout that contradicts
  an existing table is refused, naming the way out (new table, re-ingest).
- **jsonb + expression indexes, not column-per-key.** Verified in the langchain4j 1.23.0-beta33 jar
  (javap): `JSONFilterMapper` emits `(metadata->>'key')::<type>` for `isEqualTo` and
  `metadata->>'key'` for `isIn`/`isNotIn`; the `::text` cast on a text expression is dropped by the
  parser, so the expression indexes match (the IT proves it on the real statements). Column-per-key
  would also index, but `ColumnsMetadataHandler` silently drops every key without a column — the URL,
  title, source name and loader metadata.
- **EDDI creates the indexes, not langchain4j.** langchain4j's builder offers only IVFFlat, and its
  metadata-index names are `<table>_<key>`, invalid SQL for a schema-qualified table.
- **ef_search per borrow, session-level.** langchain4j runs in autocommit (a `SET LOCAL` would end
  with its own statement) and its only hook is `getConnection()`. Pools are shared across knowledge
  bases, so the value is set on every borrow — one extra round trip per operation.
- HNSW is skipped with a warning above 2000 dimensions (pgvector's limit) and when the server's
  pgvector is older than 0.5.0; the store then works with exact scans, as before.
- **The DDL checks its own inputs (CodeQL `java/sql-injection`).** `PgVectorSchema` no longer relies on
  `EmbeddingStoreFactory.physicalName` alone: `quotedTable` re-checks each part of the (optionally
  schema-qualified) table name against `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]{0,62}` and emits it as a quoted,
  lower-cased identifier — the same table PostgreSQL resolves langchain4j's unquoted name to — and the
  index names are quoted too. `m`, `ef_construction` and `ef_search` are range-checked ints where they
  meet SQL; the table lookup in `inspect` was already a bind parameter.
- **A store holds a handle, not a pool.** `PgVectorConnectionPools.get` returns a `DataSource` that
  looks the login's pool up on every `getConnection()`, so a size eviction cannot strand a cached store
  or a running ingestion: the next borrow reopens the pool, and each borrow counts as use of it. An
  evicted pool with connections still borrowed (Agroal metrics, `activeCount()`) is retired rather than
  closed under them, and closed on a later lookup once they are returned; `clearCache()` and
  `@PreDestroy` still close every pool. A borrow and a close of the same pool exclude each other through
  a per-pool read/write lock (borrow under the read lock, from the `closed` check through
  `getConnection()`; every close under the write lock, from the `activeCount()` check through
  `close()`), so a borrow that had looked the pool up before its eviction can no longer finish between
  the "nothing borrowed" check and the close; eviction and the retired sweep use `tryLock` and retire or
  skip a pool a borrow is in, rather than stall a lookup. Tested in `PgVectorConnectionPoolsTest` with
  fake pools, including a gated borrow that is inside `getConnection()` while its pool is evicted.
- **An unqualified table is inspected in `current_schema()`**, where langchain4j's
  `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` creates it, not wherever the `search_path` first finds the name — with
  `search_path = myschema, public`, `public.docs` would otherwise decide the layout of the new
  `myschema.docs`.
- **Existing indexes are looked up before their DDL runs** (`to_regclass` on the bound, schema-qualified
  index name): `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` takes a `SHARE` lock before it finds the index, so on every
  store build it would queue behind open write transactions. `IF NOT EXISTS` stays for concurrent builds.

- **Review follow-ups (CodeRabbit)**: an index that exists but is invalid (a failed `CREATE INDEX
  CONCURRENTLY`) or sits on another table is refused with `DROP INDEX` instructions instead of being taken as
  usable; the table layout is re-checked after the store has created the table, so two racing builds that ask
  for different layouts fail rather than run with the wrong metadata handling; the HNSW index name is logged,
  and `docs/rag.md` explains the unqualified-index-name rule for `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` and that
  `vectorIndex: none` does not remove an existing index.

### Follow-ups

- A Manager one-click "move to the v2 layout" action (re-embed into a new table) — out of scope here.
- Manager RAG editor fields for the new parameters (they can be set as raw `storeParameters` today).
- `hnsw.iterative_scan` (pgvector 0.8) for filtered queries on shared tables.

## ✨ feat(rag): delete a document ingested through `/ingest` (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/rag-document-delete`)

### What changed and why

A client that pushes text through `POST /ragstore/rags/{id}/ingest` could supersede a document
(`replace=true`) but never remove one. `DELETE /ragstore/rags/{id}/documents?version=N&documentName=…`
now does, with the semantics of `replace=true`:

- **The same chunks.** `RagIngestionService.delete` removes by the metadata ingestion writes —
  `source = documentName` and this knowledge base's `kbId` — on this knowledge base's own store
  (`EmbeddingStoreFactory.getOrCreate`, not `getOrCreateForIngestion`, so a delete can never replace
  an in-memory store). Every version of the name goes, legacy chunks without an ingestion id
  included; another knowledge base's document of the same name in a shared store does not.
- **The same lock.** The per-document `ReplaceKey` lock is now taken through `documentLock`, by
  replacements and deletes alike, so a delete never lands between a replacement's store and its
  cleanup (where it would take the new chunks too and leave a "completed" replacement over an empty
  document). A delete waits up to 30 seconds and then answers `409`.
- **A name is required.** Missing, blank or `unnamed` is a `400`, as for `replace=true`.
- **EDIT**, checked first, like ingestion.
- **Outcomes.** `200` deleted (also for a name nothing was ingested under); `200` with a `warning`
  when the store cannot delete by metadata; `503` when the store fails, so the client retries.
- **Source-owned names are refused (`409`).** A source's chunks carry `sourceName`/`sourceKey` and no
  `source` key, so the delete filter cannot touch them — but answering "deleted" while they stay
  retrievable would mislead. `RagSourceIngestionService.sourceHoldingDocument` checks the knowledge
  base's sources: an upload source's file names and ids, and the non-tombstoned documents a crawling
  source's state records. A hit is refused with the source's id and the endpoint to use instead.

**Platform Operator:** the endpoint stays outside the allow-list, like every RAG write
(`planning/operator-write-scope-plan.md` §5). `grantsKnowledgeBaseAuthoring` now counts it, and the
read-only line says the operator cannot delete a document either; revision bumped.

No MCP tool ingests into a knowledge base (`McpAdminTools` reads `rag` but deliberately does not write
it), so there is no delete tool to add and no plugin skill to update.

### Judgment calls

- Ownership is checked by name against the sources' state, not by chunk metadata: listing chunks
  needs a similarity search (an embedding call) and still could not express "has a `sourceKey`".
  Consequence: a document pushed through `/ingest` under the same name as a source's file cannot be
  deleted here until that file is removed.
- An ingestion without `replace` takes no lock, so one in flight when a delete answers can still
  store its chunks afterwards. Documented rather than changed.

**Files:** [`RagIngestionService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/rag/RagIngestionService.java),
[`IRestRagIngestion.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/IRestRagIngestion.java),
[`RestRagIngestion.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/rest/RestRagIngestion.java),
[`RagSourceIngestionService.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/RagSourceIngestionService.java),
[`rag.md`](../rag.md#deleting-a-document), the operator's
[`system-prompt.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/operator/system-prompt.ts),
[`tool-scopes.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/operator/tool-scopes.ts) and
[`operator-revision.json`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/operator/operator-revision.json); tests in
`RagIngestionServiceTest`, `RagSourceIngestionServiceTest` and the new
`RestRagIngestionDeleteDocumentTest`.

## ✨ feat(hitl): approval bound to the exact plan — `planHash` (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/hitl-plan-hash`)

### What changed and why

A HITL approval approved "this pause", but nothing tied it to the exact arguments or actions that then ran. Every pause now gets a **plan hash** when it is committed — inspect → plan hash → apply only with that hash:

- **What is hashed** ([`HitlPlanHash`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/HitlPlanHash.java)): SHA-256 over a canonical JSON document, reported as `v1:` + 64 hex. A tool pause covers the conversation, agent and version, the batch's `pauseEpoch`, a per-pause secret nonce, and every pending call in order (`callId`, `toolName`, `source`, `argsTruncated`, canonicalized raw arguments, request fingerprint when pinned). A rule (turn-level) pause covers the bookmark (pause id, workflow, task index) and the paused step's actions without `PAUSE_CONVERSATION`. A group pause covers the paused phase's transcript, the tasks awaiting approval and the synthesized answer.
- **Canonicalization**: keys sorted by UTF-16 code unit, arrays in order, numbers by value (`1` = `1.0` = `1e0`), strings as parsed (escapes decoded, no Unicode normalization); arguments that are not exactly one JSON document (duplicate keys, anything after the first value) are hashed as their exact text. The existing `JacksonCanonicalizer` was not reused because it keeps Jackson's number formatting (`1` ≠ `1.0`).
- **Stored with the pause**: `hitlPlanHash` on the conversation (cleared with the bookmark) and `planHash` + `planNonce` on the pending tool batch. Computed in `ConversationHitlService#populateHitlTimeoutBookmark`, i.e. after the turn's secret scrub rewrote the batch and before the store, so it covers exactly what is persisted. The nonce is never served (approver projection nulls it, names-only projection never copies it, the Mongo and PostgreSQL tail reads leave it on the server).
- **Checks** ([`HitlPlanBinding`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/HitlPlanBinding.java), one rule for both surfaces): a decision whose `planHash` is not the pause's → `409` before the CAS and again on the snapshot the resume runs; the resume re-derives the hash from the loaded plan and refuses (`409`, `WARN hitl.plan.changed`, pause restored) when it no longer matches; `ToolLoopResumer` re-derives it once more immediately before an approved call runs (`NOT_EXECUTED`, "the plan changed after it was approved").
- **Compatibility**: a decision without `planHash` is accepted and logged (`hitl.plan.not_presented`) unless strict binding is on — `hitlConfig.requirePlanHash` (agent or group) or `eddi.hitl.plan-hash.strict=true` — then it is a `400` and the pause is not consumed. `system:*` decisions are exempt. Pauses committed before this change have no hash and are decided as before, also under strict binding (`hitl.plan.legacy`).
- **A hash that cannot be computed fails closed under strict binding**: `HitlPlanHash.commit` never throws, so a hashing bug cannot stop pauses from committing, but the pause it could not hash stores the marker `"unavailable"` (conversation `hitlPlanHash` and batch `planHash`; it persists through both stores like any hash) instead of null. A null would have made it indistinguishable from a legacy pause, which strict binding accepts unbound. `HitlPlanBinding.check` refuses a human decision on a marked pause under strict binding (`409`, the pause is restored as for a mismatch). Without strict binding it accepts the decision, logs `WARN hitl.plan.unavailable` and records it as `PLAN_HASH_UNAVAILABLE`. `approval-status` (REST, MCP) and Slack cards serve `""` for the marker, and it never matches. Group plans are derived at decision time, not stored, so a failed derivation already fails the approve.
- **Surfaces**: REST `approval-status` (`planHash`) and the resume/approve bodies (`HitlDecision.planHash`); MCP `get_approval_status` / `get_group_approval_status` report it, `resume_conversation` / `approve_group_phase` take an optional `planHash` (`PLAN_CHANGED`, `PLAN_HASH_REQUIRED`); Slack buttons carry it as a fourth value field and the edit/reject modals keep it in their metadata, and a card whose plan is no longer the pending one is replaced by a "Not applied — the request has changed" notice without buttons; the Manager's `lib/hitl-pause-binding.ts` sends the shown plan from every approval surface, and the operator card sends it too.
- **Audit**: `hitl.approval` entries carry `planHash` and `planBinding` (`MATCHED` / `NOT_PRESENTED` / `SYSTEM` / `LEGACY_UNHASHED` / `PLAN_HASH_UNAVAILABLE`); every task a resume runs carries `input.hitlPlanHash`; `hitl_approved` trace entries carry `planHash`.
- **Platform Operator**: system prompt documents plan binding and strict mode; revision 60 → 61. It also names the `PLAN_HASH_UNAVAILABLE` outcome and what a pause whose hash could not be computed does under strict and non-strict binding.

**Docs:** [hitl.md → Plan Binding](../hitl.md#plan-binding-planhash), [hitl-review-and-audit.md](../hitl-review-and-audit.md), [hitl-approval-context.md](../hitl-approval-context.md), [audit-ledger.md](../audit-ledger.md), [mcp-server.md](../mcp-server.md), [configuration-reference.md](../configuration-reference.md), [cryptography.md](../cryptography.md).

### Design decisions

- **Strict mode at two levels** (agent/group `hitlConfig.requirePlanHash` and deployment `eddi.hitl.plan-hash.strict`), either one enabling it: a designer protects high-stakes agents without breaking other clients; an operator turns it on everywhere once all clients send the hash. A failed config read counts as strict (fail closed).
- **Group plans are derived, not stored**: an `AWAITING_APPROVAL` discussion cannot change except through the approval, so the read-time hash is the stored state's hash.
- **Amended arguments are not re-hashed**: the hash binds what the reviewer was shown; the amendment is the reviewer's own recorded change.
- **The tool-call plan omits the pause id** (it uses the batch's `pauseEpoch` instead) so the tool loop can re-derive it after the bookmark is cleared.

### What's next

- Strict binding could become the default once every first-party client has shipped a release that sends the hash.

## 💬 refactor(manager): the Manager's chat on `AgentConversation` (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`refactor/manager-chat-on-conversation`, stacked on `feat/ui-eddi-conversation`)

Phase 3c of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §1.3, §2.5, §3.2, §7 phase 3): the Manager's 1:1 chat runs on [`@eddi/conversation`](../../ui/packages/conversation/README.md)'s controller, and every markdown surface in the UIs renders through the one renderer.

### What changed and why

- **Host hooks on `AgentConversation`** ([`agent-conversation.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/controller/agent-conversation.ts), [README](../../ui/packages/conversation/README.md#host-hooks)). The controller can keep its state in a host's store (`store`), follow a host's conversation generation (`generation`), let a replaced conversation's stream drain for a grace period before aborting it (`detachedGraceMs`), take undo/redo from `done` instead of a read (`refreshAfterStream: false`), show files as chips (`attachmentDisplay`, `send(text, secret, { attachments })`), read outputs a host's way (`outputTexts`), and hand events, turn boundaries, the finished snapshot, a failed turn's reason and every refusal or failure to the host (`onStreamEvent`, `onTurnStarting`, `onTurnEnd`, `onTurnSnapshot`, `turnFailure`, `presentFailure`). `send` resolves `{ outcome }`. Without the hooks it behaves as before; the widget's suites are unchanged. New rule for every surface: a secret turn never forwards an attachment.
- **`Message`** renders a sent message's attachments (image previews, file chips with size, "too large to send directly") and a reply's guardrail notes; **`ThinkingIndicator`** has a guardrail state (`guardrail`, `guardrailActive(messages, processing)`: "Checking the reply…"). The widget now shows both the notes and the state ([`chat/AGENTS.md`](../../ui/chat/AGENTS.md)). Eight `conversation.*` strings in the eleven locales (the guardrail lines are the Manager's existing translations). **`MarkdownContent`** gains `escapeRawHtml` (raw HTML shown as the text it is, for third-party text whose angle brackets are prose), and its image links carry `data-testid="markdown-image-link"`.
- **`ChatPanel` on the controller** ([`use-chat.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/hooks/use-chat.ts)). The Manager's send — streamed or not, the `done` reconcile, refusals, the detached-stream grace — is `chatConversation.send`; about 400 lines of its own turn loop (`handleSSEEvent`, `classifyRejectedSend`, `RejectedSendError`, the optimistic-message refs) are gone. The Zustand `useChatStore` stays the store every component reads: the controller writes it through `chatConversationStore`, and the store's own actions (agent picker, history, reset) advance the generation the controller follows. The Manager's copy is the hooks': a 409 is a pause only when a read confirms `AWAITING_HUMAN`, otherwise a toast; an error during a turn goes into the reply, flagged for "Retry Last Step"; a failed request keeps the message and adds an error bubble; the debug drawer gets every event (`recordDebugEvent`). The load guard, start, load/resume (with its masking of secret turns), following an executing turn, undo/redo/rerun and end stay Manager code. Every `use-chat*` and `use-send*` suite and every `ChatPanel` suite passes unmodified. That covers Agent Studio and Workforce chat, which render `ChatPanel`.
- **Visible in the Manager chat:** designer `image` output items render as images (live and from history; [`chat-message.tsx`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/components/chat/chat-message.tsx)); `button` labels and `applicationLink` links in a reply are shown (@eddi/core's reader; the flat `output:text:*` keys are still read); a turn dropped into a paused or busy conversation is withdrawn with a notice instead of staying as sent; the thinking line stays on between pipeline tasks until the first token (it used to blink off at each `task_complete`); and a guardrail at work on the reply says "Checking the reply…" ([`guardrail-activity.tsx`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/components/chat/guardrail-activity.tsx)).
- **The test drawer** ([`chat-drawer.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/components/chat/chat-drawer.tsx)) sends through `useSendMessage` and reads `useChatStore`, so it runs on the controller with `ChatPanel`; it gains the guardrail activity line, and designer images through `ChatMessage`. Its own composer, quick replies and masked field are unchanged (`chat-drawer*.test.tsx` unmodified).
- **One markdown renderer, enforced (§2.5).** The thirteen Manager files that called `ReactMarkdown` themselves — the group transcript, response, structured-turn, decision and overview cards and the artifacts panel, the Workforce board, advisor card, context card, conversation viewer and member thread, the Operator chat, and the update check's release notes — render through `ConversationMarkdown` (`MarkdownContent`). Each keeps raw HTML escaped as it was (`escapeRawHtml`); what they gain is KaTeX, highlighted code with a copy button and scrolling tables. [`one-renderer.test.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/markdown/one-renderer.test.ts) fails on any import of `react-markdown`, `remark-gfm`, `rehype-raw`, `rehype-sanitize` or `rehype-katex` outside the renderer's directory, in either app or any package, tests included — no exceptions. `markdownImageAsLink` is gone from [`markdown-safe.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/markdown-safe.tsx) (the renderer has the same rule); `sanitizeAgentHtml` stays for the group card's opt-in HTML view. The update card's test now finds the image link by the renderer's test id.
- **The Workforce member thread on the controller** ([`workforce-thread.tsx`](../../ui/workforce/src/pages/workforce/workforce-thread.tsx)). Its own stream loop (about 250 lines: the placeholder, settle, drop-turn and the event switch) is replaced by an `AgentConversation` per advisor, with an internal store read through `useAgentConversation`. The thread keeps its own presentation through the hooks: the error banner with Retry (`presentFailure` — a 409 withdraws the question, an error before any token keeps it for Retry to withdraw, a partial reply keeps it), the pause notice after a paused `done` (`onTurnSnapshot`), and "the connection ended" when a stream stops without `done` or `error`. A turn of files only still names the first file as its input. Visible: designer images render; Stop also asks the server to stop the turn (`POST /agents/{id}/cancel`, as the chat does) instead of only dropping the stream; a "New conversation" that fails to start keeps the old thread on screen, as before. Every thread suite passes unmodified; one new case covers designer images.
- **Feature matrix** ([`feature-matrix.spec.ts`](../../ui/manager/e2e/feature-matrix.spec.ts)). Now assertions: `output-images` on the chat panel (an MSW reply with an `image` item, `IMAGE_OUTPUT_TRIGGER`), and `katex`, `highlight` and `output-images` on the Workforce member thread (the same triggers, sent from the thread; its textarea gained `data-testid="thread-input"`). The operator's `katex` and `highlight` move from `no` to `unit`: it renders through the shared renderer, but the MSW tier has no activated operator.
- **Platform Operator.** No endpoint, field, default or failure mode changed, so neither its prompt nor its allow-list changed and `operator-revision.json` was not bumped; its chat only changed renderer. No Stryker-scoped file changed (`lib/operator/**`, `lib/api/updates.ts`, `lib/hitl-tool-approvals.ts`).

### Review fixes (2026-11-15)

- **A second pause after one resolved elsewhere is a pause again.** The controller judges a zero-token `done` reporting `AWAITING_HUMAN` against the state it held before the send (`isSkippedTurn`). The widget re-reads after a pause (`refreshAfterPause`), but the Manager's hosts only cleared their banner, so the controller kept `AWAITING_HUMAN` and the next turn that paused again was taken for one the server dropped: its message was withdrawn as "not sent", the Manager never raised the pause banner and the Workforce thread never its notice, and a re-send got a 409. New controller method `pauseResolved(state = "READY")` (clears the approval status, moves the state off `AWAITING_HUMAN`); the Manager calls it through `clearChatPause` from the chat panel's `PauseNotice`, the approvals inbox's resume and cancel ([`use-hitl.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/hooks/use-hitl.ts); cancel passes `EXECUTION_INTERRUPTED`), and the member thread calls it from its notice. The controller's undo/redo (`applyHistorySnapshot`) and `use-chat.ts`'s snapshot install, undo/redo and rerun now write the snapshot's `conversationState` too (`adoptConversationState`), and rerun re-establishes the pause banner like undo/redo; the thread's resume read sets it as well. Tests: [`agent-conversation.test.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/controller/agent-conversation.test.ts) ("a pause resolved elsewhere"), [`chat-panel-pause-resolved.test.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/components/chat/__tests__/chat-panel-pause-resolved.test.tsx), [`workforce-thread-pause-resolved.test.tsx`](../../ui/workforce/src/pages/workforce/__tests__/workforce-thread-pause-resolved.test.tsx).
- **Controller notices are system lines.** A `kind: "notice"` message (the controller's "…was not sent", "Undo is not possible right now") rendered as an agent markdown bubble with a copy button. `ChatMessage` and the member thread render it through the new `ChatNotice` ([`chat-message.tsx`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/components/chat/chat-message.tsx)): a centred, plain-text `role="note"` line, no avatar, no copy, no rating.
- **Secrets do not outlive the conversation.** `reset()` now drops the raw texts of secret turns, and `forgetSecrets()` does it alone; the Manager's app-wide `chatConversation` kept every secret typed in a session across agent switches and logout. The chat store's `setSelectedAgent`, `clearMessages` and `reset` call it ([`use-chat-forget-secrets.test.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/hooks/__tests__/use-chat-forget-secrets.test.ts)).

### Merged with #1280's final fixes (2026-11-16)

- **`main` merged after #1280 was squash-merged.** Both sides' controller behaviour is kept: #1280's `turnsSent` guard reaches the extracted `readStream` (a late re-read no longer writes undo/redo over a newer turn's), `resume()` aborts the running turn and takes a generation first (read through `currentGeneration`, so a host generation still governs), the local transport's stale-turn checks return the turn result, and the restored-step state comment covers both the ERROR and the resolved-pause case.
- **The live tool is a `ToolActivity` in the Manager too.** `use-chat.ts`'s `activeTool` and `recordDebugEvent` follow `@eddi/core`'s `ToolActivity`: `tool_call` goes through `parseToolCallEvent` / `toolCallActivity` (the designer's `toolDisplay` label), and `tool_result` settles the newest call. `ThinkingIndicator` keeps #1283's guardrail state with #1280's tool phrasing.
- **Test ids for the CodeRabbit nitpicks.** `message-no-response` ([`chat-message.tsx`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/components/chat/chat-message.tsx)), `thread-no-response` and `thread-send` ([`workforce-thread.tsx`](../../ui/workforce/src/pages/workforce/workforce-thread.tsx)); the image and pause-resolution tests use them instead of copy.

### Not done

- **The member thread renders no quick replies, requested field or secret input yet.** The controller tracks them for it; rendering them (the Manager's quick-reply buttons and `SecretInputField`) and an MSW reply that offers them are what flip those cells. Its history cell stays `no` (a thread is one pinned conversation).
- The thread's resume and welcome read `conversationSteps` with its own parser, not the controller's `applySnapshot`.
- The matrix has no test-drawer column: the MSW tier cannot open the drawer without the save-and-deploy flow. The drawer shares the chat panel's controller and bubble.
- Start, load/resume, undo, redo and rerun in `use-chat.ts` are not the controller's yet: they install snapshots with the Manager's secret masking and attachment-preview carry-over, which the controller's `stepsToMessages` does not do.

### Next

Quick replies and the requested field in the member thread, the controller's snapshot install for `use-chat.ts`'s load/undo/redo; then phase 4.

## feat(rag): retrieval benchmark — golden queries, recall@k, MRR, baseline diff (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/rag-retrieval-eval`)

### Why

There was no way to tell whether a RAG change — chunking, embedding model, `minScore` — made
retrieval better or worse. `POST /ragstore/rags/{id}/search` shows one query at a time, and
comparing two configurations meant eyeballing result lists.

### What changed

- **`POST /ragstore/rags/{id}/evaluate?version=N`** (`IRestRagIngestion`, `RestRagIngestion`):
  takes golden queries `[{id?, query, expected[], k?}]`, runs each through the **same**
  `KnowledgeBaseInspector.search` call `/search` uses (no second retriever), and returns per query
  `firstHitRank`, `hit` (hit@k), `reciprocalRank`, `recall` (recall@k), `found`, `missing` and the
  ranked identifiers retrieved, plus `summary` (`hitRate`, `recall`, `mrr`). With a `baseline` (an
  earlier response's `queries`) it adds a `comparison`: per-query and aggregate deltas, regressions
  flagged. EDIT, like `/search`.
- **`RetrievalMetrics`** (`modules/rag/evaluation`): the metric math as a pure class, unit-tested on
  its own.
- **`eddi.rag.evaluation.max-queries`** (default `100`): queries per request; more is a 400 naming
  the setting. Every validation (empty query, no expected documents, `k` outside 1–50, `minScore`
  outside 0–1, duplicate query keys) runs before the first embedding call.
- Docs: [rag.md → Evaluating retrieval](../rag.md#evaluating-retrieval), the endpoint table, and
  [configuration-reference.md](../configuration-reference.md). OpenAPI snapshot for the Manager's
  contract test gains the operation.

### Design decisions

- **Hit = exact, case-sensitive equality** of an expected identifier with the value of one of the
  `matchKeys` in a chunk's metadata. Default keys `source` (a direct ingest's `documentName`),
  `documentId` and `url` (an ingestion source's document), so both ingestion paths work without
  configuration; `matchKeys` overrides them (e.g. `title` for an upload's file name).
- **recall@k is per-document** (distinct expected documents found / expected, averaged), and hit
  rate is returned beside it — they differ whenever a query expects more than one document.
  Chunks of one document count once.
- **Reciprocal rank is cut at k** (0 without a hit in the top k), so `mrr` is MRR@k.
- **A query with nothing expected is refused** (400) at the endpoint; the math class treats it as
  unscorable (null recall, left out of every average) rather than as a perfect or failed answer.
- **Baseline pairing by `id`, else query text**; duplicate keys on either side are a 400. Aggregate
  deltas are over the queries both runs contain; the rest are listed (`onlyInCurrent`,
  `onlyInBaseline`). A query **regressed** if its reciprocal rank *or* recall fell; `kChanged`
  marks a different cut-off.
- **A knowledge base `maxResults` above 50 is clamped** to the search ceiling for the default k,
  while an explicit `k` above 50 is refused — the benchmark never reports a k it did not look at.
- **One failure fails the run** (502 with `queryIndex`), no partial results: a benchmark missing
  queries compares badly with its baseline.

### Not done / next

- No MCP tool (there is no RAG tool family yet; adding one means a new tool class, roles and the
  tool-count docs) and no Manager UI. The Platform Operator is not given the endpoint, matching
  `/search`, which it also does not hold.

## ♻️ refactor(ingestion): provider-neutral source connector contract (F1) (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`refactor/ingestion-source-connector-contract`)

### What changed and why

This is the foundation of the ingestion source types plan (`planning/rag-ingestion-source-types-plan.md` §3.1, §3.2, §3.5).
`IngestionPipeline` was hard-wired to the web crawler and the uploaded-file store. It now dispatches
every run through a `SourceConnector` chosen by `IngestionSource.type`, so that provider-specific Java
lives only in connectors. **There is no behaviour change**: the existing ingestion tests pass without
modification.

- **The contract** (new package `modules/ingestion/connector`):
  - `SourceConnector`: `discover(DiscoveryContext) → DiscoveryResult` lists from a cursor and streams
    `WorkItem`s to `context.accept`. `fetch(WorkItem, SourceContext) → SourceDocument` reads one
    document. `listPartitions` and `verifyAccess` default to one implicit, always-accessible partition.
  - `WorkItem` carries `action` `UPSERT`/`UNCHANGED`/`DELETE`, a version token, hints, and an
    optional prefetched document.
  - `SourceDocument` has `Content` `HtmlPage`/`FileBytes`/`Text`, a version token, HTTP validators
    and metadata.
  - `SourceMetadataKeys` defines the standard vocabulary and reserves `acl.principals`, which is
    schema only.
  - `Coverage` is `FULL_LISTING`/`FULL_TRAVERSAL`/`INCREMENTAL`/`PARTIAL`. `StopReason` uses the same
    names as `WebCrawler.StopReason`.
  - `SourceError` is a sealed type: `Transient`, `Throttled`, `Auth`, `NotFound`, `Permanent`,
    `Rejected` and `CursorInvalid`, plus `isDefinitive()`. The checked `SourceException` carries it.
    `Permanent` is an answer about the document (it can never be read as it is). `Rejected` is a
    request the provider refused as made (malformed, an unknown field mask). It says nothing about the
    document and is not definitive, so a connector defect or an API change cannot tombstone a corpus.
  - `KnownVersion`, `Partition` and `SourceContext`.
  - `SourceConnectors` is the CDI registry by type, and fails startup when two beans claim one type.
- **Adapters:** `crawl/WebSourceConnector` wraps `WebCrawler` unchanged. Pages arrive prefetched, a
  304 becomes `UNCHANGED`, and crawl errors are classified so that `isDefinitive()` is exactly the
  crawler's `!contentUnknown`. `files/UploadSourceConnector` lists without reading, uses the hash of
  the bytes as the version token, and maps a deleted-meanwhile file to `NotFound` and a store failure
  to `Transient`. The `toCrawlRequest` and upload listing loop moved into the adapters verbatim.
- **Pipeline:** `Collector` no longer implements `CrawlSink`. It processes work items generically.
  Text extraction stays in the framework, selected by content kind, which keeps the
  page-skipped-when-blank and file-failed-when-blank semantics. The old 8-argument constructor
  remains and builds the two connectors. CDI uses a new constructor that takes `SourceConnectors`.
- **Traversal evidence is counted, not inferred.** The blank-site guard (`learnedSomething`) used to
  treat `failed - unreachable` as definitive answers. A source-level failure (no document id) counts
  as failed but was never unreachable, so a `FULL_TRAVERSAL` run that learned nothing about any
  document passed for one that saw the corpus gone, and its absence-based deletion ran. `Collector`
  now counts `definitive` where it is learned: a document-level `NotFound`/`Permanent`, or a file
  with no text. Embed and budget failures no longer stand in for evidence either. That is the safe
  direction, since the only effect is that reconciliation runs less often. The now write-only
  `unreachable` counter is gone.
- **Config models only, not wired:** `SourceFilters` (`formats`, `excludeNamePatterns`,
  `includePaths`, `maxFileBytes`) and `SourcePartitions` (`LISTED` | `ALL_ACCESSIBLE`, `ids`).
- **Docs:** a "Source connectors" section in [`rag.md`](../rag.md#source-connectors-for-contributors).

### Not in this slice

The following are not in F1:

- **Cursors are not stored.** The pipeline passes none and ignores a returned one, so every run
  lists in full.
- **No multi-partition runs.** More than one partition is refused with a run failure.
- **No work queue or workers, and no superseding of item versions.** These are F2 and F3.
- **`DELETE` items** are applied directly: vectors are removed first, then the item is tombstoned.
  No built-in connector emits them yet.

**Files:** [`IngestionPipeline.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/IngestionPipeline.java),
[`connector/`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/connector/SourceConnector.java),
[`WebSourceConnector.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/crawl/WebSourceConnector.java),
[`UploadSourceConnector.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/files/UploadSourceConnector.java),
[`SourceFilters.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/model/SourceFilters.java),
[`SourcePartitions.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/rag/model/SourcePartitions.java)

## ♻️ refactor(email): opaque mailbox cursor and a processedAction vocabulary for the next mail transports (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`refactor/mail-transport-cursor`)

### What changed and why

`MailTransport.MailboxSession` was IMAP-shaped (`uidValidity()`, `uidNext()`, `messagesAfter(long lastUid, …)`), and
the poller and the state store kept UIDs. The Gmail API transport (cursor = `historyId`) and the Microsoft Graph
transport (cursor = a mail-folder `deltaLink`) that come next must each be one class with no poller change (email plan
§7.2, Phases 4 and 6). No behaviour change for IMAP: every existing email test passes with its assertions unchanged.

- **`MailboxCursor`** (new, `integrations/email/transport`) — opaque, `<transport>:<position>`; the poller never reads
  inside it. **`ImapCursor`** is IMAP's reading (`<UIDVALIDITY>:<last UID>`, exactly the two values stored before).
- **`MailboxSession`** is now `startCursor()` and `messagesAfter(MailboxCursor, int) → Batch`. A `Batch` carries
  entries (each message with the cursor that settles it), a `start` cursor (kept when nothing is settled), an `end`
  cursor (stored when everything is settled — for a history transport it may lie past the last message) and
  `cursorReset`, which replaces the poller's "UIDVALIDITY changed" branch: same WARN, same
  `eddi.email.poll.cursor_resets`, same rescan deduplicated by the ledger.
- **`UidFolderSession`** (new) — the IMAP cursor logic on three UID primitives; `ImapMailTransport.ImapSession`
  extends it. A cursor written by another transport is treated as a reset.
- **`MailboxMessage.uid()`** is a default (`-1`); only UID sessions use it.
- **State store** — `MailboxState` carries a `MailboxCursor` (`uidValidity()`/`lastUid()` remain as derived IMAP
  views); `saveCursor(…, MailboxCursor, …)`, with the old `(uidValidity, lastUid)` overload as a default method.
  MongoDB adds the field `mailboxCursor`, PostgreSQL the column `mailbox_cursor` (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`).
  `StoredCursor` maps both the same way.
- **`processedAction`** is parsed by the new `EmailProcessedAction`: `flag`, `move:<folder>`, `label:<name>`.
  `flag` and `move:` behave as before; `label:` is refused at save time for `imap` ("needs a transport with labels;
  IMAP has none") because IMAP marking only ever sets the fixed keyword `$EddiProcessed`. `EmailChannelSettings`
  carries the parsed action; `moveToFolder()`/`movesProcessed()` are derived from it.
- **Find by Message-ID** was already a transport method (`MailboxSession.findByMessageId`); no shared code searched
  IMAP, so nothing moved.

### Design decisions

- **Persisted state stays compatible in both directions.** An IMAP cursor is written to the new serialised field
  *and* to the legacy `uidValidity`/`lastUid` (`uid_validity`/`last_uid`); for IMAP the legacy fields win on read.
  State written before this change has no serialised cursor and loads as the same IMAP cursor, without migration.
  A node still on the old version (rolling upgrade, rollback) only writes the legacy fields, so letting them win
  means a stale serialised cursor can never override them. A non-IMAP cursor is stored only in the new field, with
  the legacy fields at 0 ("no IMAP cursor").
- `MailboxCursor.toString()` names the transport only — a Graph delta link does not belong in a log line.
- Test edits are mechanical: `EmailMailboxPollerTest`'s fake session is now `new UidFolderSession()` instead of
  `new MailboxSession()`, and the test-only `InMemoryEmailMailboxStateStore` stores a cursor.

Docs: [`email-integration.md`](../email-integration.md) (`processedAction` row, the ledger section on the cursor).

**Files:** [`MailboxCursor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/email/transport/MailboxCursor.java),
[`ImapCursor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/email/transport/ImapCursor.java),
[`UidFolderSession.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/email/transport/UidFolderSession.java),
[`StoredCursor.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/email/ledger/StoredCursor.java),
[`EmailProcessedAction.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/channels/model/EmailProcessedAction.java),
`MailTransport.java`, `ImapMailTransport.java`, `EmailMailboxPoller.java`, `IEmailMailboxStateStore.java`,
`MongoEmailMailboxStateStore.java`, `PostgresEmailMailboxStateStore.java`, `EmailChannelSettings.java`.
Tests: `MailboxCursorTest`, `StoredCursorTest`, `EmailProcessedActionTest`, `EmailMailboxPollerCursorTest`.

### Next

The Gmail API transport (Phase 6) and the Graph transport (Phase 4) as one `MailTransport` each, plus a transport
registry so the poller picks the transport by `platformConfig.transport` instead of injecting `ImapMailTransport`.

## 📝 docs(release): close the 6.7.0 upgrade-guide and release-notes gaps (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`docs/release-6.7.0-docs-gaps`)

### What changed and why

"Changes in 6.7" in [`upgrading-from-6.5.md`](../upgrading-from-6.5.md) and the 6.7.0 row of
[`release-notes.md`](../release-notes.md) were written before the team learning loop, team lessons, the agent
operations tools, the tenant cost reservation, per-run `toolQuotas`, the email channel, conversation ratings and alert
rules were all merged. The upgrade guide now lists each in "New opt-in features" with its switch and page, and
"Rolling upgrades" says which stored fields (`TaskItem.bet`, `lessonConfig`) an older pod drops on save, which new
collections and tables older pods ignore, and that the alerts maintenance schedule exists by default and should be switched off until every pod runs 6.7, and how to delete the already-created marked schedule (MongoDB and PostgreSQL) before rolling back, since disabling alerts does not remove it and REST refuses to. The release-notes
row gets the release date 2026-11-05 (the `6.7.0` tag date) and the missing headline items. [`email-integration.md`](../email-integration.md) said
"Since v6.6.0"; the channel merged after the 6.6.0 tag, so it now says v6.7.0.

### Design decisions

- The date is the plain release date: the `6.7.0` tag exists and the release was published on 2026-11-05, and the table defines dates
  as tag dates.

## 🧪 test(rag): document replacement verified on a real Chroma server (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`test/chroma-replacement-it`)

### What changed and why

[`rag.md`](../rag.md#store-support-for-replacement) said replacing a document's chunks — the
`removeAll(Filter)` on `documentId` + `sourceKey` that a re-ingest, a tombstone and a deleted upload
all end in — was verified only on `in-memory` and `pgvector`, with Chroma merely "expected". Nothing
checked it, and a driver that cannot translate the compound filter leaves stale chunks retrievable
for good.

New [`ChromaReplacementIT`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/ChromaReplacementIT.java)
starts `chromadb/chroma:1.1.0` (the image `docker-compose.chroma.yml` ships) and drives the real
`IngestionPipeline` through the real `EmbeddingStoreFactory`, so the store is the one `buildChroma`
makes, on its default `apiVersion` `V2` with its bounded JDK client. Only the embedding model is an
offline bag-of-words stand-in. Three cases, each asserting on what is actually in the collection:

- a changed page is re-ingested: the new text is there, the old text is gone, `replaceUnsupported` is
  false — this exercises the three-clause filter including `runId $ne`;
- a page removed from the site is tombstoned: its chunks are deleted, the surviving page's are not;
- a deleted upload is forgotten: `ForgetOutcome.REMOVED`, and the source's other document survives.

All pass locally against the container, so the docs now list Chroma as verified. `mongodb-atlas`,
`elasticsearch` and `qdrant` remain "expected".

### Notes

- Run locally from a sandboxed Windows shell, the JDK `HttpClient` could not open its selector
  ("Unable to establish loopback connection" — the NIO pipe's Unix-domain socket under a long temp
  path). `-DargLine=-Djdk.net.unixdomain.tmpdir=<short dir>` works around it; CI is unaffected.

**Files:** [`ChromaReplacementIT.java`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/ingestion/ChromaReplacementIT.java),
[`rag.md`](../rag.md)

## 💬 feat(ui): `@eddi/conversation` — the agent kind (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/ui-eddi-conversation`, stacked on `feat/ui-eddi-ui`)

Phase 3b of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §1.3, §2.5, §3.1, §3.2, §7 phase 3, D9): the third shared package, [`@eddi/conversation`](../../ui/packages/conversation/README.md), with the `AgentConversation` controller.

### What changed and why

- **`AgentConversation`** ([`agent-conversation.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/controller/agent-conversation.ts)) — the 1:1 turn state machine, framework-free (`subscribe` / `getSnapshot`, read through `useSyncExternalStore`), on `@eddi/core`: streaming, a 409 that restores the draft, dropped vs paused turns, `inputField` on `done`, undo/redo, rerun, stop, cancel, the refresh after a pause, and `resume(conversationId)`, which restores the last output's quick replies. Its suite is the union of the 1:1 loops' turn rules, without React.
- **The shared `conversation.*` strings** in the eleven locales; the Manager's `i18n:check` reads a package's own locale files with the app's.
- **The shared components** — `Message`, `MessageList`, `Composer`, `SecretInput`, `QuickReplies`, `ThinkingIndicator` / `TypingIndicator`, `PausedCard`, `RatingCard`, `StepRating`, `ScrollToBottom` — moved from the widget with their history, and **the one markdown renderer** (`MarkdownContent`, §2.5): images in model text as links, raw HTML only through rehype-sanitize (parser lazy, still rendered for every agent until D9's setting), KaTeX (`trust: false`) and highlight.js lazy. GFM is now parsed without its serialiser ([`remark-gfm-parse.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/markdown/remark-gfm-parse.ts)): remark-gfm also registered mdast-util-to-markdown and markdown-table, which a renderer never calls — about 4 KB of gzip off every first load that renders markdown; a test renders a corpus both ways and compares.
- **The widget (`ui/chat`) on the controller.** `ChatProvider` owns one `AgentConversation` and keeps the widget's own state beside it (config, agent name, review notice, rating profile); `useChatState()` / `useChatDispatch()` are unchanged for components. `ChatWidget` keeps the embedding — route, `?token=`/postMessage auth, URL colours, `hide*` flags, the profile gate, ratings, HITL polling — and hands the route to the controller as a transport (conversation, managed, or the demo's `createDemoTransport`). Deleted: `turn-notices.ts`, `api/hitl-api.ts`, `ime.ts` (now in the package). The component tests stay in the widget and import from `@eddi/conversation`; `chat-store.test.tsx` and every `ChatWidget.*` suite pass unmodified. The 108 moved strings left `src/i18n/locales` for the package's (`conversation.` prefix, same translations). Visible changes: none intended; a guardrail notice is now recorded on the reply it explains (the widget does not render it yet). First load 167.7 KB gz (was 169.3; budget 170).
- **The Manager's chat bubble on the one renderer.** `chat-message.tsx` — the Chat page, Agent Studio, the test drawer and Workforce chat — renders replies through `MarkdownContent` ([`conversation-markdown.tsx`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/i18next/conversation-markdown.tsx), with the Manager's `t` for the shared strings, which `i18n/config.ts` now merges in). Visible: KaTeX for `$$…$$`, highlighted fenced code with a copy button, links in a new tab, and raw HTML rendered **sanitised** instead of shown as escaped text (D9: every agent until the per-agent setting). `@eddi/conversation/markdown.css` styles the copy button, tables and highlight tokens from the shared tokens. The feature matrix's KaTeX and highlight cells for `chat-panel` are now assertions (an MSW reply with math and code, `RICH_MARKDOWN_TRIGGER`).
- **Quick replies on resume (Manager).** A conversation reopened from history, or resumed on opening an agent, now offers the last reply's quick replies again (`installSnapshot`, `use-chat-load-pause.test.tsx`) — the same rule as `AgentConversation.resume`, kept in `use-chat.ts` until `ChatPanel` moves onto the controller. Mutation-checked: without the line, the new test fails.
- **Widget RTL e2e.** [`chat-widget-rtl.fullstack.spec.ts`](../../ui/manager/e2e/fullstack/chat-widget-rtl.fullstack.spec.ts) opens `/chat/demo/showcase?lang=ar` against the real server (the only tier that serves `/chat`) and checks `dir="rtl"` and the mirrored composer and message layout.
- **Gates.** CI's `UI Chat` job runs the package's type check, lint and tests; `sharedPackage({ routerFree: true })` and `boundaries.test.ts` keep it router-free, off `window.__EDDI_*__`, and unimported by `@eddi/core` and `@eddi/ui`; `AutoApproveCopilotPolicyTest` covers its config files.
- **Archived changelog links.** Nine links in `docs/changelog/2026-09.md`, `2026-10.md` and the phase-0 fragment named widget components this change moves; they now point at the package files (`DocumentationLinksTest`). Only the link targets changed.
- **Platform Operator.** No endpoint, field, default or failure mode changed, and the operator's own chat is untouched (phase 6), so neither its prompt nor its allow-list changed and `operator-revision.json` was not bumped. No Stryker-scoped file changed.

### Not done

- **`ChatPanel` (`use-chat.ts`), the test drawer, Workforce chat and the Workforce member thread are not on `AgentConversation` yet.** The Manager loop differs from the widget's in ways each of its 30 suites pins (error bubbles and toasts where the widget writes notices, the conversation epoch and detached-stream grace, the load guard, following an executing turn, debug events, agent selection); moving it is a step of its own. They share the renderer and the resume rule only. The Operator stays on its own code (phase 6).
- `Message` does not render a sent message's attachments or a reply's guardrail notes yet (the widget never had them; the Manager's bubble still does), and `ActivityIndicator` has no guardrail state. `MessageList` is not windowed (sticky scroll only).
- The §2.5 test that fails on a `ReactMarkdown` import bypassing the shared renderer is not written: thirteen Manager files (groups, Workforce, the Operator, update check) still use their own.
- Feature matrix: `output-images` stays unsupported on the chat panel (the Manager's bubble renders text parts only); the widget's `history` and `inline-hitl` cells are unchanged by design.

### Next

`ChatPanel` and the test drawer onto `AgentConversation` and the shared components (then Workforce chat and the member thread), with the Manager's error copy as the controller's notices; then phase 4.
