## ✨ feat(memory): contested and superseded user memories instead of log-only contradictions (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/user-memory-contested`)

### What changed and why

Dream detected user-memory contradictions (one key, different values — possible because `self`/`group` entries are keyed
per agent and the `global` row is shared) but only counted and logged them, and `contradictionResolution` was documented
as "Reserved". It is now implemented as a small claim ledger: no strategy deletes an entry or silently picks a winner.
See [User Memory → Contradiction resolution](../user-memory.md#contradiction-resolution).

- **Model** — [`UserMemoryEntry`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/properties/model/UserMemoryEntry.java) gains
  `assessment` (`ACCEPTED` / `CONTESTED` / `SUPERSEDED`; `null` and unknown read as `ACCEPTED`) and `supersededBy` (kept
  only on a superseded entry). The 13-argument constructor stays and builds an `ACCEPTED` entry, so every existing call
  site and old JSON body is unchanged. `conflicted` — already on the record and shown by the Manager, but never set —
  now mirrors `CONTESTED`.
- **Stores** — MongoDB and PostgreSQL read and write both fields on every path (upsert, `insertIfAbsent`,
  `upsertIfOwnedBy`, `replaceIfUnchanged`, the mappers); the flat `mergeProperties` resets to `ACCEPTED`. PostgreSQL adds
  the two columns with `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` (default `ACCEPTED` for existing rows). Every value
  write — upsert, `upsertReserved`, `insertIfAbsent`, `upsertIfOwnedBy`, `replaceIfUnchanged`, on both stores — stores
  the entry as `UserMemoryEntry.asStatement()` (`ACCEPTED`, no `supersededBy`, `conflicted=false`) whatever it carries,
  so a REST client that PUTs back an edited contested entry cannot keep the old verdict (review finding on PR #1295);
  `UserMemoryWriteRules.withDefaults` applies the same rule at the REST boundary. New
  `IUserMemoryStore.setAssessmentIfUnchanged` — a compare-and-set on `updatedAt` that deliberately does **not** bump
  `updatedAt`, one conditional statement on both backends, and now the only write that can record a verdict (its
  interface default refuses instead of going through `upsert`).
- **Dream** — the decision is a pure function,
  [`UserMemoryContradictions`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/internal/UserMemoryContradictions.java);
  `DreamService.resolveContradictions` (replacing `detectContradictions`) logs exactly as before and writes the changes.
  `keep_newest` (default) changes nothing; `mark_contested` flags both sides; `supersede_older` retires the older side
  only where the newer one reaches all of its readers, otherwise contests. Writes stay inside the agent-scoped set
  (`crossAgentMaintenance`, `_gdpr_` keys excluded) like pruning; a lost CAS counts in `conflictsSkipped`. Stale
  verdicts are lifted in every mode. Unknown values WARN and act as `keep_newest`. Contested and superseded entries are
  held back from summarization (`acceptedOnly`).
- **Loading** — `Conversation.mostSpecificPerKey` drops a superseded entry when its key has a non-superseded visible
  entry; contested entries load as usual plus a conversation-scoped `contestedMemories` map (`key → true`). Persisting a
  rewritten key drops it from the marker, and so does a `rememberFact` write of the key (the tool writes the store
  directly, so end-of-turn persistence never saw it and the marker outlived the user's confirmation; lifting lives in
  `ContestedMemoryMarker`, review finding on PR #1295). `UserMemoryTool` marks contested/superseded lines in recall and search, and a
  re-statement of a contested value is a write (it confirms the fact). `forgetFact` deletes the whole supersession chain
  of the forgotten entry (every same-key entry linked through `supersededBy`, in either direction), limited to entries
  this agent may see and delete; before, forgetting the newest version left the `SUPERSEDED` one behind, and the loader
  served its older value again as the reader's only entry for the key (review finding on PR #1295). The linked versions
  are deleted before the forgotten entry, farthest first, so a failure part way leaves the forgotten entry and a chain
  still connected to it for a retry; deleting the entry first left older versions whose replacement was gone, served
  again and unreachable by a retry's chain walk (review finding on PR #1295).
- **Metrics** — `eddi.dream.contradictions.contested`, `.superseded`, `.cleared`, charted with a legend on the Full Metrics dashboard
  (Kubernetes copy re-synced) and listed in [metrics.md](../metrics.md). GDPR export carries the new fields
  ([gdpr-compliance.md](../gdpr-compliance.md)).

### Design decisions

- Default stays log-only; the name `keep_newest` is historical — Dream keeps no value, the loader's precedence decides.
- An assessment is not a write of the fact, so it leaves `updatedAt` alone (staleness pruning and `most_recent` recall
  unaffected).
- The competing value is never exposed to the agent — it may be another agent's `self` memory.
- Known limit: a property setter re-setting a contested property to its current value writes nothing, so it does not
  resolve the contest (use `rememberFact`, REST or MCP).

### Next

Manager: show `assessment`/`supersededBy` on the memory page (it already highlights `conflicted`).

---

## Workforce as its own app — frontend unification phase 5 (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/workforce-app` (stacked on `feat/workforce-sidebar-home`, PR #1284)

Phase 5 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md`, §3.1, §4.1, §4.2, §7): Workforce leaves the Manager's bundle and becomes `ui/workforce`, an app of its own beside `ui/manager` and `ui/chat`, with its own `workforce.html`, `main.tsx`, router, i18n, tests and E2E suite. It loads only what it uses.

**What changed**

- **`ui/workforce`** — `src/app.tsx` holds the `/workforce/**` routes the Manager's `app.tsx` used to; `src/main.tsx` boots through `startApp`. The Workforce components, pages, hooks and libs moved here with `git mv` (64 modules, 55 test files), so `git log --follow` traces them. Dev server on port 3002, MSW `ui` E2E tier (`playwright.config.ts`), its own Vitest config, ESLint, tsconfigs, `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`.
- **`@eddi/app-kit` (`ui/packages/app-kit`)** — a fourth shared package: the layer the two signed-in apps share. Workforce rendered some two hundred Manager modules (the API client and hooks, the Keycloak session, the UI primitives, the group, chat and editor components, the mock API); they moved here at the paths they had, imported as `@eddi/app-kit/<path>`. Also new in it: `startApp` and `AppProviders` (both apps boot and wrap the same way), `setupAppI18n` (each app's strings deep-merged with the package's and `@eddi/conversation`'s), `styles/app.css` (the base stylesheet, with `@source`), and `AppLink` / `AppNavigate` / `useAppNavigate` over `lib/app-paths.ts`.
- **Moving between the apps is a page load.** A `<Link>` from one router to the other app's path matches nothing, so every cross-app link (the welcome chooser's Workforce card and its `eddi-user` redirect, the mode switcher, Workforce's links into the Manager, the Operator link, the pause notice's review link) goes through `AppLink` / `AppNavigate` / `useAppNavigate`, which keep the router inside the app. Theme, language, pins and the Keycloak session carry over through same-origin storage (keys unchanged).
- **The Manager loses the Workforce routes**, the `workforce.html` build input, `workforce-pins` (now `@eddi/app-kit`, since the Manager's sign-out clears it) and its Workforce tests. Six mixed tests were split between the apps; the group rendering matrix keeps one shared fixture list (`@eddi/app-kit/test/group-message-matrix`).
- **The mock API is shared, not split.** Every MSW handler the Workforce screens call is also called by the Manager (the catalog included: `use-chat` reads it), so there was no Workforce-only set to drop; the handlers moved to `@eddi/app-kit/test/mocks`, and both apps' tests and dev servers run on them. The OpenAPI contract test stays in the Manager.
- **i18n** — the Manager's locale files were split by who uses each key: 1,133 keys to `@eddi/app-kit`, 613 to Workforce, 5,128 stay in the Manager, in all 11 locales. `check-i18n.mjs` moved to `ui/scripts/` and checks the app it runs from.
- **Guards** — `appBoundary` in `ui/eslint.config.base.js` (with the tests-must-fail rules, now shared as `signedInApp`) fails a Manager import of Workforce code and the reverse; `src/__tests__/app-boundary.test.ts` in each app checks resolved paths. `route-integrity` is a shared suite each app runs over its own table and the shared modules it renders, and fails a router link into the other app. `core/src/boundaries.test.ts` resolves relative paths (app-kit has `components/chat/` and `components/workforce/` of its own), keeps the three packages below app-kit from importing it, and checks that its `exports` reach every module.
- **The production bundles no longer carry the mock API.** `main.tsx` passes `startApp` the MSW loader as `import.meta.env.DEV ? () => import(…) : undefined`.
- **Build** — `pom.xml` builds `ui/workforce` (`workforce-build`), copies its `dist/` beside the Manager's (`workforce.html` at the root, chunks under `assets/`), cleans `ui/workforce/dist`, and `-DskipUi=true` skips it. The jar layout is unchanged: `RestWorkforceResource` still serves `workforce.html` for every `/workforce/**` path, so deep links and reloads (`?agentId=&conversationId=`) work as before, with no Java change. `ui/scripts/` now holds the shared build tooling (`vite-plugin-brand.mjs`, `vite-backend-proxy.mjs`, `install-playwright-chromium.mjs`); the dev proxy gained `/catalog`, which it never forwarded.
- **CI** — a `UI Workforce` job (app-kit type check and lint, lint, i18n, type check, tests, build, budget, MSW E2E) in the UI Gate; Build Image checks the packaged assets against both builds; the SBOM gains `bom-ui-workforce.json`; CodeQL scans `ui/workforce/src`; Dependabot's `/ui` entry already covered it.
- **Budgets** (`ui/bundle-budget.json`) — Workforce gets its own: 251.2 KB gz first load measured at the split (it loaded the Manager's whole 629.4 KB entry before), budget 280 KB. The Manager's first load fell from 629.4 to 604.1 KB gz with what left it (Workforce's strings above all), so its budget is lowered from 630 to 620 KB; the widget's is unchanged.

**Design decisions**

- *A fourth package rather than the plan's three.* The modules Workforce needs read the router, call the backend and read `window.__EDDI_*__`, which `@eddi/ui` and `@eddi/conversation` forbid; loosening those rules, or letting Workforce import the Manager, were the alternatives. `@eddi/app-kit` is app code shared by two apps; what phase 6 moves into `@eddi/conversation` (the team kind) leaves it.
- *Paths kept, not redesigned.* Modules kept their relative paths, so a Manager import changed from `@/x` to `@eddi/app-kit/x` and nothing else — reviewable, scriptable, and `git mv` keeps the history.
- *No index barrel.* The package's `exports` map directories, so an app's first load holds only what it reaches.
- *Rejected: `/workforce/index.html`.* The shells stay at the jar root as the backend expects (`BrandedShell`); a new layout would have needed Java and test changes for no user-visible gain.

**Follow-ups:** phase 6 (Operator and team kinds) drains the group components out of `@eddi/app-kit`; `use-current-screen-context.ts` still maps `/workforce` screens, which the Manager no longer renders.

## ✨ feat(workforce): sidebar of conversations, Home, the chat in the URL; the widget follows rawHtml (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/workforce-sidebar-home`, stacked on `refactor/manager-chat-on-conversation` with #1273 and #1282 merged in)

The frontend half of phase 4 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md`, T1.1, T1.2, T1.4 and T1.15; B1/B2/B4; decisions D7 and D9), for every Workforce role, end users first.

### What changed and why

- **Sidebar (T1.1, T1.2).** [`session-list.tsx`](../../ui/workforce/src/components/workforce/session-list.tsx) lists the signed-in person's chats and team sessions in the Workforce sidebar: Pinned, Today, Yesterday, Previous 7 days, Previous 30 days, then one group per month ([`workforce-sessions.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/lib/workforce-sessions.ts), local calendar days). A chat shows its B2 `title`, or the agent's name and the date before it has one; a team session shows its question. Chats are renamed inline (Enter saves, Esc cancels, one request even though the field blurs as it closes), pinned and unpinned through `PATCH /conversationstore/conversations/{id}`, and deleted through the owner `DELETE` after a confirmation; team sessions can only be deleted (the backend keeps no title or pin for them). Every write updates the TanStack cache first and puts it back when the server refuses ([`use-workforce-sessions.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/hooks/use-workforce-sessions.ts)). Reads are the owner-scoped `GET /conversationstore/conversations` and `GET /groups/{id}/conversations` for the teams the person can see (catalog or store by role) — nothing an `eddi-user` is refused. The chats are read with `?mine=true&excludeGroupMembers=true` (below), so an admin's sidebar holds only their own chats and the conversations members hold inside a team session (owned by the person who started it) are not listed as chats; `memberConversationIds` stays as a fallback for member conversations started before the descriptor recorded their group.
- **Listing filters (backend, review follow-up).** `GET /conversationstore/conversations` gains `mine` and `excludeGroupMembers` ([`IRestConversationStore`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/memory/rest/IRestConversationStore.java)). `mine=true` applies the owner filter for an admin too — pushed into the descriptor query, so it holds on MongoDB and PostgreSQL alike and an admin's own chats are no longer the few of the 100 newest of every user that happened to be theirs — and admits a row only if the caller IS its owner (`ConversationAccessGuard.ownsConversation`, no admin bypass, no reviewer exception, no ownerless row). It replaces the sidebar's client-side comparison with `user.username`, which never matched where the principal claim is not `preferred_username` and let other users' legacy (ownerless-descriptor) rows through. A member conversation now records `groupConversationId` on its `ConversationDescriptor`: `ConversationService.startConversation` reads it from the start context `MemberTurnExecutor` passes, and `ConversationSetup` writes it; `excludeGroupMembers=true` filters on its absence in the query and again per row. It is a listing hint, not an access attribute. An eight-argument default method keeps the in-process signature the MCP tools call. [`rest-api-reference.md`](../rest-api-reference.md#the-conversation-store) documents both parameters.
- **Review follow-ups (frontend).** The team sessions are read with `?mine=true` too (`RestGroupConversation.listGroupConversations` applies the owner query to an admin when asked). Deleting the chat or team session that is open returns to Home and clears the chat store, instead of leaving the deleted conversation on screen. Home's first message waits on the review/controls lookup of the agent of *this* render (`ChatPanel` no longer reads `selectedAgentId` from the live store there, which could pair a just-switched agent with the previous one's settled profile). Home's starters fall back to the agent's profile `appearance` when the catalog has none for it (an author's unlisted agents). The Shift+Enter test waits for sending to be possible first, so it can no longer pass vacuously.
- **Home (T1.4).** [`workforce-home.tsx`](../../ui/workforce/src/components/workforce/workforce-home.tsx): a composer first with an agent picker (the agent of the most recent chat, else the first listed), the picked agent's review notice and its `appearance.starters` as chips, then the five most recent conversations, then the catalog. Agent cards show the B4 display name, avatar (initials on the accent colour when there is none or it fails to load) and accent colour. Sending, or a starter, opens a new chat with the text as its first message; the text travels in **router state** ([`workforce-chat-start.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/workforce-chat-start.ts)), never in the URL, so a link still only preselects an agent (the chat's deep-link rule). Authors get the composer and the recent list above their dashboard.
- **Catalog appearance (backend).** `IRestCatalog.CatalogAgent` gains `appearance` and `conversationControls`, built with `AppearanceProfile.of` / `ControlsProfile.of` from the configuration `CatalogService` already reads for the deployed version — so a card needs no profile call. A six-argument constructor keeps the old shape (no appearance, default controls). [`CatalogServiceTest`](../../src/test/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/catalog/CatalogServiceTest.java) covers both; [`catalog.md`](../catalog.md) shows the new fields. No new endpoint, so `openapi-operations.json` is unchanged.
- **T1.15 — the conversation in the URL.** `ChatPanel urlSync` (Workforce only) keeps `?agentId=…&conversationId=…` in the address bar in both directions: the parameters are acted on once per pair, and the conversation on screen is written back once the URL's own conversation has loaded (read from the live store, so a half-switched render cannot overwrite it). A reload or a second tab opens the same conversation. The Manager's `/manage/chat` still clears the parameters once read. The widget's direct routes (`/chat/:environment/:agentId[/:userId]`, risk R10) are untouched; `location.test.tsx` pins them.
- **`conversationControls` (B4) in the shared chat.** The Manager's `ChatPanel` (Manager and Workforce) reads them from the profile (`useAgentReviewProfile`) and drops undo, redo, attachments (button, paste and drop), ratings and quick replies the agent switched off; `streaming: false` sends without streaming (`agentStreaming` in the chat store). The rule itself — every switch on unless `false`, `rawHtml` off unless `true` — is `parseConversationControls` in `@eddi/core`, shared with the widget.
- **The widget follows `rawHtml` (D9) — a behaviour change.** `Message` now renders raw HTML only when the host passes `enableRawHtml` (default **off**) and otherwise shows it as text (`escapeRawHtml`). `ChatWidget` passes the agent's `conversationControls.rawHtml` from the profile; until the profile answers, when it reports no controls, and for every agent that does not set `rawHtml: true`, `<b>bold</b>` in a reply is shown as that literal text. The parser stays a lazy chunk. The widget honours the other switches too (undo, redo, attachments via a new `Composer attachments` prop, quick replies, ratings, streaming); the `hide*` URL parameters can only hide more. [`chat-ui-embedding.md`](../chat-ui-embedding.md#upgrading-html-in-replies-is-off-by-default) says what to do when upgrading.
- **The Manager's chat history** shows a conversation's title instead of its id once it has one.
- **Platform Operator.** The two prompt bullets #1273 and #1282 each added are both kept; the revision goes from their 53 to **54**. The prompt also learns that catalog entries carry appearance and controls (avatars on other hosts need the Manager's `img-src`) and what the Workforce sidebar does. No allow-list change: renaming stays the owner's act.
- **i18n.** `Workforce.sessions.*` and `Workforce.home.*` in all eleven locales; the layout uses logical properties only.
- **Docs.** A new end-user guide, [`workforce-guide.md`](../workforce-guide.md) (in `SUMMARY.md`); `catalog.md`; `chat-ui-embedding.md` (upgrade section rewritten for the shipped behaviour); `ui/manager/AGENTS.md` (Workforce sidebar, Home, `urlSync`), `ui/chat/AGENTS.md` (4b), the `@eddi/conversation` README.
- **Tests.** Unit: date grouping, labels and the router-state hand-over; the sidebar (groups, rename, Escape, refused rename, pin/unpin, confirmed and refused delete, team sessions and hidden member chats); Home (composer first, default agent, starters, Enter vs Shift+Enter, recent, accent colour); `ChatPanel urlSync` (both ids kept, reload, a started conversation written back, nothing started from the URL alone, the handed-over first message, the Manager still clearing); the widget's controls and D9 (`ChatWidget.controls.test.tsx`, `MessageBubble.test.tsx`); `parseConversationControls`. MSW: titles and a pin on the conversation fixtures, a `PATCH` handler and a `DELETE` that removes from the listing (both reset after every test), the catalog fixtures' appearance and controls, a profile that answers them. Playwright: `workforce-sidebar.spec.ts`, `workforce-home.spec.ts`, `workforce-reload.spec.ts`, and `workforce-end-user.spec.ts` extended (Home and the sidebar read no store).

### Design decisions

- The first message goes through router state, not `?q=`: a crafted link must not make anyone say anything (the same reason deep links only preselect). A reload cannot resend it — the chat drops the state the moment it takes it, and the URL write that follows replaces the history entry.
- Team sessions are listed but cannot be renamed or pinned: B2 lives on conversation descriptors, and group conversations have no such fields. Pinning them would need a backend change, not a client-side store.
- The catalog carries appearance rather than each card fetching `/profile`: one request for the whole Home, and the same views the profile serves.
- Raw HTML off by default in `Message` itself, not only in the widget: any future host of the shared component starts safe.

### Verification (local, Windows)

- `ui/`: `npm ci`, `npm run lockfile:check` (lock untouched). `@eddi/core` typecheck, lint, 209 tests (+3); `@eddi/ui` 62; `@eddi/conversation` 109 (+1); Chat UI typecheck, lint, 550 tests (+6, 2 todo), build, budget **170.0 KB gz of 170** (from about 169.8: no headroom left); Manager lint, typecheck, `i18n:check` (6,990 keys), 9,449 of 9,453 tests (+33) — the three known environmental failures in `resource-detail-rag-upload-source.test.tsx`, and `group-detail-selection.test.tsx`, which fails only under load (a fixed 150 ms wait) and passes on its own — build and budget **629.4 KB gz of 630** (the session list is a lazy chunk; eager it was 632.3). `npx playwright test --list`: 410 tests in 45 files, the four Workforce specs among them (not run: the local browser does not match).
- Mutation check: `enableRawHtml={true}` forced in `ChatWidget` fails two of `ChatWidget.controls.test.tsx`'s cases; reverted.
- Backend, from PowerShell: `./mvnw compile`; `CatalogServiceTest`, `RestAgentProfilePresentationTest` and the guards `ImportStyleTest`, `BuildQualityGatesTest`, `ChangelogRotationTest`, `ChangelogFragmentTest`, `DocumentationLinksTest`, `DocumentationAccuracyTest`, `DocumentedRestPathsTest`, `SkillToolReferencesTest`, `AutoApproveCopilotPolicyTest`, `BrandDeliveryTest` — 127 tests, a report from each class.

### What's left

- Not run here: the Playwright specs (they type-check, lint and are listed; the local browser does not match), Stryker over `src/lib/operator/**` (only prompt text changed), CI.
- Team sessions are read per team (up to 20 teams, 10 sessions each): a per-user listing endpoint would replace that fan-out. Member chats no longer depend on it (`excludeGroupMembers`), except those started before `groupConversationId` was recorded.
- Neither the MCP `list_conversations` tool nor the Platform Operator knows `mine` / `excludeGroupMembers` yet (the operator's allow-list already holds the listing endpoint; its prompt does not mention the parameters). Search (T1.3, B3) is phase 7.
- The widget does not yet show `appearance` (display name, avatar, accent): `agentAppearanceLayer` in `ui/chat/src/api/brand.ts` is still the empty seam, and the budget (170 KB gz) has almost no room.

## ✨ feat(workforce): Tier 1 of the end-user chat — Stop that tells the truth, edit and regenerate, rich output, attachments, keyboard, installable, accessible (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/workforce-tier1-ux` (stacked on `feat/conversation-operator-team`)

The frontend half of phase 7 of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §2.5, §3, §4.2, §5 Tier 1): T1.5, T1.6 (part), T1.7, T1.8, T1.10–T1.14. Search (T1.3), the tool notice of T1.6 and tool cards (T1.9) wait for the backend's B3 and B6, built on another branch. The changes are in the shared controller and components, so the Manager's chat, Agent Studio, the Workforce chat and member thread, and the embeddable widget get them where they apply.

### What changed and why

- **Stop that says when it did not take (T1.5).** `POST /agents/{id}/cancel` stops a running turn only on the pod running it ([HITL](../hitl.md)); a `200` says the request was accepted, not that the turn stopped. [`AgentConversation`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/controller/agent-conversation.ts) now re-reads the conversation after Stop (`stopVerifyDelaysMs`, 1.5 s and 5 s): a turn that ran on gets a notice — it *finished anyway* (its whole reply replaces the part Stop left) or is *still running* — a `409` means it had already finished, and a cancel that could not reach the server says that. A `409` is read from any host client's error now, not only `@eddi/core`'s `ApiError` (the Manager's was never recognised).
- **Reconnect (T1.5).** A stream that drops after part of the reply arrived is followed (`reconnectDelaysMs`, about half a minute; new `reconnecting` state): the conversation is re-read until the turn settles, and its reply is applied as its `done` would have been. "Connection lost" only when it never settles. Opt-in; the widget, `ChatPanel` and the member thread pass the shared defaults.
- **`ChatPanel` (Manager, Studio, Workforce).** Stop in place of Send while a turn runs, **Esc** stops it (not while a dialog is open), a "Reconnecting…" line, client notices as system lines (`ChatMessage` `kind: "notice"`), no pulsing bubble while text streams, a stable scrollbar gutter, the composer refocused after a send, a new chat and Stop. Sticky scroll with "jump to latest" was already there (`useSmartAutoScroll`).
- **Edit the last message, regenerate the last reply (T1.6, T1.10).** *Edit* (or **↑** in an empty composer) undoes the last step and puts its text back in the composer; *Regenerate* reruns it. Only when the agent's `conversationControls` allow undo (and redo); never for a secret turn or one that carried files. **`renderTurnNotice`** on `ChatPanel` is the seam for "this turn ran tools whose effects stay" once B6 reports which tools ran; no tool detection here.
- **Rich output (T1.7)**, in the one renderer ([`Markdown.tsx`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/markdown/Markdown.tsx)): a fenced block names its language; a ```` ```mermaid ```` block is drawn by Mermaid with `securityLevel: "strict"` once its source has been still for 400 ms, and stays the code block until then or when it does not parse. [`MermaidDiagram.tsx`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/markdown/MermaidDiagram.tsx) and Mermaid are lazy chunks in every app. Tables, code copy, message copy, KaTeX (`trust: false`) and images-as-links were in place. `one-renderer.test.ts` also forbids `mermaid` outside the renderer, and now reads `workforce/src` and `workforce/e2e`, which phase 5 had added without telling it.
- **Attachments (T1.8)** ([`use-attachment-staging.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/hooks/use-attachment-staging.ts)): a file from 256 KB goes up through XMLHttpRequest and shows its own progress (bar and percentage); a failed chip keeps the server's reason, announced as an alert, with *Try again*. Paste, drop, picker, thumbnails and the cap were in place. An upload the user walks away from is aborted: removing the chip, switching conversation, sending without waiting and unmounting all call `abort()` on the request (`uploadAttachment` takes an `AbortSignal`; a cancelled upload rejects with code `UPLOAD_CANCELLED`), the handlers then stay silent (no error toast, no chip update), and a file whose reply still arrives after its chip was discarded is deleted from the server again.
- **Keyboard in Workforce (T1.10).** ⌘K / Ctrl+K opens a [quick switcher](../../ui/workforce/src/components/workforce/quick-switcher.tsx) over the sidebar's chats and team sessions, the agents and "New chat" (combobox + listbox, a lazy chunk); ⌘⇧O / Ctrl+Shift+O goes Home with the composer focused. The shortcuts dialog lists every shortcut. The widget: Esc stops.
- **Installable Workforce (T1.11).** `GET /workforce/manifest.webmanifest` ([`WorkforceManifest`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/ui/WorkforceManifest.java), `RestWorkforceResource#manifest`) renders the manifest from the current brand: name, short name, primary colour, the brand's icon slot (a PNG's real size from its header, an SVG `any`), standalone, scoped to `/workforce`. A literal JAX-RS path beside the catch-all; public like the shell. The shells link it, an `apple-touch-icon` and the theme colour, with `viewport-fit=cover, interactive-widget=resizes-content`; the layout pads by the safe-area insets and takes its height from the visual viewport while an iOS keyboard shrinks it ([`use-visual-viewport-height.ts`](../../ui/workforce/src/hooks/use-visual-viewport-height.ts)).
- **Accessibility (T1.12).** [`ReplyAnnouncer`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/components/ReplyAnnouncer.tsx): a polite status region says each completed reply once, with its speaker, and the client's notices; the transcript `log` is `aria-live="off"` (it used to be live, with `aria-relevant="additions"`, which reads streamed text piecemeal). Muted text and controls in the chat no longer fade the token below contrast (`/40`–`/80` modifiers), with a guard test; the conversation-info toggle has a translated name and `aria-expanded`. Focus returns to the composer after a send, a new chat and Stop, and to where it was when the quick switcher closes.
- **Locales and themes (T1.13).** Every new string is in the eleven locales (5 in `@eddi/conversation`, 10 in `@eddi/app-kit`, 13 in Workforce); new layout uses logical properties; the new surfaces follow the light, dark and system themes through the shared tokens.
- **Ratings (T1.14)** were complete (thumbs per reply, reasons and a comment after a thumbs down); a test now pins the whole path through `ChatPanel`.
- **Widget budget.** The widget's first load must stay within 170 KB gz. `PausedCard`, `RatingCard` (new entries `@eddi/conversation/paused-card`, `/rating-card`) and `StepRating` now load when first shown, which pays for the new code: **169.4 KB gz** (it was 170.0).
- **Platform Operator.** The prompt learns how users install Workforce, that the manifest follows the branding, that there is no service worker on purpose, and what Stop does in a cluster. No allow-list change. `operator-revision.json` 54 → **55**.
- **Docs.** [`workforce-guide.md`](../workforce-guide.md) (while the agent answers, editing, what a reply can contain, files, ratings, keyboard, installing; for administrators: the manifest and Stop in a cluster), [`chat-ui-embedding.md`](../chat-ui-embedding.md) (§5, what the widget does during a reply), `ui/manager/AGENTS.md`, `ui/chat/AGENTS.md`, `ui/workforce/AGENTS.md`, the `@eddi/conversation` README.

### Design decisions

- **No service worker.** Browsers install without one, Lighthouse dropped its PWA category, and Workforce shows signed-in conversations that must never come from a cache; an app shell would only risk a stale UI after a deploy. An e2e test fails if any worker other than the MSW mock registers.
- **The manifest is rendered, not shipped.** A static file could not carry a deployment's name, colour or icon; rendering it from `BrandingService` follows a rebrand at once, as the shells do.
- **Stop is checked, not trusted.** Two re-reads after the cancel rather than one: the cancel's own mark (`EXECUTION_INTERRUPTED`) is what an immediate read sees even when the turn runs on elsewhere.
- **Reconnect follows the turn instead of reopening the stream:** the backend has no resumable stream; the conversation read is the source of truth.
- **Progress only from 256 KB.** A small file is up before a bar could show anything, and fetch stays the path the rest of the client uses.
- **Mermaid ~12.2.0, not 12.3.0** (three days old at the time); `securityLevel: "strict"`, rendered lazily as §2.5 asks. 112 packages enter the lockfile, all in lazy chunks.

### Not done

- **Lighthouse** (exit criterion: accessibility and PWA ≥ 95 on Workforce) was not run here: no Chrome for Lighthouse in this environment, and current Lighthouse (12+) has no PWA category at all. Verified instead: the manifest (Java and e2e tests), installability criteria by hand (name, icons, `start_url`, `display`, HTTPS from EDDI), live region and focus (unit tests), contrast (guard test). Run it with `npx lighthouse http://localhost:7070/workforce --only-categories=accessibility --view` against `./mvnw quarkus:dev` after a `./mvnw package`, signed in (or `npm run dev` in `ui/workforce`, which serves the mock API).
- **No axe** in the repo (neither vitest-axe nor `@axe-core/playwright`); adding one is a dependency decision left for the follow-up.
- The widget's composer has no paste, drop or per-file progress: its first load has 0.6 KB left.
- Ratings from Workforce are recorded with channel `manager` (`MANAGER_RATING_CHANNEL`): a `workforce` channel needs the backend's `KNOWN_CHANNELS`.
- Not run here: the Playwright specs (listed and type-checked; the local browser build does not match), Stryker (no file in its scope changed: only `system-prompt.ts` text and `operator-revision.json`).

## 🧪 test: raise branch coverage back over the merged 80% gate (2026-11-05)

**Repo:** EDDI (`test/raise-branch-coverage`)

### Why

After the last day's feature merges (cost accounting, team handoff, the SERVICE judge, HITL
edit-on-approve and approval-card context, setup-api, argument schemas, the configs/llm/secrets
fixes), the `Integration Tests` job on `main` failed the JaCoCo `merged-check`:
branches covered ratio 0.79 against a minimum of 0.80. The threshold stays where it is; this
branch adds tests.

### What changed

Test code only — no production source is touched. Plain JUnit 5 + Mockito, no `@QuarkusTest`.
Every test asserts behaviour (refusal messages, routing decisions, masking, audit units), not
just execution. Classes covered, grouped by the PR that introduced or changed them:

- **Argument schemas (#1268):** `ToolArgumentSchemaValidator` (keyword reduction, nullable type
  lists, the violation cap, violation wording), `OpenApiArgumentSchemas` (no direct test before),
  `McpApiToolBuilder` helpers (unsendable inputs, header-answering operations, server variables).
- **Team handoff (#1223):** `TeamHandoffToolsProvider` — the standalone filed ledger in step data,
  artifact references, unusable targets, participant rules.
- **Cost accounting (#1222):** `MeteredTokenUsage`, `PricingLint` (cascade, judge, summary and
  tool-summarizer pricing), `UnpricedSightings`.
- **HITL (#1228, #1230):** `HitlDecisionRecords`, `EditMetrics`, `ApprovalCategories`,
  `GroupApprovalContextRenderer`, `HitlNotificationContent`.
- **Cluster rate limits:** `ToolRateLimiter` in NATS mode — shared buckets across replicas, the
  global layer giving the conversation its token back, degraded local-share and reject.
- **Elsewhere:** `ArtifactSchemaComplexity`, `EmailAgentToolsInspector`, `EmailTargetRouting`,
  `EmailReplyThread`, `TurnBudgetValidator`, `TurnObservation`, `ResourceSharingService.describe`,
  `SpaceTenants`, `JsonBodyErrors`, `AlertPayloads`, `DeliveryTarget`, `OutboundSettings.fromConfig`,
  `AttachmentClassifier`, `WebhookEventParser`, `PropertyAccessValueResolver`, `IngestionSource`
  validation, `EmbeddingParameters`, `ChatCompletionRequest`.

### Measured

Unit suite only (integration tests need Docker): branch ratio **0.7793 → 0.7915**, +867
branches. The merged unit + IT ratio could not be measured locally; CI's `Integration Tests` job
is the check.

### Noticed, not changed

`JsonBodyErrors` renders an unknown field's JSON path including the field itself
(`Unknown field 'x' at x.`): it calls `StrictConfigurationParser.jsonPath(e)`, which renders the
whole path, where the private overload documents "one short of it for an unknown field". The new
test asserts only the prefix of the message, so it does not pin either form.

## End-user catalog and Workforce for `eddi-user` (frontend unification, phase 2) (2026-11-04)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/catalog-workforce-end-users`

Phase 2 of the frontend-unification plan: a signed-in `eddi-user` can open Workforce, see what they
may use, and chat, without any page answering 403.

**Backend**

- **B1 Catalog.** `GET /catalog/agents` and `GET /catalog/groups` (`IRestCatalog`, `CatalogService`,
  `RestCatalog` in `engine/catalog`), for `eddi-user`, `eddi-editor` and `eddi-admin`. An agent is
  returned when it is READY in production (read from `IAgentFactory`, as `AgentModelResolver` does),
  the caller holds `USE` on it (the existing `ResourceAccessGuard` check, with no new access
  control), and it is **listed**. A group is returned under the same rules, using the group's own
  `USE` check, the one `POST /groups/{id}/conversations` applies. Agent items carry the profile
  fields (name, description, review notice, rating controls), `callerLevel` and `agentId`. Group
  items carry `groupId`, the current version, name, description, style, members and `callerLevel`.
  An entry that cannot be read is skipped without failing the list. See
  [End-User Catalog](../catalog.md).
- **`listed` flag** (`Boolean`, default `false`) on `AgentConfiguration` and
  `AgentGroupConfiguration`. With workspaces enforced, `published` and `internal` visibility count
  as listed too. The Platform Operator (its agent id is read from the `platform.operator` global
  variable) is listed only by its explicit flag. `OperatorRevisionCheck.OPERATOR_VARIABLE` became
  public for that. The flag is part of the JSON document, so ZIP export/import and agent sync carry
  it. Over MCP only a group's flag can be set, with `update_group`; `update_resource` does not
  write the agent document, so an agent is listed from the Manager or over REST.
  `ListedFlagSerializationTest` pins the round trip and the default.
- **B5.** `RestUserMemoryStoreCrossUserTest` runs the real `OwnershipValidator` and pins that
  `eddi-user` A gets 403 on every read and write of user B's memories, before the store is touched.
- `landing-redirect.js` now sends everyone to `/welcome`, which knows the roles (D6). It used to
  shortcut a remembered `workforce` preference straight to `/workforce`, which let a dedicated
  `eddi-viewer` or `eddi-approver` reach the end-user catalog; `LandingPage` now applies the role
  decision to those visitors too.
- Docs: new `docs/catalog.md` (plus SUMMARY), a pointer from `workspaces.md`, and a section in the
  `eddi-admin` Claude Code skill. `openapi-operations.json` gains the two operations.

**Manager (`ui/manager`)**

- Workforce is role-aware (`useWorkforceAccess`). For a user without `eddi-editor` or `eddi-admin`:
  - the dashboard is the catalog (`EndUserCatalog`), and the sidebar, the chat picker
    (`useDeployedAgents`) and the board, thread and history pages (`useWorkforceGroup`) read
    `/catalog/*` instead of `/groupstore` and `/agentstore`;
  - the wizard, team settings and analytics routes redirect (`AuthorRoute`);
  - the authoring actions on team cards, the board's settings link, the agent details panels, the
    Insights links and the mode switcher are hidden.
- **D5:** the Workforce top bar no longer mounts the Operator drawer. Admins get a link to
  `/manage/operator`.
- **D6:** `/welcome` sends an end user straight to `/workforce`, including with `?choose` and with
  a stale `manage` preference. A dedicated `eddi-viewer` or `eddi-approver` cannot read
  `/catalog/*`, so it goes to the Manager instead: an approver to `/manage/approvals`, a viewer to
  `/manage`.
- The `GET /catalog/agents` OpenAPI description no longer claims an anonymous caller sees published
  agents (copied from `/v1/models`): with authorization on an anonymous caller is refused, with it
  off visibility is not consulted.
- The catalog's empty state explains how to list an agent to authors (shown on their dashboard as
  **What end users see**). A plain user gets a neutral sentence.
- A **Listed in the catalog** switch on the agent page, in the Manager's group panel (saves at once),
  and in Workforce team settings.
- i18n: 18 new keys (`Workforce.operatorLink`, `Workforce.catalog.*`, `catalogListing.*`) in all 11
  locales.
- MSW handlers for both endpoints, plus `e2e/workforce-end-user.spec.ts`. It runs as `eddi-user`
  through a new dev-only seam (`lib/mock-roles.ts`, which reads `localStorage["eddi-mock-roles"]`
  only with the mock API active) and fails if any Workforce page requests a store the backend
  refuses that role.
- Platform Operator: the prompt explains `listed` and the catalog, the allow-list gains
  `GET /catalog/agents` and `GET /catalog/groups`, and `operator-revision.json` gets a revision bump.

**Not done here / next:** phase 3 of the plan (`@eddi/ui` and the agent kind). The catalog has no
paging; it is bounded at 2,000 group descriptors per request.

## Shared PII detectors; `pii-redaction` finds national identifiers of 27 countries (2026-11-04)

**Repo:** EDDI · **Branch:** `feat/pii-detectors`

The first piece of the reversible pseudonymization work: a detector layer that the `pii-redaction` guardrail and a later pseudonymization block share, so both find exactly the same values. It recognizes identifiers from around the world, not only German ones.

### What changed

- **New package `modules/llm/pii/`.**
  - `PiiDetector`: an SPI of CDI beans selected by `type()`, like `ContentGuardrail`. It comes with `PiiSpan`, `DetectorContext` and `DetectorUnavailableException`. A detector that cannot finish throws the exception; it never reports "nothing found" for a text it did not check.
  - `BuiltinPiiDetector` (`builtin`) and `BuiltinRecognizers`: one pattern-plus-validation recognizer for each of 40 entity types.
    - Universal types: `EMAIL`, `PHONE`, `CREDIT_CARD`, `IBAN`, `IP_ADDRESS`, `PASSPORT_MRZ` (ICAO 9303 TD3) and `DATE_OF_BIRTH`. Birth keywords are recognized in eleven languages.
    - National identifiers of 27 countries, each named `<ISO code>_<id>`: US, CA, BR and MX; GB, IE, FR, IT, ES, PT, NL, BE, DE, AT, CH, PL, SE, DK and FI; IN, CN, JP, KR, SG, AU and NZ; and ZA.
    - The patterns of the original four types moved here unchanged.
  - `PiiCountries` holds the standard set (`EMAIL`, `US_SSN`, `CREDIT_CARD`, `PHONE`, `IBAN`) and the country→types map.
  - Check digits:
    - `PiiChecksums`: Luhn, ISO 13616, and the German identifiers.
    - `NationalChecksums`: the rest. These include mod-97 (NIR, Belgium), mod-23 letters (DNI/NIE, PPS), mod-11 variants (NHS, BSN, NIF, PESEL, SVNR, CPF, TFN, IRD, My Number, CN ID), Verhoeff (Aadhaar), EAN-13 (AHV), mod-31 (HETU), the codice fiscale letter, CURP, NRIC/FIN and the ICAO MRZ digits.
    - Each validator is tested against published valid examples, not against values generated with the code under test.
  - `PiiSpanMerger` and `TermMatcher`: overlap resolution. The priority order is: the author's always-terms, then the longer span, then the higher score, then the earlier detector. Spans inside never-terms or inside skip ranges (placeholders EDDI wrote) are dropped. Term matching is whole-word and case-insensitive.
- **`PiiRedactionGuardrail`** is rebuilt on `BuiltinRecognizers`.
  - `patterns` selects kinds. When it is absent, the standard set runs, which is the original four kinds plus `iban`.
  - The new `countries` parameter adds every identifier of the listed countries. The two combine: `{"patterns": "email", "countries": "DE,FR"}`.
  - The kinds run one after another in a fixed order. E-mail runs first, so an address whose local part is an IBAN or a tax ID is redacted whole. The checked national formats come next, and the loose card and phone patterns run last, so they cannot take an identifier's digit groups (before, `DE89 3704 0044 0532…` came out as a partial `[REDACTED:phone]`).
  - The four original kinds keep their names, their `{kind}` value and their reason, and their output is byte-identical. `PiiRedactionGuardrailTest` checks this against `LegacyPiiRedaction`, a frozen copy of the old rewrite, over a fixed corpus and 3,000 random fragment combinations.
  - An unknown kind or country is now a deploy warning. Before, an unknown kind selected nothing, silently.
- **Manager:**
  - The PII section lists the universal kinds, then the 27 countries grouped by region. Ticking a country writes it to `countries`; ticking one of its identifiers writes that to `patterns`.
  - Country names come from `Intl.DisplayNames` in the UI language. Identifiers show their official names (PESEL, NIR, Codice fiscale, …).
  - Labels are in all 11 locales. A stored `patterns` that uses the entity-name aliases `CREDIT_CARD` or `US_SSN`, or that is whitespace only, ticks the right boxes.
- **Operator:** the system prompt describes `patterns`, `countries` and the opt-in rule; its revision is 66 after merging `main`.
- **Docs:** a new [PII kinds and countries](../langchain.md#pii-kinds-and-countries) section with the full table; [`security.md`](../security.md).

### Design decisions

- **National identifiers are opt-in.** Each one matches only its own country's format. Turning all 27 countries on by default would only add false positives in texts that never mention them. A compact 15-digit French NIR, for example, also passes the card Luhn check. The standard set stays what agents always had, plus the international IBAN.
- **Weak check digits need a keyword.** Where a check digit lets about one in ten random numbers of the right length through, a keyword must stand within 40 characters before the value. This applies to PESEL, NHS, BSN, NIF, SVNR, CPR, SIN, Aadhaar, My Number, TFN, Medicare, IRD and the German ID card, and to dates of birth. Identifiers with a strong check or a distinctive shape need none.
- **No check digit where the issuer dropped it.** Korean RRNs issued since October 2020 and Danish CPR numbers since 2007 carry no reliable check digit, so only their date and structure are checked, and CPR also needs a keyword.
- **Not included:** EU VAT numbers and Brazilian CNPJs, which identify businesses rather than people.
- **The guardrail applies kinds sequentially** rather than through the merger. Its replacement text is author-configurable, and the legacy result depends on later kinds seeing earlier replacements, so only sequential application keeps the output byte-identical. The merger is for pseudonymization, which replaces each value with its own token.
- **Keywords are looked for in the original text.** The kinds still rewrite the text one after another, but a keyword-bound kind checks the 40 characters before the value's position in the text as it arrived. Before, an earlier redaction could shift a keyword out of the window (a short address became the 16-character `[REDACTED:email]`), or a replacement that names a keyword could stand in for one. The guardrail keeps a position map from the rewritten text to the original for this.
- **Review fixes.** The Singapore pattern also takes the M series of FINs, issued since 1 January 2022 (ICA). ICA does not publish the check-letter algorithm, so the commonly implemented one is used: offset 3 and the table `KLJNPQRTUWX`. ICA's example `M1234567B` fails it and only shows the format. `IBAN_LENGTHS` now has every country of the IBAN registry; BI, DJ, FK, HN, LY, MN, NI, OM, RU, SD, SO and YE were missing. After an accepted value the scan resumes at its end, not at the end of the raw match, so two IBANs in a row are both found. The card scan is the exception and keeps the legacy behaviour. IPv6 matches are checked against the IPv4 spans in one pass rather than against every accepted span. The Swedish day check formats with `Locale.ROOT`, so a JVM locale with Arabic-Indic digits cannot reject a valid personnummer. A second round: IBANs are found in lower case too (`de89 3704 …`), upper-cased before the mod-97 check, because a guard that misses a typed IBAN is a gap rather than a documentation issue. The Austrian SVNR accepts the fictitious months 13–15 that the Hauptverband assigns when a birth date is unknown or its serials are used up (parliamentary answer 4690/AB XXIII. GP, 2008). The New Zealand IRD upper limit is 200,000,000, as Inland Revenue announced on 10 February 2026. An IPv6 address with seven groups and a leading or trailing `::` (RFC 4291 §2.2) is found.
- **The detector honours an explicit request.** When `DetectorContext.entities()` names types, those run even if they are outside the standard set. The standard set is the default only when nothing is requested.
- **No new regex budget class, and no budget on the guardrail.** `RegexDeadline` already provides one, and `BuiltinPiiDetector` honours the caller's deadline, which a pseudonymization chain will need. Every pattern is bounded, so a scan is linear; a test runs all 40 recognizers over about 600,000 characters of adversarial digit, letter, separator and keyword runs within 10 seconds. `regex-filter` keeps its budget because its patterns are the author's.
- **The package is `modules/llm/pii`,** not `modules/llm/pseudonymization` as the plan said, because the detectors serve both features.

### Next

PR 2 of the pseudonymization plan: the token codec, key provider, session and restorer, unit-tested and not yet wired into `LlmTask`.

## ⚡ perf(ui): frontend unification phase 0 — bundle budgets, per-locale fonts, a lighter widget (2026-11-04)

**Repo:** EDDI (`perf/frontend-phase0-baseline`)

Phase 0 of the frontend unification plan (revision 3, branch `claude/amazing-thompson-jzrvz7`, `planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §7): measure first, take the two quick wins, write down where Open WebUI is the better answer, and record what each conversation surface can do today.

### First load, gzip -9, before any lazy chunk

| Page | Before (main, 2026-11-04) | After | Budget |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/manage`, `/workforce`, `/welcome` | 743.8 KB | 616.4 KB | 630 KB |
| `/chat` | 224.8 KB | 160.6 KB | 170 KB |

KB are 1000 bytes. The plan's §1.2 measured 714 KB and 217 KB on 2026-10-29; main grew in between, so the "before" column is re-measured on today's `origin/main`. `/manage`, `/workforce` and `/welcome` still load the identical file set — separating them is phase 5.

### What changed and why

- **Budget check in CI.** [`ui/scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs`](../../ui/scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs) reads each built HTML shell, sums the gzipped scripts, module preloads and stylesheets it references, and compares them with [`ui/bundle-budget.json`](../../ui/bundle-budget.json). Server-rendered files (`/__*__.js`, `/branding/*`) are skipped by name; any other reference missing from `dist/` fails the check rather than shrinking the number. `UI Manager Checks` and `UI Chat` now run `vite build` and `npm run budget` after their tests. Raising a budget is a decision (plan §8), not a fix.
- **Manager: script fonts per locale.** [`main.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/main.tsx) imported all eight Noto families; their `@font-face` rules were 128 KB gz of the 158 KB main stylesheet. Only Noto Sans stays eager. [`locale-fonts.ts`](../../ui/packages/ui/src/i18n/locale-fonts.ts) (moved to `@eddi/ui` in phase 3a) imports a family's CSS when its locale becomes active: `ar` → Arabic, `th` → Thai, `hi` → Devanagari, `ja` → JP, `ko` → KR, `zh`/`zh-CN`/`zh-Hans` → SC, `zh-TW`/`zh-HK`/`zh-MO`/`zh-Hant` → TC (from the detected tag; the Manager ships one `zh` locale). The first paint waits up to 3 s for the active locale's font CSS, so a Japanese user does not see a font swap. **Text in another script inside a Latin UI** (a Japanese agent reply in an English Manager) still renders: `--font-sans` names every family, and where one is not loaded the browser falls through to `system-ui`, which has these scripts on every desktop and mobile OS. It is drawn in the system's face rather than in Noto.
- **Widget: no react-router.** [`location.ts`](../../ui/chat/src/location.ts) parses the three shapes — `/chat/managed/:intent/:userId`, `/chat/:environment/:agentId/:userId`, `/chat/:environment/:agentId` — with react-router's matching rules (case-insensitive literals, `managed` outranks `:environment`, trailing slashes ignored, params percent-decoded). Unknown paths still go to `/chat/production/default`, and `?token=` is still removed with `history.replaceState`. [`ChatLocationProvider`](../../ui/chat/src/components/ChatLocationProvider.tsx) replaces `BrowserRouter`, and its `initialPath` mode replaces `MemoryRouter` in the tests. `location.test.tsx` pins every URL shape, because embedders link to them.
- **Widget: raw-HTML parser on demand.** `rehype-raw` (and parse5 with it) is now a lazy chunk like KaTeX and highlight.js, loaded when a reply contains `<` followed by a letter, `/`, `!` or `?`. Per D9 the widget **still renders sanitised HTML for every agent**; only the loading changed. Once the chunk is in, the output is the same: [`raw-html-lazy.test.tsx`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/markdown/raw-html-lazy.test.tsx) compares the two pipelines on a corpus. The two byte-level differences of parse5's round trip (whitespace text hoisted before a table, `data-footnote-ref=""` rather than `"true"`) render the same and are normalised in the comparison. While the chunk loads, a reply's HTML is briefly missing and its text shows.
- **Docs (D10).** [`open-webui-integration.md`](../open-webui-integration.md) and the README now say that a deployment wanting only generic chat can use Open WebUI over `/v1`, and list what it goes without: approvals in the chat, quick replies and `inputField`, secret input, ratings, review notices, group discussions, memory transparency, branding.
- **Feature matrix.** [`ui/manager/e2e/feature-matrix.spec.ts`](../../ui/manager/e2e/feature-matrix.spec.ts) has one cell per capability (KaTeX, highlight.js, output images, quick replies, `inputField`, secret input, history, inline HITL decision, i18n) and surface (`ChatPanel`, Operator, Workforce member thread), tagged `@surface:` and `@capability:`. `yes` cells assert against MSW. `no` cells are `test.fixme` with the reason. `unit` cells are supported but unreachable in MSW (no activated operator, no agent asking for a secret) and are skipped with the unit tests that cover them. The widget's column is [`ui/chat/src/feature-matrix.test.tsx`](../../ui/chat/src/feature-matrix.test.tsx), a Vitest spec, because `ui/chat` has no Playwright tier.

### Not done, or not verified

- The Manager feature-matrix spec was **not run locally**: the installed Playwright browser build does not match `@playwright/test` 1.64.1, and downloading the browser was out of scope for this session. CI's `UI Manager E2E (MSW)` runs it.
- The widget's matrix is unit-level, not Playwright. Adding Playwright to `ui/chat` is a lockfile change better done with phase 1's single root lockfile.

### Next

Phase 1 (workspaces, one lockfile, `@eddi/core`) and phase 2 (catalog, Workforce for `eddi-user`) can start. The remaining 616 KB of the Manager's first load is mostly its own code, which phase 5 splits.

## 🧩 refactor(ui): `@eddi/core`, and the 1:1 turn loops on it (2026-11-04)

**Repo:** EDDI (`refactor/ui-eddi-core`, stacked on `chore/ui-npm-workspaces`)

Phase 1b of the frontend unification plan (revision 3, `planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §1.3, §2.7, §3.1, §3.2, §7 phase 1, §9): the first shared package, `@eddi/core` under [`ui/packages/core`](../../ui/packages/core/README.md), with the union of the turn loops' protocol tests, and the widget and the Manager's `ChatPanel` moved onto it.

### What changed and why

- **`@eddi/core`** — framework-free TypeScript, no React and no DOM beyond `fetch`, consumed as source through the workspace link (`exports` → `src/index.ts`; no build step). Every module was derived from code the apps already had twice or three times, not written afresh:
  - `http.ts`: `HttpClient` — status-aware fetch, `ApiError`, the `{error, code}` envelope (`errorPayload`), an injected `AuthProvider` with one 401 retry, and an injectable transport and error factory, so an app keeps its own error type. The module-level `setBaseUrl` / `setAuthToken` / `setUnauthorizedHandler` / `request` drive a default client, the way the Chat UI always used this layer.
  - `sse.ts`: **one** frame parser and stream reader for agent and group streams, replacing three that each got a different subset right. It strips exactly one delimiter space, joins multi-line `data:`, normalises CRLF on the accumulated buffer (so a `\r\n` split across two reads still splits the frame), accepts bare CR, flushes the decoder and the last unterminated frame at EOF, and skips frames with no `event:` line where the caller asks (group streams). The two readers that disagreed: the Manager's chat reader did not flush the decoder; the Chat UI's `.trim()`med event names. The package follows the majority (flush) and the spec (one optional space). **For the Manager's agent stream the flush is a deliberate reversal**: its old reader left the decoder unflushed on purpose, so a body cut mid multi-byte character dropped the malformed tail; it now ends in one U+FFFD in the transcript. `decode(value, {stream: true})` only ever holds back an *incomplete* trailing sequence, so no complete character is affected either way, and one reader with one rule was the point of the change: a visible replacement glyph marks a truncated reply where the old reader hid the truncation.
  - `conversation.ts`: start, read, send, stream, undo, redo, rerun, end, the managed variants and the profile, as `createConversationApi(http)` plus bound functions over the default client. `hitl.ts`: approval status, cancel, resume, deadline maths, poll cadence. `stream-events.ts`: error frames, `UNCONSUMED_STREAM_ERROR_CODES`, tool activity (`parseToolCallActivity`), `inputField`, dropped vs paused turns, output texts and images. `snapshot.ts`, `attachments.ts`, `ratings.ts` (with `createRatingClient(http, channel)`), `rating-steps.ts` (the rule the two apps held twice, with the union of their "belongs to no step" and "not ratable" predicates) and `types.ts` (eleven SSE event types — `guardrail_triggered` was missing from the Chat UI's list; `tool_result` came with the merge of `main`). `tool-labels.ts` (`humanizeToolName`, `ToolActivity`) moved in from the Chat UI when `main` was merged, because the `tool_call` parser and `gatedToolNames` now name a tool in words and the package cannot import an app.
  - Its README is the backend contract list that was in `ui/chat/AGENTS.md`, each rule now naming the test that pins it.
- **Tests first.** The Chat UI's protocol suites (`http`, `chat-api`, `sse-events` with its Java-pinned review test, `snapshot`, `hitl-api`, `attachments-api`, `rating-api`, `rating-steps`) and the Manager's `parse-sse-frame` suite moved into the package. [`turn-protocol.test.ts`](../../ui/packages/core/src/turn-protocol.test.ts) encodes the protocol-level cases the five loops' suites (`use-chat`, `use-operator-chat`, the Workforce member thread, `ChatWidget` + `chat-store`) each held in UI terms: a 409 (and a pre-turn `error` code) is a refusal whose input was never consumed, dropped vs paused turns, `inputField` on `done`, undo/redo's empty 200 and 409, rerun's parameters, cancel, resume, the injected auth and error factory, and the framing cases the three readers disagreed on.
- **Boundaries.** `sharedPackage()` in [`ui/eslint.config.base.js`](../../ui/eslint.config.base.js) forbids, in a package, an app import, React, `@eddi/ui` and the DOM globals; [`boundaries.test.ts`](../../ui/packages/core/src/boundaries.test.ts) checks the same over every `ui/packages/*` and fails on an import cycle (its cycle finder is tested against a synthetic one). The package's `tsconfig.json` is as strict as the strictest app (the Manager's `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, ES2020), because each app compiles it.
- **Widget (`ui/chat`).** `ChatWidget`, `ChatInput`, `PausedCard`, the HITL polling and rating hooks and the store import the protocol from `@eddi/core`. Deleted: `api/http.ts`, `api/chat-api.ts`, `api/sse-events.ts`, `api/snapshot.ts`, `api/attachments-api.ts`, `rating-steps.ts` and their tests. The widget keeps what is its own: `embed-auth.ts`, the brand, the demo, `pauseHeadline` (`api/hitl-api.ts`), the `chat-ui` rating channel and error copy (`api/rating-api.ts`) and `skippedTurnMessage` (`turn-notices.ts`). `@/types` re-exports the contract types. Its consumer tests changed in import paths only; `vite-proxy.test.ts` also scans the package for the path prefixes it calls.
- **Manager (`ui/manager`).** `lib/api/chat.ts` is now the Manager's binding of the package's conversation API: same signatures and message types (the Operator and the Workforce member thread import it too, so neither changed), with an `HttpClient` built on a new `ApiClient.authorizedFetch` (pre-flight refresh, one forced refresh and retry on a 401, network failures as `ApiClientError` status 0, an abort left an `AbortError`) and `apiErrorFromResponse` — so the raw-fetch stream and `text/plain` send now refresh the token like every `api.post`. `startConversation` stays an `api.post`: `ApiClient` also reads a `location` field from the body, which the MSW fixtures rely on. `groups.ts`'s group reader is `readSseStream`; `use-chat.ts` takes `UNCONSUMED_STREAM_ERROR_CODES` and `parseErrorEvent` from the package; `ChatPanel` and `use-chat-ratings` take the rating steps from it. Deleted: `lib/api/sse-utils.ts` (and its test, ported), `lib/chat-rating-steps.ts`.
- **Gates.** CI's `UI Chat` job runs the package's type check, lint and tests. `i18n:check` walks `ui/packages/*/src` (the Manager compiles them). `auto-approve-copilot.yml` protects a package's vitest, ESLint and tsconfig files, as it does an app's (`AutoApproveCopilotPolicyTest`).
- **Archived changelog links.** Eight links in `docs/changelog/2026-09.md` and `2026-10.md` named files this change deletes; they now point at the package module the code moved to (`DocumentationLinksTest`). Only the link targets changed.
- **Platform Operator.** No endpoint, field or failure mode changed, so neither its prompt nor its allow-list needed an update; its code (`use-operator-chat.ts`, `lib/operator`) is untouched (phase 6).

### Not done, or not verified

- The CI jobs were not run (a stacked PR gets none). Locally, on Windows: `npm ci` and `npm run lockfile:check` at `ui/` (the lock was regenerated in a `node:26.13.0` container); `@eddi/core` typecheck, lint and 203 tests; the Chat UI typecheck, lint, 512 tests (+2 todo), build and budget (162.2 KB gz of 170); the Manager typecheck, lint, `i18n:check`, 9,322 of 9,325 tests (the three failures are the known environmental ones in `resource-detail-rag-upload-source.test.tsx`), build and budget (617.8 KB gz of 630, from 616.4). `./mvnw compile`, `./mvnw package -DskipTests` (947 entries under `META-INF/resources`, as on the base branch) and `BuildQualityGatesTest`, the `Documentation*Test`s, `ChangelogFragmentTest`, `ImportStyleTest` and `AutoApproveCopilotPolicyTest`.
- Not run: the Manager's Playwright tiers and Stryker. Stryker's scope (`lib/operator/**`, `updates.ts`, `hitl-tool-approvals.ts`) has no changed file, though `write-canary.ts` now reaches the backend through the package.
- The Workforce member thread (`workforce-thread.tsx`) and the Operator use the package only through `lib/api/chat.ts`; their own error-frame parsing and state machines are untouched. Both, and the Manager's `lib/api/hitl.ts`, `lib/api/attachments.ts` and `conversations.ts` extractors, move in phase 3 with `AgentConversation` (and phase 6 for the Operator).
- `use-chat.ts` still parses the `error` frame inline in `handleSSEEvent`: the package's `parseErrorMessage` falls back to English for an empty payload, the Manager to an empty reason it then translates.

### Next

Phase 2 (catalog and Workforce for `eddi-user`) or phase 3 (`@eddi/ui` and the agent kind: `AgentConversation` over this package, then every 1:1 surface on it).

## 🎨 feat(ui): `@eddi/ui`, and both apps on its tokens, theme and i18n (2026-11-04)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/ui-eddi-ui`, stacked on `refactor/ui-eddi-core`)

Phase 3a of the frontend unification plan (`planning/frontend-unification-plan.md` §3.1, §3.3, §7 phase 3, D3): the second shared package, `@eddi/ui` under [`ui/packages/ui`](../../ui/packages/ui/README.md), with the Manager and the Chat UI moved onto its foundations. Phase 3 is split: 3b builds `@eddi/conversation` (`AgentConversation` and the shared chat components) on top of this.

### What changed and why

- **`@eddi/ui`** — consumed as source like `@eddi/core` (`exports` → `src/`), React allowed, no API calls:
  - **Tokens** (`@eddi/ui/theme.css`): the Manager's Tailwind v4 `@theme` — semantic colours, the `brand-*` / `warn-*` / `ochre-*` scales, radius, font stack, light and `.dark` — moved verbatim out of `ui/manager/src/index.css`, which now imports it. Plain `@theme`, not `inline`, so `/branding/brand.css` still reaches the utilities. `@source "../"` lets Tailwind see the package's classes. `BrandDeliveryTest`, `brand-css.test.ts` and `no-hardcoded-amber.test.ts` read the new file.
  - **`--chat-*` as documented aliases** (`CHAT_TOKEN_ALIASES`, `SEMANTIC_TOKENS`). Fifteen of the widget's variables are now `var(<shared token>)` (`--chat-bg` → `--color-background`, `--chat-accent` → `--color-primary`, `--chat-user-bg` → `--chat-accent`, …); the widget gives the tokens its own palette per theme, so nothing renders differently. The names, the URL colour parameters and `brand.css`'s `--chat-accent` keep working because an override of the `--chat-*` name still wins. [`chat-aliases.test.ts`](../../ui/chat/src/styles/chat-aliases.test.ts) pins `variables.css` to the table, every one of the 31 names to the colour it had before (both themes), every name a colour parameter writes, and `--chat-accent` staying overridable by `brand.css`.
  - **Theme**: `ThemeProvider` / `useTheme` (moved from the Manager; marks `<html>` with a class or with `data-theme`) and the helpers under it (guarded storage, resolve, apply, an OS-change subscription that re-reads the preference). The widget's `useTheme` keeps its own `?theme=` precedence on top of the helpers.
  - **i18n**: `setupI18n` — English bundled, every other locale its own chunk and only the active one loaded (`partialBundledLanguages` + a lazy backend over a loader map), region tags matched, `{{productName}}` default variables, `dir`/`lang` from the RESOLVED language; `setDefaultVariables` for a brand saved at runtime; `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES` (the eleven), `RTL_LANGUAGES`, `matchLanguage`. The Manager's `i18n/config.ts` is now its locale loaders plus one `setupI18n` call.
  - **Per-locale fonts**: phase 0's `locale-fonts.ts` moved in as the separate entry `@eddi/ui/fonts`. Separate on purpose: re-exported from the main entry, it made the widget's build emit all 468 Noto font files into `chat/dist` although nothing called it.
  - **Primitives**: `Button` / `buttonVariants` (and `cn`) and the theme-following, direction-aware `ThemedToaster` (it takes `rtl` as a prop; the Manager's new `AppToaster` passes it). The 174 Manager files that imported `@/components/ui/button` or `@/components/layout/theme-provider` import from `@eddi/ui`; `lib/utils.ts` re-exports `cn`.
  - **Boundaries**: `sharedPackage({ react, noApi })` in [`ui/eslint.config.base.js`](../../ui/eslint.config.base.js) lints `.tsx` with the hook rules and forbids `@eddi/core`, `fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest`/`EventSource`/`WebSocket` and `window.__EDDI_*__` in `@eddi/ui`; [`boundaries.test.ts`](../../ui/packages/core/src/boundaries.test.ts) checks the same (and still that core imports no UI package, and no cycle).
- **Widget i18n**: the Chat UI's own `t()` and four string tables became i18next bundles in [`ui/chat/src/i18n/locales`](../../ui/chat/src/i18n/locales/en.json), set up through `setupI18n` without react-i18next (the locale is chosen once; `main.tsx` awaits `initLocale` before the first render, so `t()` still works outside React). The seven new locales — `ar`, `zh`, `th`, `ja`, `ko`, `pt`, `hi` — are translated with the Manager's terminology for agent, conversation, approval and rating. Placeholders moved from `{name}` to `{{name}}`; a missing value still stays visible, and a value is never interpolated twice. `?lang=ar` lays the widget out right to left. [`i18n.test.ts`](../../ui/chat/src/i18n/i18n.test.ts) checks all eleven files for parity, placeholders and real translation, and every key the code passes to `t()`.
- **Gates**: CI's `UI Chat` job runs `@eddi/ui`'s type check, lint and tests next to `@eddi/core`'s. `i18n:check` already walks `ui/packages/*/src`; the package has no `t()` keys of its own (a primitive takes its text as a prop). `AutoApproveCopilotPolicyTest` covers the new package's config files. The seven script-font packages moved from the Manager's dependencies to the package's; `ui/package-lock.json` was regenerated in a `node:26.13.0` container.
- **Docs**: the three AGENTS.md files, the packages README, the new package README, the Chat UI README (eleven languages, the token layers) and [`chat-ui-embedding.md`](../chat-ui-embedding.md) (`lang`, the aliases). Two links to moved files were repointed (`docs/changelog/2026-10.md`, the phase-0 fragment) for `DocumentationLinksTest`.
- **Platform Operator**: no endpoint, config field, default or failure mode changed; neither its prompt nor its allow-list needed an update, so no revision bump. Stryker's scope (`lib/operator/**`, `updates.ts`, `hitl-tool-approvals.ts`) has no moved file. No API change, so no MSW handler changed.

### Behaviour and visual changes

- **Widget**: speaks eleven languages instead of four; its first load grew from 162.2 to 169.1 KB gz (budget 170) — i18next, less the three string tables that left the entry chunk. Each locale is a 2.8–3.4 KB gz chunk. `chat/dist` gains those ten files.
- **Manager**: none intended. The tokens, button and toaster were moved, not edited: the same declarations, classes and props (the main stylesheet's size is in the table below). A stored theme value that is not `dark`/`light`/`system` is now ignored instead of trusted.

### Review fixes (2026-11-15)

- **Focus ring under a brand.** `--chat-input-focus` aliased `--color-ring`, but `/branding/brand.css` (`BrandDelivery.stylesheet`) writes the Manager's `--color-ring` (its primary) on `:root`, and `chat.html` loads that stylesheet, so a branded widget's focus ring followed the Manager primary instead of the chat accent. It now aliases `--chat-accent`, like `--chat-user-bg` (`?accentColor=` already set all three). Unbranded, nothing changes; branded, the ring is the chat accent (on `main` it stayed the literal EDDI blue under a brand, so this also ends that mismatch). `--chat-accent` keeps its `--color-primary` alias: the brand stylesheet writes `--chat-accent` on `:root` whenever it writes `--color-primary` there (the chat accent defaults to the primary), and the widget's dark theme is `data-theme`, not the `.dark` class the brand's dark block targets. [`chat-aliases.test.ts`](../../ui/chat/src/styles/chat-aliases.test.ts) now applies that stylesheet's output and checks the accent, the user bubble and the focus ring take the chat accent in both themes while every other `--chat-*` name ignores the brand.
- **RTL.** `chat.css` used physical sides that `dir="rtl"` does not flip: the accent stripes (`border-left`, five rules) are `border-inline-start`, the absolutely placed eye toggles and the copy/close buttons (`right`) are `inset-inline-end`, and the paddings that reserve room for them (the secret fields, the rating card, the attachment chip) are `padding-inline`. Identical in LTR. The toast's `left: 50%` + `translateX(-50%)` centring stays physical.
- **`i18next` declared.** The widget imports `i18next` directly (`src/i18n/index.ts`) but relied on `@eddi/ui`'s copy being hoisted; `ui/chat/package.json` now declares it (`^26.4.7`, as the Manager and `@eddi/ui` do) and the lock's `chat` entry says so.
- **Merging `main` (through `refactor/ui-eddi-core`).** The eleven Manager files `main` added since this branch was cut import `Button` from `@eddi/ui`. `main`'s checks on the Manager's own font wiring (`main.tsx` imports only the default Noto family and installs the loader; `index.css` puts the active locale's CJK family first) were Manager tests in the file that moved into the package; they are now `ui/manager/src/i18n/__tests__/locale-fonts-wiring.test.ts`.

### Moved and deferred

- **Moved to `@eddi/ui`**: tokens, theme provider, i18n setup, locale fonts, `Button`, `ThemedToaster`, `cn`.
- **Not moved**: the Manager has no tooltip or icon-button component to move (icon buttons are `Button size="icon"`; tooltips are Radix used in place), and its dialogs, badges and inputs are not needed by the 3b chat components from both apps. The widget is not restyled onto Tailwind: its BEM stylesheet reads the shared tokens through the aliases, which is what 3a needs; D3's move to Tailwind comes with the shared components in 3b. The widget keeps the Noto Sans faces `chat.html` declares rather than loading script fonts per locale.
- **3b**: `@eddi/conversation` with `AgentConversation`, `MessageList`, `Message`, `Composer` and the rest, and the `conversation.*` namespace.

### Not done, or not verified

- The CI jobs were not run (a stacked PR gets none). Locally, on Windows: `npm ci` and `npm run lockfile:check` at `ui/` (lock regenerated in a `node:26.13.0` container); `@eddi/ui` typecheck, lint and 62 tests; `@eddi/core` typecheck, lint and 205 tests; the Chat UI typecheck, lint, 532 tests (+2 todo), build and budget (169.1 KB gz of 170, from 162.2); the Manager typecheck, lint, `i18n:check`, 9,032 of 9,035 tests (the three failures are the known environmental ones in `resource-detail-rag-upload-source.test.tsx`), build and budget (619.1 KB gz of 630, from 617.8). `./mvnw compile`, `./mvnw package -DskipTests` (951 entries under `META-INF/resources`, from 947: the widget's ten locale chunks, and six fewer files from the Manager's chunk graph — all 476 script-font files and seven family stylesheets are still there) and `BuildQualityGatesTest`, the `Documentation*Test`s, `ChangelogFragmentTest`, `ImportStyleTest`, `AutoApproveCopilotPolicyTest` and `BrandDeliveryTest`.
- Not run: Playwright (the local browser revision does not match), so the RTL e2e on the widget the plan asks of phase 3 is still to write and run; Stryker (no file in its scope changed).
- The widget's budget headroom is 0.9 KB gz. 3b, which moves it onto Tailwind and the shared components, has to find room (the router-free widget still carries `react-markdown` and `remark-gfm` eagerly) or raise the budget as a decision.

### Next

Phase 3b: `@eddi/conversation` — `AgentConversation` and the shared chat components, with `ChatPanel`, the test drawer, the Workforce chat and member thread and the widget moved onto them.
