## 🔒 fix(llm): agent-findings batch llm-tools — tool rate limit, memory ownership and erasure, selector and PDF work bounds, guardrail number parsing (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/agent-findings-llm-tools`)

### What changed and why

The 29 CodeRabbit "agent findings" filed against `modules/llm/tools/**` and `modules/llm/guardrails/**` (scans of 2026-10-26 … 11-02), each re-traced through the current code. Many were already closed by #1160, #1163, #1192 and #1194; the ones still live after those are fixed here:

- **`enableRateLimiting: false` no longer skips the operator's deployment-wide limit.** The flag turned off the whole admission call, so an agent designer's task setting opted out of `eddi.tools.ratelimit.global.*`. It now skips only the task's own per-conversation limit (`ToolRateLimiter.refusedByGlobalLimit`, same bucket, cluster-aware). Documented in [`langchain.md`](../langchain.md) and [`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md).
- **`forgetFact` no longer deletes another agent's `global` memory.** Read visibility was treated as permission to delete, so an agent could delete a foreign global key and then write it again with its own value — the replacement `rememberFact` refuses without `allowGlobalKeyOverwrite`. The same rule now applies to deletion ([`user-memory.md`](../user-memory.md)).
- **An update in `rememberFact` cannot recreate a memory a GDPR erasure just deleted.** Updates go through the update-only compare-and-set `replaceIfUnchanged` first; when that no longer applies, the inserting write runs and counts as an insert, so the existing post-write cancellation check takes it back.
- **`extractWithSelector` refuses positional pseudos on a page too wide for them.** jsoup's `:eq`/`:lt`/`:gt`/`:nth-*`/`:*-of-type`/`:only-child` walk every sibling of every candidate — quadratic in one parent's width, CPU the parse timeout cannot interrupt. With such a pseudo in the selector, a page whose sum of squared child counts exceeds 50 million is refused with a message; ordinary tables are unaffected.
- **PDF tool extraction has the ingestion extractor's two missing bounds**: unfiltered page-content references are charged to the decode budget before PDFBox parses them (`PdfTextExtractor.chargePageContents`, now public), and glyph layout stops at the character cap plus slack instead of holding millions of glyphs before the cap is applied — the text laid out so far is kept.
- **The expression guardrail compares against the real downstream number reading.** Both of its readers parsed numbers exactly, so a value only exact parsing refuses (`1e2147483648`) made both skip the same earlier object and agree on a later one while the task's double-based conversion took the earlier. The tolerant reader is now the default mapper, compared number-aware.

### Verdicts

FIXED 9 · ALREADY_FIXED 14 · ACCEPTED_RISK 1 · NEEDS_DECISION 5 · FALSE_POSITIVE 0.

Open decisions (not implemented): process-local tool budget lost on cost-tracker eviction (two findings); quota refunds read failure from result text; `converse_with_agent`'s per-task count resets on a HITL resume; dynamic-agent USE checks treat a `/v1` shared-key conversation's self-asserted user id as a verified principal. The `OutboundHttp` DNS-rebinding residual (a blind request before the post-connect re-check) stays the documented accepted risk in [`security.md`](../security.md#dns-rebinding).

---

## 🔒 fix(secrets): agent-findings pass over the vault, grant checks, redaction and connections (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/agent-findings-secrets-connections`)

### What changed and why

The 45 CodeRabbit "agent findings" filed against the secrets vault, the grant and host checks, the redaction layer and connections were each traced through the current code. Most had already been closed by the October hardening rounds; nine were still real and are fixed here.

- **Space-membership check could miss a tenant** ([`VaultGrantChecker`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/VaultGrantChecker.java)). Tenant discovery used one scanner for `${vault:` and `${vars:`, so a malformed opener of one namespace (`${vars:a${vault:t.finance…/key}`) swallowed a real reference of the other into a single bogus tenant, while the runtime resolved the inner reference with its own rules. Vault tenants now come from a vault-only scanner and variable tenants from `GlobalVariableResolver.VARS_PATTERN`, the grammar the resolver uses (made public for this).
- **Rotation racing a delete** ([`VaultSecretProvider.store`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/impl/VaultSecretProvider.java)). A rotation whose row vanished was turned into a create, and the create checks its endpoint runs had been skipped. The Manager's rotate call, which restates the agent grant but no host list, re-created the key sendable to any host. The rotation now fails and writes nothing.
- **Stale conditional grant edit** ([`RestSecretStore.updateGrant`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/rest/RestSecretStore.java)). With `expectedAllowedAgents` and `allowedHosts` together, the host list was committed before the precondition was checked, so a stale request answered 409 "nothing was written" after it had changed where the key may go. The precondition is now checked before the host write. An edit landing in the moment between that check and the grant write is still refused by the conditional grant write, after the hosts. One atomic operation for both lists would need a new persistence operation on both backends.
- **Linear unresolved-reference check** ([`CredentialReferenceResolver`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/connections/CredentialReferenceResolver.java)). The last `find()` over a `[^}]*` reference regex in the area, run on decrypted credential text, was quadratic on unterminated openers. It is replaced by an exact linear equivalent.
- **Read redaction** ([`ConfigSecretRedactor`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/sanitize/ConfigSecretRedactor.java)). A numeric value in an embedded `customHeaders` / `customQueryParams` map is judged like a string. A JSON request body has every literal under a credential-named field masked whatever its length, because the shape scan's eight-character floor left `{"password":"hunter2"}` legible to editors. Only one whole JSON document is re-serialized, never a prefix.
- **Long non-JSON text** ([`SecretRedactionFilter`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/sanitize/SecretRedactionFilter.java)). The 1 MiB bound that assumes a JSON carrier now applies only to text that could be JSON. Text that cannot be JSON keeps apostrophe-quoted passwords whole across an embedded double quote.
- **Export scrubbing** ([`SecretScrubber`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/secrets/sanitize/SecretScrubber.java)). The additional query-name rule (`key`, `sig`, …) now also judges the percent-decoded name on both export branches, so `%6Bey=` no longer survives export.

Docs: [`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md) (rotation racing a delete, the grant precondition and host lists) and [`security.md`](../security.md) (JSON body masking).

### Verdicts (45 findings)

FIXED 9 · ALREADY_FIXED 27 · ACCEPTED_RISK 6 · NEEDS_DECISION 2 · FALSE_POSITIVE 1.

- **Accepted (documented):** the connections master switch does not stop `${connection:…}` resolution ([`connections.md`](../connections.md)). Unreadable or absent grant metadata is allowed at deploy time, with the grant checked at creation instead, and a deployment racing a secret's creation is a named gap ([`secrets-vault.md`](../secrets-vault.md#what-counts-as-granted)).
- **Open:** whether an OAuth service grant should carry the connection's immutable id, so that a stale configuration minting during a delete and re-create cannot store its token under the successor. And whether GDPR erasure should report the auto-vaulted-secret step incomplete when the vault is disabled (no master key) but secret rows remain.

### Design decisions

- No runtime per-agent grant check was added. `VaultGrantChecker`'s class Javadoc records why resolution time is the wrong place, and the race findings that ask for one are accepted as documented.
- The rotation now prefers "the delete wins" over re-creating the key. A re-create would have to repeat every endpoint check (host list, deployed-agent impact) inside the provider, which knows neither the mode nor the deployments.

## 🔒 fix(manager): agent-findings batch for the Manager and Chat UI (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/agent-findings-ui`)

Verified 19 findings from the CodeRabbit agent-findings export against the current code. Most had already been closed by the review-fix PR #1200; the rest are fixed here.

### What changed and why

- **Per-call approval decisions survive a `__proto__` call id.** `ApprovalBanner` built its `toolDecisions` on a plain `{}`; a provider-chosen call id of `__proto__` re-prototyped the object, the reviewer's explicit rejection vanished from the JSON, and the backend read the omitted call as approved. The dictionary now has no prototype.
- **Secret rotation no longer restates the grant.** `rotateSecret` sent the grant and description read just before the PUT, so a concurrent revocation could be put back (last-writer-wins). Both backends keep an omitted grant and description on an update (`VaultSecretProvider.store`, `MongoSecretPersistence.secretWrite`, the PostgreSQL upsert), and the Manager is bundled with the backend that does, so the PUT now carries the value only.
- **Operator gate verification refuses exemptions it cannot judge.** `ToolApprovalGate.addressesOf` matches a call by `http.<method>:<path>`, `<source>:<name>` and the bare name, so an `exempt` entry or `AUTO_APPROVE` rule such as `deployAgent` or `http:*` could un-gate a write while the Manager certified the gate. `gateLooksInstalled` now accepts such an entry only when it fixes a read method (`http.get:`, `http.head:`, `http.options:`) or a non-`http` source literally; anything else is refused (the safe direction: write scope withheld, or a warning on a created agent). `operator-revision.json` is not bumped: neither the system prompt nor the allow-list changed.
- **Hostile-input bounds in the group transcript and diff viewer.** The markdown autolink matcher stops at the next `<` and is bounded (it ran to the end of the text from every opener); the JSON repair of malformed task arrays is a single forward pass that tracks string and escape state (a regex retried from every escaped quote); `reindentJsonText` caps printed indentation at 64 levels (N openers printed N lines of up to N levels) and trims a trailing-comma line by hand instead of with a regex scanning the whole output on every closer.

### Verdicts (19)

FIXED 6; ALREADY_FIXED 11 (by #1200: the diff-viewer budget, JSON pretty-print bounds, markdown destination, indented-run and verification-line bounds, selective export, conversation/agent binding, erasure cache sweep, latest-version matching, operator pause binding, and the every-write-method gate check); ACCEPTED_RISK 2 (`fc72ab76`, `96a089e2`); FALSE_POSITIVE 0; NEEDS_DECISION 0. The URL-token adoption in the chat widget is off unless the operator sets `eddi.chat.url-token.enabled` and is documented as a residual login-CSRF risk; the release check's `Origin` header cannot be suppressed by a cross-origin fetch and the card says so (a same-origin proxy would be a new backend endpoint).

**Files:** [`approval-banner.tsx`](../../ui/manager/src/components/hitl/approval-banner.tsx), [`secrets.ts`](../../ui/packages/app-kit/src/lib/api/secrets.ts), [`operator.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/api/operator.ts), [`group-utils.ts`](../../ui/packages/conversation/src/team/group-utils.ts), [`reindent-json.ts`](../../ui/manager/src/lib/reindent-json.ts)

## ✨ feat(hitl): config-driven context on approval cards (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/approval-card-context`), stacked on `feat/hitl-edit-on-approve`

### What changed and why

An approval card said *that* something was waiting and *why*. For a decision that is more than "run it" — publish this
post, send this reply — the approver also needs the thing itself. `approvalContext` lets the agent or group designer
say what to show: short templates over the conversation's own data, rendered **once, when the pause is committed**, and
served unchanged on every surface.

- **Config** — `hitlConfig.approvalContext` (agent, at most 8 blocks), `toolApprovals.rules[].context` (at most 4,
  with `call.*` = the one gated call the rule matched) and the group `hitlConfig.approvalContext` (at most 8, with
  `group.*`: the phase, members, transcript, `lastByRole`/`lastByType`/`lastBySpeaker`, tasks awaiting approval and
  shared artifacts). A block is `{label, template, maxChars}`
  ([`ApprovalContextBlock`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/hitl/model/ApprovalContextBlock.java)). All
  optional; absent config renders nothing, stores nothing and leaves the Slack card byte-identical.
- **Save-time validation** — [`ApprovalContextValidation`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/hitl/ApprovalContextValidation.java)
  from `HitlConfigValidation`, so every store and the ZIP import refuse a bad block with a 400 that names it: limits, a
  template that does not parse on the restricted runtime engine
  ([`RuntimeTemplateParseCheck`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/templating/impl/RuntimeTemplateParseCheck.java),
  extracted from `TemplateSyntaxMigrator`, which now delegates to it), the `vault:`/`eddivault:`/`connection:`/`caller:`
  namespaces (they would print a secret's name on a card), and `call.*` outside a rule.
- **Rendering** — [`ApprovalContextRenderer`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/context/ApprovalContextRenderer.java)
  (regular conversations and rule blocks per gated call) and
  [`GroupApprovalContextRenderer`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/context/GroupApprovalContextRenderer.java),
  on a dedicated restricted `TemplatingEngine` with a 500 ms / 16,000-character / 2,000-iteration budget per block and
  8,000 characters in total. Reference tokens become `[secret]`, `SecretRedactionFilter` runs, and tool arguments come
  only from `argumentsRedacted` — `argumentsRaw` is never read. A block that throws, times out or exceeds a limit
  becomes an item with an `error` and **never fails or delays the pause**.
- **Persistence** — one field, `hitlApprovalContext` ([`RenderedApprovalContext`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/context/RenderedApprovalContext.java):
  items, `truncated`, a SHA-256 `digest`), on the conversation snapshot and the group conversation. Written at the
  single pause-commit hooks (`ConversationHitlService.populateHitlTimeoutBookmark`, `GroupHitlCoordinator.commitPause`
  and the restore after a failed group resume), overwritten by a re-pause, cleared with the bookmark in memory, in the
  MongoDB and PostgreSQL stores and on every group resume/reject/cancel/fail. The raw conversation read strips it.
- **Surfaces** — `approvalContext` on REST `approval-status` and MCP `get_approval_status` (one shared
  [`ApprovalContextViews`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/hitl/context/ApprovalContextViews.java), re-redacted
  at serve time; the MCP summary map is now `Map<String,Object>`, its existing values unchanged), on the group
  approval-status (REST and MCP) and on the group conversation read.
- **Slack** — [`SlackApprovalContextBlocks`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/integrations/slack/SlackApprovalContextBlocks.java)
  inserts the blocks before the `actions` block (which stays last), as `plain_text` (so no mention, link or bold
  spoofing), within Slack's 50-block budget ("+N more context items — open in Manager"). Because everyone in the
  approval channel sees a card, the channel needs `platformConfig.hitlApprovalContext = "true"`; without it the card
  gets one line, "More context available in the Manager". A card that carries context is posted with
  `unfurl_links`/`unfurl_media` off (`SlackWebApiClient.postBlocksMessage(…, unfurl)`); cards without context are posted
  by the unchanged call. `HitlPauseEvent` gains a `@JsonIgnore approvalContext` component and keeps its old constructors.
- **Manager** — `ApprovalContext` shows the items as plain text (`whitespace-pre-wrap`, never markdown or HTML) above the
  decision on the Approvals page, the conversation page and the group discussion; a block editor
  (`ApprovalContextEditor`) in the agent's Human-in-the-Loop section and in the group approval editor, with the same
  limits shown as the author types. New keys under `approvalContext.*` in all 11 locales.
- **Docs and metrics** — new page [HITL Approval Card Context](../hitl-approval-context.md) (config, the data model, the
  audience table, validation, Slack), linked from `hitl.md`, `group-conversations.md`, `security.md` and `SUMMARY.md`;
  `eddi_hitl_approval_context_render_total{outcome,surface}` in `metrics.md` and on the Full Metrics Reference dashboard
  (panel 420); two properties in the configuration reference. The operator and MCP follow-up is in
  [`planning/operator-followups/approval-card-context.md`](../../planning/operator-followups/approval-card-context.md).

### Not in this change

- No Platform Operator edits and no new MCP tools (both go to the combined follow-up).
- The Manager's tool-approval editor has no rule editor, so `rules[].context` is written in the agent's JSON.
- No `hitl.draft` template root: this branch does not resolve a rule pause's draft for templates.
- Stacked on `feat/hitl-edit-on-approve`: merge after it. Expect a trivial conflict with the approval-analytics branch in
  the Full Metrics Reference dashboard's HITL row and in `GroupConversation`/`HitlConfig` field lists.

## 🐛 fix(cluster): leases stop trusting themselves, survive lost replies, and cost fewer round trips (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/cluster-lease-validity`) — workstream A of the cluster production-readiness review (`planning/cluster-production-readiness-review.md`, findings A1, A2, A11, D1–D5, D7, and the lease part of C12).

### What changed and why

**A1 — a holder cut off from NATS stops itself.** A renewal that could not reach NATS used to be ignored forever (`KvLeaseManagerTest.heartbeatIoFailureKeepsLease` asserted it), so a partitioned holder kept running — LLM calls, tools, the unfenced user-memory upsert, a 200 to its client — after another node had taken the conversation over at `lease.ttl`. A lease is now trusted for `ttl − 2 × heartbeat` (10 s by default) after the last renewal NATS *acknowledged*, counted from when that renewal was *sent*, so the server-side TTL always ends later. Past that `LeaseHandle.isLost()` is true at once and the lost callbacks fire (from the tick every quarter heartbeat, or from `isLost()` itself, on the I/O executor). The test is rewritten to assert the new contract. Contract for the turn side (WS-B): check `isLost()` before the user-memory upsert and before delivering the reply.

**A2 — a write applied on the server whose reply was lost.** Every lease value now carries a lease id (`lid`) unique within the process. A renewal that conflicts re-reads the key from the stream leader: if it still holds this lease (same node, boot and `lid`), an earlier renewal landed although its reply timed out — the lease is kept at the revision found (`eddi_cluster_lease_adopted_total{op="renew"}`) instead of cancelling a healthy turn. An acquire that finds its own `lid` adopts the lease its timed-out create wrote; a lease of this process that nobody here holds (an acquire that gave up) is deleted instead of blocking the conversation for the TTL — this node included. `release()` of a lost handle now still deletes the key when it is provably still this lease.

**A11 — waiter markers and peek.** A granted waiter deletes its `w.<key>` marker by compare-and-set at the revision it wrote, so another node's marker survives; a waiter that times out, is drained or shut down removes its own. New `IConversationLeaseManager.peekStrict` / `peekKeyStrict` throw `ClusterUnavailableException` instead of reading an outage as "no lease" (for `ClusterHitlRecovery.eligible`, owned by WS-B).

**D1 — fewer round trips.** Acquire reads the key's last record (`ISharedKv.latest`, which also returns a delete marker's revision) and writes at that revision: 2 round trips instead of jnats' 3 for a `create` on a deleted key. Release is one guarded delete that returns the marker's revision (`deleteReturningRevision`); the unconditional waiter-marker read and the broadcast are gone. An uncontended turn's lease went from about 6 to 3 round trips; a waiter woken by a release acquires with 1.

**D2 — contention.** The coordinator hands a healthy lease straight to the next turn of the same conversation queued on this node (`IConversationLeaseManager.handOver`: no I/O, refused when less than a heartbeat of validity is left, when another node is known to wait, or after 16 handoffs in a row). The waiter poll is capped at 500 ms (250–750 ms jittered; it was 2.5–7.5 s at the default TTL).

**D3 — reopen storm.** `NatsSharedKv` reopens a bucket handle once, under a lock, for all the callers that failed on it; it never drops a handle younger than 1 s or on a full outgoing queue / closed connection, and a failed open is not retried for 250 ms.

**D4 — heartbeats at scale.** At most 64 renewals in flight, least recently confirmed first; each lease is renewed when 3/4 of a heartbeat has passed since its last renewal, from a tick every quarter heartbeat, so thousands of leases no longer go out in one burst.

**D5 — stale-lease sweep.** Runs once per process (leases of an earlier boot cannot appear after it started), with one watch instead of a read per key — no more whole-bucket scans on every node at every reconnect.

**D7 — release notifications.** A waiter tells the holder's node that it waits (`<root>.lease.to.<nodeId>`, core NATS, repeated with each poll); the holder addresses the release only to those nodes, with the delete marker's revision. The cluster-wide broadcast is kept only for an administrative force-release.

**C12 (lease part).** `eddi_cluster_lease_acquire_seconds` (every outcome × contended), `eddi_cluster_lease_lost_total` (every reason), `eddi_cluster_lease_adopted_total` and `eddi_cluster_degraded_decisions_total{area="turns"}` are registered at construction, so `increase()` sees the first occurrence on each pod.

### Rolling upgrade: why old and new nodes keep mutual exclusion

- **Acquisition stays create-if-absent.** The new acquire is a compare-and-set at the revision of the key's last record, which succeeds only while the key holds no live value — the same server-side check jnats' `create` makes. An old node's `create` and a new node's write race on the one stream leader; exactly one wins. `LeaseProtocolIT.earlierVersionInteroperates` runs both against real NATS.
- **The delete marker is jnats' own.** `deleteReturningRevision` publishes the `KV-Operation: DEL` marker with `Nats-Expected-Last-Subject-Sequence`, exactly as jnats' `delete(key, revision)` does, only keeping the acknowledgement's sequence; old nodes read it as a deleted key.
- **The value only gains a field.** `lid` is ignored by old nodes, which read `node`, `boot` and `since` as before. A new node treats a value without `lid` as somebody else's lease, so it never adopts or deletes an old node's lease as its own.
- **Takeover rules are unchanged** (presence, boot id, three unchanged heartbeats); renewals and releases stay compare-and-set on the revision.
- **Notifications are hints.** Old nodes still broadcast on `lease.released.<key>`, and new nodes still listen there. New nodes no longer broadcast a plain release, so an old node waiting for a lease a new node releases falls back to its poll (`ttl/4`, 5 s by default) until it is upgraded, and a new holder does not see an old waiter's marker when it decides to yield or hand off — fairness toward old-version waiters is weaker for the length of the upgrade. Neither affects mutual exclusion or the fence.

### Tests

`KvLeaseManagerValidityTest` (A1, A2, A11), `KvLeaseManagerScaleTest` (D1, D2, D4, D5, D7, C12), `NatsSharedKvReopenTest` (D3), two handoff tests in `ClusterConversationCoordinatorTest`, and `LeaseProtocolIT` against a real NATS: the delete-marker round trip, interoperation with an earlier version's jnats `create`/`delete`, a renewal and a create applied on the server whose replies are reported as timeouts, and addressed release notifications with no broadcast. Rewritten: `KvLeaseManagerTest.heartbeatIoFailureKeepsLease` (now `…KeepsLeaseOnlyUntilItsValidityRunsOut`).

**Files:** [`KvLeaseManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/lease/KvLeaseManager.java), [`NatsLeaseManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/lease/NatsLeaseManager.java), [`IConversationLeaseManager.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/lease/IConversationLeaseManager.java), [`ISharedKv.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/ISharedKv.java), [`NatsSharedKv.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/NatsSharedKv.java), [`InMemorySharedKv.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/cluster/InMemorySharedKv.java), [`ClusterConversationCoordinator.java`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/runtime/internal/ClusterConversationCoordinator.java); docs: [`clustering.md`](../clustering.md), [`configuration-reference.md`](../configuration-reference.md), [`metrics.md`](../metrics.md).

### Not done here

- The turn side of A1 (checking `isLost()` before the user-memory upsert and the reply) and the switch of `ClusterHitlRecovery` to `peekStrict` belong to WS-B.
- D2's "load memory after the lease is granted" and the reference ingress configs' consistent hashing are outside this workstream.
- Sharding `LEASES` by hash prefix (D3–D5 plan item) is not attempted.

## Clear the Guava advisory flagged by Scorecard; OpenTelemetry blocked on Quarkus (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI, branch `fix/dep-advisories-guava-otel`

**What changed** (`pom.xml`, `dependencyManagement` only): `com.google.guava:guava` 33.6.0-jre -> 33.7.2-jre, for GHSA-xxph-c9ww-hj94 (uncontrolled memory allocation when `CompactHashMap`, `CompactHashSet` or `MapMakerInternalMap` is deserialized). Pulled in by `langchain4j-qdrant`. Not reachable from a known code path; fixed because OpenSSF Scorecard counts advisories regardless of reachability.

**Not fixed - GHSA-ccrj-gw8r-854m** (quadratic CPU in W3C `tracestate` parsing, `opentelemetry-api` <= 1.66.0, fixed in 1.67.0). Quarkus 3.40.1.1, the latest platform, manages OpenTelemetry at 1.62.0, and 1.67.0 cannot be forced in either shape; both were tried and both fail at startup (Backend E2E caught the first, a local boot the second):
- Whole family at 1.67.0 (BOM imports): `NoSuchMethodError: io.opentelemetry.exporter.internal.ExporterBuilderUtil.validateEndpoint(String)`, because `quarkus-opentelemetry` calls an internal exporter method that 1.67 changed.
- API line only (`opentelemetry-api`, `-context`, `-common`) at 1.67.0 with SDK and exporter at 1.62.0: `NoClassDefFoundError: io.opentelemetry.api.internal.ConfigUtil`, which 1.67 removed and the 1.62 SDK still uses.

Neither showed up in the unit tests run locally (the tracing, style and repo-guard tests; the full suite was not run), so only a real boot shows it. Revisit when a Quarkus platform manages OpenTelemetry 1.67.0 or later. Until then the exposure is a long inbound `tracestate` header; capping header size at the proxy (or `quarkus.http.limits.max-header-size`) bounds it.

The `minimatch` advisory (GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249, dev tooling only) is handled in a separate PR.

## 🐛 fix(groups): a caller without access to a group gets 403 from the workspace endpoints, not 500 (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/group-workspace-forbidden`)

### What changed and why

`RestGroupWorkspace` (`readWorkspace`, `readBacklog`, `addBacklogTask`, `addCadence`, `deleteCadence`) calls `ResourceAccessGuard.requireAccess`
inside a `try` whose last `catch (Exception e)` logs and answers 500. The guard denies with `io.quarkus.security.ForbiddenException`, so a
caller without access to the group received a 500 and an error log line instead of 403. Each of the five methods now catches
`ForbiddenException` ahead of the generic catch and rethrows it, so Quarkus maps it to 403.

`RestGroupWorkspaceTest` gains `everyEndpoint_accessDenied_propagatesForbidden`, which makes the guard throw the real Quarkus type for each of the
five endpoints. Mutation-checked: with the new catch removed the test fails because the endpoints answer 500 instead of throwing.

## ✨ feat(hitl): approval analytics, sampled audit and earned autonomy (2026-11-02)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/approval-analytics-autonomy`, stacked on `feat/hitl-edit-on-approve`)

### What changed and why

Approval gates have a capacity: a gate that asks about everything gets rubber-stamped. This puts human attention where
it matters — see [HITL Oversight](../hitl-oversight.md).

- **Config** — one nested field, agent-level `hitlConfig.oversight {sampledAudit, autonomyPolicy}`
  ([`OversightConfig`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/hitl/model/OversightConfig.java)), validated on every save
  and import by [`OversightConfigValidation`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/hitl/OversightConfigValidation.java)
  (one line in `HitlConfigValidation`). Absent = nothing sampled, nothing recommended or suspended.
- **Approval analytics** — `ApprovalDecisionProjector` observes F3's `HitlDecisionRecorded` (`@ObservesAsync`) and stores
  one row per decided unit in a new `approval_decisions` store (MongoDB + PostgreSQL), keyed `decisionId#index` so the
  event and `ApprovalBackfillService` (rebuild from the ledger via `HitlApprovalAudit.parse`; once on first start for
  90 days, and `POST /hitl/analytics/rebuild`) never double-count. Pre-6.7 entries become `legacy:` rows that are never
  evidence. `GET /hitl/analytics/summary` / `decisions`.
- **Sampled audit** — `SampledAuditRecorder` is an `AllowedToolCallObserver` set on `ToolLoopRunner` (a setter, no
  constructor change) and called after each allowed call in both loops. Deterministic HMAC sampling keyed from the audit
  key, a per-(agent, category, UTC day) cap through `IToolQuotaStore`, a bounded write queue, card-level redaction. New
  `audit_samples` store; `/hitl/samples` review queue with one-at-a-time verdicts (CAS, no bulk).
- **Earned autonomy** — `AutonomyEvaluator` and `PromotionPatchBuilder` (pure), `AutonomyService` (apply, dismiss,
  suspend, lift; demotion from flagged samples and demote-code rejections), `autonomy_state` store, `/hitl/autonomy`.
  Apply re-checks the evidence, needs the reviewed agent version, validates and writes a new version through
  `IRestAgentStore` — never deploys.
- **Suspension overlay** — `AutonomyOverlayService` copies the effective tool-approval config with a transient,
  `@JsonIgnore` `suspendedPatterns` list plus a synthetic `WAIT_INDEFINITELY` rule per pattern (no `ConversationHitlService`
  edit: "strictest rule wins" does the rest); `ToolApprovalGate` gates an allowed call matching one with
  `suspended:<pattern>`. Applied in `LlmTask` (memory carrier and effective config), `McpCallsTask`, and carried through
  `TaskToolApprovalsResolver`'s strict merge. `ToolApprovalGate.firstExemptMatch` tells exempt from unmatched calls.
- **Security** — `X-EDDI-Agent-Call` set last on an httpcall aimed at this deployment only — its own origin
  (`SelfUrlResolver`), a loopback address or a host in the new `eddi.hitl.oversight.self-hosts`; third-party APIs see no
  EDDI header and their wire format is unchanged (`AgentCallMarker`, one line in `ApiCallExecutor`);
  `AgentOriginatedRequestFilter` answers 403 `AGENT_ORIGINATED` to marked oversight writes; `SelfUngatingGuard` delegates
  to `OversightWriteGuard` (no oversight-API writes, no change to `hitlConfig.oversight`).
- **Maintenance** — `hitl-autonomy-maintenance` system schedule (fast path right after the alerts one in
  `ScheduleFireExecutor`, reserved in `RestScheduleStore`): sample expiry, round-robin evaluation, retention, the
  first-start rebuild. Gauges refreshed per node.
- **Manager** — new `/manage/oversight` page (sidebar next to Approvals): analytics tiles and a per-category table with
  tiers, the sample review queue (one sample at a time, a flag needs a reason code, no select-all), and recommendations
  (evidence, change list, configuration preview, a second confirmation when the change leaves no gated tool) and
  suspensions (lift needs a note). `oversight` i18n namespace in all 11 locales; MSW handlers in `oversight-handlers.ts`;
  OpenAPI snapshot refreshed for the new endpoints.
- **Docs** — new [`hitl-oversight.md`](../hitl-oversight.md); one-line links in `hitl.md`, `audit-ledger.md`,
  `gdpr-compliance.md`, `SUMMARY.md`; metrics in `metrics.md` and dashboard panels 400–405; properties in
  `configuration-reference.md`.

### Not in this change

MCP read tools and the Platform Operator (see
[`planning/operator-followups/approval-analytics-autonomy.md`](../../planning/operator-followups/approval-analytics-autonomy.md));
the alerts `HITL_AUTONOMY` source and a Slack sample digest; Slack verdict buttons; an `oversight` form in the agent
editor (JSON only for now).

**Tests.** The three stores have shared contracts (`OversightStoreContracts`) run against both backends by
`MongoOversightStoresIT` and `PostgresOversightStoresIT` (Testcontainers): idempotent insert, window and field filters,
compare-and-set review and save, expiry, retention, erasure and the indexes. They compile but were not run on the machine
this was built on (no Docker); CI's `verify -DskipITs=false` runs them.

## ✨ feat(groups): HUMAN members execute task-force tasks (2026-11-02)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/group-human-task-executors`, stacked on `feat/group-human-tiebreak`)

### What changed and why

I6 shipped human group members but refused them in any group with PLAN/EXECUTE/VERIFY phases, because task waves
assign and time work on agent latencies. A HUMAN member may now **execute** the tasks assigned to them; planning
and verifying stay with agents.

- **Engine** — the EXECUTE wave loop (`TaskForceEngine`) filters HUMAN-assigned tasks out of its waves. When no
  agent task is left but a human task is executable, it throws the existing `HumanTurnRequired` for the
  highest-priority one ([`GroupHumanTasks`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/GroupHumanTasks.java)),
  now carrying a `HumanRequestDetail` of kind `TASK`, which the phase dispatch commits as the usual I6 pause.
  `GroupHitlCoordinator.resolveHumanTurn` applies the answer to the task inside the same compare-and-swap
  (complete, or submit for approval under TASK-granularity HITL; a `SKIP_TURN` timeout fails it) and puts it back
  on a rolled-back resume; the resumed wave loop runs its dependents. A human task is a turn and costs $0. A VERIFY
  rework re-runs EXECUTE with human pauses disabled and skips HUMAN-assigned tasks (they stay FAILED, with a note).
- **Assignment** — round-robin never picks a HUMAN member (`TaskListParser.roundRobinAssign` and
  `TaskForceEngine.resolveAssignee("ALL")`); explicit `ROLE:` / principal assignment, agent-filed tasks and the
  planner (told which members are human) still can. Bidders were already agents only.
- **Save time** — `AgentGroupStore.humanMemberProblems` no longer refuses task phases; it calls
  `GroupGapsValidation.humanTaskForceProblems`, which refuses a HUMAN planner/verifier (the moderator, or the first
  member by speaking order without one) in a group with PLAN or VERIFY phases. `targetEachPeer` phases and nested
  groups containing humans stay refused.
- **Surfaces** — the summaries' `pendingKind: TASK` / `pendingTaskId` (from the tie-break PR), a Slack task notice,
  and in the Manager a task variant of the human-turn banner (subject and description) plus a **Task** inbox badge;
  new i18n namespace `groupHumanTasks` (11 locales).
- **Docs** — [Group Human Decisions](../group-human-decisions.md) gains *Humans as task-force executors*;
  [Group Conversations](../group-conversations.md) points there from the human-member bounds and the TASK_FORCE
  section.

### Not done

- The save-time warning for a standing-team cadence whose `eddi.groups.cadence.claim-ttl` is shorter than the human
  turn timeout: the TTL is a deployment property the group store cannot see. The docs say to keep `turnTimeout` well
  inside it.
- Reassigning an unanswered human task to an agent, and humans as bidders.

### What's next

Operator/MCP follow-up: [`planning/operator-followups/group-human-task-executors.md`](../../planning/operator-followups/group-human-task-executors.md).
Rides schema v5 from the tie-break PR (no further bump).

---

## ✨ feat(groups): `tiePolicy: HUMAN_DECIDES` — a named person breaks a tied vote (2026-11-02)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/group-human-tiebreak`)

### What changed and why

`HUMAN_DECIDES` was a real enum value that every save refused: a tie that waits on a person needed resume
machinery a finished VOTE phase did not have. It now pauses the discussion until the decider chooses, riding
I6's human-input path rather than the approval path.

```json
"voteConfig": {
  "method": "MAJORITY", "optionsSource": "EXPLICIT", "options": ["Double down on SEO", "Launch the paid pilot"],
  "tiePolicy": "HUMAN_DECIDES",
  "humanTieBreak": { "decider": "founder@acme.com", "timeout": "PT24H", "onTimeout": "MODERATOR_DECIDES" }
}
```

- **Config** — [`HumanTieBreakConfig`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/model/HumanTieBreakConfig.java)
  as an eighth `VoteConfig` component (the seven-argument constructor stays). Save time
  ([`GroupGapsValidation`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/validation/GroupGapsValidation.java),
  called from `AgentGroupStore.validateVotePhases` in place of the old throw) needs a resolvable decider (named,
  or the single HUMAN member), a positive ISO-8601 timeout, and a moderator for `MODERATOR_DECIDES`.
- **Engine** — `PhaseExecutionEngine.recordVoteDecision` records the NONE tally as before and returns a
  `PendingTieBreak` ([`HumanTieBreakEngine`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/HumanTieBreakEngine.java):
  decider, prompt from option texts and totals only, choice matching, decision building).
  `GroupConversationService` pauses on it after the persist and `onPhaseComplete`, after applying a deferred
  `INSERT_VOTE` and dropping a deferred escalation with a note.
  [`GroupTieBreakPauses`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/GroupTieBreakPauses.java) commits
  the pause (`speakerIdx = -1`, `RESUME_KIND_TIE_BREAK`, no turn charged), resolves a choice (validated before
  any mutation, CAS out of the pause, rollback on executor refusal) into a `vote+human-tiebreak` VOTE record with
  the losing ballots as dissents, and resumes at the next phase — or pauses for the vote phase's own approval
  gate. Timeouts: `NO_DECISION`, `MODERATOR_DECIDES` (turn- and cost-gated, one turn) or `ABORT` (the plain ABORT
  policy, so the timeout handler needs no change). `GroupHitlCoordinator` dispatches to it from
  `submitHumanInput` and `skipHumanTurnOnTimeout`.
- **Persisted shape** — `PendingHumanInput` gains a `detail`
  ([`HumanRequestDetail`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/model/HumanRequestDetail.java): kind, options,
  taskId, transcriptFrom, timeoutResolution); the nine-argument constructor stays. **Schema v5**
  (`GroupConversation.CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`), an identity hop, so an older pod refuses a tie-break pause instead
  of re-running the vote.
- **Surfaces** — REST `HumanInputRequest` gains an optional `choice` (and `content` loses `@NotBlank`; the service
  keeps requiring it for every other kind); MCP `submit_group_human_input` gains an optional `choice`; the REST and
  MCP status summaries add `pendingKind`/`pendingOptions`/`pendingTaskId`
  ([`PendingHumanInputView`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/internal/groups/PendingHumanInputView.java)), the
  inbox `PendingApprovalSummary` a `pendingKind`; `HumanInputRequestedEvent` carries the `detail`; Slack posts a
  tie-break notice (still notification-only).
- **Audit and metrics** — one `hitl.approval` entry per resolution: `verdict TIE_BROKEN`,
  `approvalCategory group.vote_tiebreak`, `reasonCode`, options, choice — and no `decisionOutcome`, because a
  tie-break is a human turn. Counter `eddi.group.vote.tiebreaks{policy, resolution}`.
- **Manager** — the phase editor enables HUMAN_DECIDES with decider/timeout/on-timeout fields; the human-turn
  banner's tie-break variant asks for one option plus an optional rationale; the inbox labels tie-breaks. New i18n
  namespace `groupTieBreak` (11 locales).
- **Docs** — new page [Group Human Decisions: Tie-Breaks](../group-human-decisions.md); links from
  [Group Conversations](../group-conversations.md) and [HITL](../hitl.md); the AGENTS.md roadmap row no longer
  lists HUMAN_DECIDES as pending.

### Compatibility

Every other tie policy behaves as before. A document without `detail` is a speaking turn; a pending record's
`onTimeout` is still `SKIP_TURN`/`ABORT`. The schema bump to v5 collides with any other branch that bumps it —
whichever merges second renumbers.

### What's next

Humans as task-force executors (`feat/group-human-task-executors`) reuse `HumanRequestDetail` (`kind TASK`).
Operator/MCP follow-up: [`planning/operator-followups/group-human-tiebreak.md`](../../planning/operator-followups/group-human-tiebreak.md).

### Review follow-ups (2026-11-05)

- `AgentGroupStore.validateHumanMembers` now also refuses a nested GROUP member whose VOTE phase uses
  `tiePolicy: HUMAN_DECIDES`. The parent's turn cancels a nested pause and records `SKIPPED`, so such a tie
  could never be decided; it now fails at save time, like a nested HUMAN member.
- The Manager inbox reason for a tie-break row is cause-neutral ("Waiting on … to decide the vote", 11 locales):
  a vote that misses quorum pauses with the same `pendingKind`.
- [Group Human Decisions](../group-human-decisions.md) separates the role check (`eddi-viewer` cannot answer)
  from the principal check, says the principal check needs authentication, says every new discussion is
  stamped v5 (an older pod refuses any resume during a rolling upgrade), and that `choice` is required for a
  tie-break; [MCP Server](../mcp-server.md) says the same for `submit_group_human_input`.

Review follow-ups (round 3):

- `AgentGroupStore.hasHumanDecidesVote` reads only `VOTE` phases: a `voteConfig` the Manager keeps on a phase
  whose type was switched never starts a vote, so it no longer refuses the parent that nests the group.
- The Manager inbox reason for a human turn carries `data-testid="human-turn-reason-<id>"` and
  `data-reason-kind`, and the tie-break test asserts those instead of the English copy.
- [MCP Server](../mcp-server.md) says a `TIE_BREAK` pause is answered by `humanTieBreak.decider`, a principal who
  need not be a member; [Group Human Decisions](../group-human-decisions.md) says the audit entry and the counter
  are written only when the audit ledger and the meter registry are present.

Review follow-ups (round 4):

- Docs: `pendingOptions` appears only for a caller who may read the pending content; the tie-break audit entry records a choice only for a human submission; an `eddi-approver` may answer a tie-break when their own principal is the decider.

## ✨ feat(groups): per-team monthly budgets — a calendar-month dollar cap per group, metered from the cost ledger, enforced at the gate and inside the run (2026-11-02)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/team-budgets`, stacked on `feat/cost-accounting`)

### What changed and why

Several standing teams share one tenant and one monthly cost budget. One busy team could spend the whole month and
leave the others nothing, and nothing said who had spent what. A group can now carry a `teamBudget`; see
[Team Budgets](../team-budgets.md).

- **Config** — [`TeamBudgetConfig`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/model/TeamBudgetConfig.java)
  (`monthlyBudgetUsd`, `onExhausted`, `oversight.teams`) on `AgentGroupConfiguration`, validated at save
  ([`TeamBudgetConfigValidator`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/configs/groups/TeamBudgetConfigValidator.java), 400),
  and changeable only by `eddi-admin` while `eddi.groups.team-budget.admin-only-edits=true` (default).
- **Store** — one document per `(groupId, UTC month)` in `team_budget_months` (MongoDB and PostgreSQL twins) holding
  spend, the month's cap adjustment, a bounded adjustment history and the applied transfer ids; atomic `$inc` /
  `ON CONFLICT` spend, one conditional update for a debit, 13-month retention.
- **Off when absent** — a group without a `teamBudget` is not metered, gated or ceilinged: no store access, no state on its discussions. `eddi.groups.team-budget.metering-enabled=false` is the global kill switch. Spend before a cap is added is not back-filled.
- **Metering** — [`TeamSpendMeter`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/groups/budget/TeamSpendMeter.java) charges
  the increase of the root discussion's `gc.totalCost` after each member turn, each nested roll-up and each leg. A nested
  child charges nothing (its parent pays). Never fails a discussion; an outage is carried to the next charge.
- **Gate** — a new root discussion, a follow-up or a continuation is refused (409) when the team or the tenant has no
  budget left or the budget cannot be read (fail closed); a cadence fire is skipped, or failed when unreadable.
- **Inside a run** — the team ceiling is written in the discussion's `totalCost` terms
  ([`TeamBudgetCeilings`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/engine/groups/budget/TeamBudgetCeilings.java)), so the
  existing `maxCostPerDiscussion` checks enforce it unchanged, with the team's policy and its own transcript text.
- **REST** — snapshot, history, batch listing, set this month's cap, idempotent transfers; reads are VIEW-guarded,
  money moves are `eddi-admin`, both write the audit ledger.
- **Template values** — `{context.teamBudget}` and `{context.teamBudgets}` (a board's `oversight.teams`, filtered by
  the discussion owner's view; numbers only).
- **Manager** — a team budget card on the group workspace page (cap/spent/remaining, history), a team table on the Quotas page, and admin-only adjust-this-month and transfer dialogs (transfers send an `Idempotency-Key`); strings in a `teamBudget` namespace in all 11 locales.
- **Metrics** — `eddi_team_budget_{metering,refusals,adjustments}_total`, a Team Budgets dashboard row (the
  Kubernetes copy is synced).

### Design decisions

- The root discussion's group pays; the ledger delta is the charge. The F1 `ISpendSink` feed was evaluated and not
  used: it attributes a nested child's turns to the child's group, carries no root, and fires only for
  tenant-accepted charges, while the in-run ceiling must live in `totalCost` terms.
- The team ceiling excludes its boundary (`Math.nextDown`) so a team at its cap stops instead of taking one more turn.
- One document per team per month (history for a board) rather than the tenant's roll-in-place row.
- 409 for refusals through the existing `GroupDiscussionException` handling; no edits in the hot REST resource.
- Equality exhausts, like the tenant.

### Not done / next

- Deferred to the combined follow-up (`planning/operator-followups/team-budgets.md`): the MCP tools, the Platform
  Operator grants and prompt text, and a `HandoffAdmission` bean that refuses a handoff to a team over budget (after F2).
- The LLM budget-transfer proposal tool was not built in this change.
- GDPR erasure does not yet pseudonymise the admin principal recorded in a month's adjustment history (the audit-ledger
  entry carries it as its user id and is covered by the ledger's own erasure).
- No oversubscription warning (sum of team caps above the tenant budget); `limitedBy` shows the tighter limit.

### Review follow-ups (2026-11-05)

CodeRabbit's full review of the merged-with-main head:

- `TeamSpendMeter`: the kill switch now drops a persisted team state at the next leg, so it lifts every in-run
  ceiling; a state created on a discussion that already spent takes that spend as its baseline (no back-fill).
- `GroupConversation.CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 4 → 5 for `teamBudgetState`: an older pod re-saving a paused document
  would drop it, so it must refuse the document instead (identity hop, no migration).
- Follow-ups: `resolveEffectiveCostCeiling` starts a team leg and includes the team ceiling, and the follow-up
  callback charges the team (`GroupLifecycleOps`).
- Transfers: each step id carries a fingerprint of source, target and amount, so a reused `Idempotency-Key` with
  another request is refused instead of crediting what was never debited; a retry checks the reversal and credit
  ids in `appliedTransferIds` before the shorter adjustment history.
- REST and the group-save check read the group with `readConfig`: a store failure is 503 (not 404, not a team
  silently missing from the listing), and an unreadable stored version makes a `teamBudget` save need admin.
- Manager: the Quotas table shows a read failure with a retry, polls like the card, and a transfer replaces its
  key after a 4xx; the MSW mock refuses same-team transfers; the card test waits for each response.
- Docs: kill-switch timing, the overshoot of `PARALLEL` phases and task-force waves, retries across a month
  boundary, the 50-group table limit, and the corrected upgrade note and `TeamBudgetConfig` Javadoc.

Second round of review follow-ups:

- The `teamBudget` edit check treats a group that does not exist as having no cap, so a non-admin's update of a
  missing id gets the store's 404 instead of a misleading admin-only 403; other store failures still fail closed.
- Manager: a failed background poll of the Quotas table keeps the cached table and any open transfer dialog (and
  its `Idempotency-Key`) mounted, with a "could not be refreshed" notice and a retry; only a failure with nothing
  loaded replaces the table.
- Docs: the synthesis-phase overshoot applies only under `SYNTHESIZE_NOW`; `ABORT` runs no synthesis phase.

## ✨ feat(llm): `toolCallGuardrails` — guardrails on tool-call arguments before execution (2026-11-02)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/tool-call-guardrails`)

### What changed and why

An agent designer can now screen the **arguments** of the tool calls a model asks for — the body of a GitHub comment
it is about to post, an e-mail, a handoff brief — with the guardrail types that already screened input and output.
Until now nothing looked at what went *into* a tool: `toolResultGuardrails` governs results, and the approval gate
only asks a human. A public-posting agent needed "never name a prospect", "no PII", "only approved claims" and a
disclosure label enforced before an approver ever saw the call.

```json
"toolCallGuardrails": [
  { "tools": ["mcp:add_issue_comment"], "fields": ["/body"], "type": "regex-filter", "action": "block",
    "parameters": { "patterns": "\\b(acme|globex)\\b" } },
  { "tools": ["mcp:add_issue_comment"], "fields": ["/body"], "type": "disclosure", "action": "redact",
    "parameters": { "text": "— AI-assisted reply" } }
]
```

- **Config** — `LlmConfiguration.Task.toolCallGuardrails`, a list of
  [`ToolCallGuardrailConfig`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/model/ToolCallGuardrailConfig.java): a
  guardrail plus `tools` (tool-approval patterns, the same matcher as `toolApprovals`) and `fields` (JSON pointers with
  a `*` segment; `""` the whole arguments; absent = every string). Absent or empty: nothing runs.
- **Phase** — `GuardrailPhase.TOOL_CALL` (`tool_call` in audit keys, metric tags and SSE). Every built-in type opts in:
  `regex-filter`, `pii-redaction`, `max-length`, `prompt-injection`, `judge`, `classifier`, `expression`, `json-object`.
  The judge's `{output}` is the screened field (`GuardrailModelClient.templateData`, one line).
- **New type `disclosure`** ([`DisclosureGuardrail`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/impl/DisclosureGuardrail.java)) —
  adds a fixed label, idempotently; `redact` only. Runs on output too.
- **Live path** — [`ToolCallGuardrails`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/ToolCallGuardrails.java),
  hooked into `ToolLoopRunner` after the call caps and **before** the approval gate. `block` / `reprompt` answer the
  call with a `NOT_EXECUTED` envelope built from configuration and EDDI vocabulary only (the screened text is never
  echoed); `redact` rewrites the call in the model's own message, so transcript, trace, pending batch and tool all see
  the same request. Reprompts are counted per entry per turn from the trace, so a HITL pause does not reset them.
- **Resume** — `ToolLoopResumer` re-screens each approved call, block-only, before the journal claim. Amended calls get
  every type; unamended ones only the deterministic ones (`screensHistory()`). Journal replays are not re-screened.
- **Engine reuse** — [`ToolCallGuardrailScreener`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/ToolCallGuardrailScreener.java)
  reuses `GuardrailService`'s resolve/evaluate/emit (four visibility modifiers, no copy);
  [`ArgumentFields`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/guardrails/ArgumentFields.java) walks and rewrites
  fields with exact numbers and key order, re-serializing only on change. Caps: 256 fields / 256 KiB per call and
  entry, 20 model-backed checks per loop run.
- **Cost** — a judge or classifier on arguments is admitted against the conversation ceiling and tenant budget and
  charged as a priced pseudo-tool call (`toolCallGuardrail`), through
  [`LoopSideCallSpend`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/impl/LoopSideCallSpend.java) — the summarizer's
  admit-and-charge logic extracted into one shared helper, which the summarizer now uses too.
- **Validation** — save time
  ([`ToolCallGuardrailConfigValidator`](../../src/main/java/ai/labs/eddi/modules/llm/tools/guardrail/ToolCallGuardrailConfigValidator.java),
  400; strict action, valid patterns and pointers, `disclosure` needs `redact`); deploy time
  (`ToolCallGuardrailValidation`: parameters by type, unknown or wrong-phase types refused).
- **Self-ungating** — `SelfUngatingGuard` rule 3 now also refuses an approved LLM-config write (body, patch or raw
  arguments) carrying `toolCallGuardrails`.
- **Metrics** — `eddi.guardrail.triggered{phase=tool_call}` and a new `eddi.guardrail.toolcall.decisions{stage,outcome}`.
- **Manager** — a "Tool-call guardrails" section in the LLM editor (tools and fields per entry, the guardrail side
  sheet with a tool-call phase), the `disclosure` type in the output list too, and a new `toolCallGuardrails` i18n
  namespace in all 11 locales.
- **Docs** — new page [Tool-Call Guardrails](../tool-call-guardrails.md); links from `langchain.md`, `hitl.md`,
  `mcp-client.md`, `metrics.md` and `SUMMARY.md`.

### Not in this change

- Configurable rules on tool **results** (`toolResultGuardrails.rules`, deterministic types only) — follow-up; the
  shape is in the docs page.
- Agent- and group-level `toolCallGuardrails` inheritance; refusing a full-PUT that *removes* another agent's
  `toolCallGuardrails` (v1 refuses bodies that carry the field).
- Platform Operator prompt and gate-guard, and MCP tools — see `planning/operator-followups/tool-call-guardrails.md`.

### Review follow-ups

- `SelfUngatingGuard`: a patch `add` or `remove` at an array index at or before a stored task's gate or guardrails,
  under the same parent, is now refused for both gates and guardrails. Two `remove /tasks/0` operations could
  otherwise drop a gated `/tasks/1` unseen. Appends (`/-`) and later indexes still pass.
- [Tool-Call Guardrails](../tool-call-guardrails.md) and the dashboard panel no longer read `live/blocked` as
  "approvals never asked for" or `resume/blocked` as "edits refused". The labels do not separate gated from
  ungated calls, or edited from unchanged ones.

## 🐛 fix(loadtest): the load-test stack admits its plaintext mock LLM to the vault transport check (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`fix/loadtest-vault-plaintext`)

### What changed and why

The `Load Test DB Ops` workflow (check "DB ops per turn") went red on `main` from the 2026-11-03 nightly: `ci-gate-10vu-10t: 0 ok ... errors: app_error=100`. Every turn failed in the EDDI container with `VaultHostAllowlist$HostNotAllowedException: LLM provider 'openai' sends ${vault:...apiKey} to wiremock:8080 over unencrypted http`. #1175 added a separate plaintext-transport check, so a vault secret is no longer sent over `http://` to a non-loopback host unless `eddi.vault.allow-plaintext-remote-hosts=true`, even when the host is listed in `allowedHosts`. The load test points its OpenAI-compatible model at the plain-http mock `http://wiremock:8080/v1` and stores the API key as a vault secret, so every LLM call was refused before it left EDDI. The 2026-11-02 nightly ran before #1175 merged.

Both load-test compose files ([`docker-compose.loadtest.yml`](../../scripts/loadtest/docker-compose.loadtest.yml), [`docker-compose.cluster.yml`](../../scripts/loadtest/docker-compose.cluster.yml)) now set `EDDI_VAULT_ALLOW_PLAINTEXT_REMOTE_HOSTS=true`, the deployment-level opt-in the check documents. The setting still admits plaintext only to a host on the secret's own `allowedHosts`.

### Design decisions

- The opt-in goes on the throw-away benchmark stack, not into the driver: switching the mock to https would measure TLS rather than turns, and the check is correct in production.

## ✨ feat(llm): cascade judge grades against the request, MCP on the capped client — langchain4j 1.23 follow-ups (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI (`feat/langchain4j-1-23-adaptations`)

Dependabot moved EDDI to langchain4j 1.23.0 / 1.23.0-beta33 (#1214) with no code change, and CI passed. EDDI
implements no `McpTransport` and uses none of the Jackson 2 MCP methods the release removed (#6824), so nothing
broke. This change adopts what the release made possible, and one lesson from its new Confidence Cascade pattern
(langchain4j#6765).

### What changed and why

- **The `judge_model` cascade judge sees the request.** It was sent only the answer and asked to rate it, so a
  fluent answer to the wrong question scored as well as a right one. langchain4j's cascade grades "does the answer
  fully and correctly address the request?". `ConfidenceEvaluator` now sends the text of the latest user message
  the step's model saw (8,000 characters at most: a single long text keeps its end, where a templated prompt puts the question; with attachments the typed question is kept whole and the head of the attached text fills the rest) together with the
  answer, and asks for the probability that the answer is acceptable for the request. With no user text (an
  image-only message) the judge rates the answer on its own, as before. `CascadingModelExecutor` passes the request
  from both the legacy and the agent-mode step.
- **`modelCascade.acceptanceCriterion`** (optional string): one sentence an acceptable answer must also meet, added
  to the judge's instructions. It is langchain4j's `yesWhen` for the same pattern. Only the judge reads it, so
  `CascadeConfigValidator` warns when it is set without a `judgeModel`. The Manager's cascade editor shows it under
  the judge model editor (all 11 locales).
- **Untrusted text is data, not instructions.** The request and the answer go to the judge as JSON string values
  (Jackson-encoded, so neither can close its field), and the system message tells it to ignore instructions and
  self-reported scores inside them. The author's criterion is trusted config and sits in the system message. The
  docs now say so plainly: this keeps graded text from posing as instructions, but the grade can still be swayed,
  so the cascade is a cost/quality trade-off and not a security boundary.
- **MCP runs on `BoundedJdkHttpClient`.** langchain4j 1.23 made the `StreamableHttpMcpTransport` backend pluggable
  (#6726). The transport now uses EDDI's capped JDK client: a response past `eddi.llm.max-response-bytes` (32 MB)
  fails instead of being read into the heap whole, and the per-request URL re-check against DNS rebinding moved from
  the header hook into the client's request guard, which runs before every send. `followRedirects(false)` stays
  pinned, and `streamingBackpressure(true)` keeps what the transport's own backend set. The cap's error message no
  longer says "LLM provider", since MCP shares it. This puts MCP on the same client as the LLM builders, not on
  `OutboundHttp`: the HTTP client consolidation's Phase 3 (a langchain4j `HttpClient` adapter over `OutboundHttp`) is
  not built yet, and its Vert.x engine is Phase 4. When Phase 3 lands, MCP moves with the builders through this same
  `httpClientBuilder` hook — the hook the October security review noted was missing.
- **Version stamps.** `docs/rag.md`, `InputTypedEmbeddingModelTest` and `VertexGeminiLanguageModelBuilder` named
  1.22. Checked against the 1.23 jars: still only Gemini (and Cohere) declare `INPUT_TYPE`, and the Vertex streaming
  builder still has no `seed`.

### Tests

`ConfidenceEvaluatorJudgeRequestTest` (6): the request and criterion reach the judge, hostile text stays inside its
JSON field, no request means no `request` field, request extraction and its tail truncation.
`CascadingModelExecutorToolReplayTest.judgeSeesRequestAndCriterion` covers the executor wiring.
`McpTransportResponseCapTest` serves a 40 MB handshake answer from a loopback server; it and
`McpTransportRevalidationTest` both fail when the `.httpClientBuilder(...)` line is removed (mutation-checked). In the
Manager, two `task-cascade-section` tests cover editing and clearing the field and hiding it for other strategies.

### Considered, not done

- **Skip the judge on a step that is accepted anyway** (the last step, a null threshold). langchain4j never grades
  its last tier. Here the last step's confidence feeds `audit:confidence`, `cascadeConfidence`, the confidence
  metric and `returnBestAcrossSteps`, so skipping the call would change what those report. That needs its own
  decision.
- **A `decision_model` strategy** on langchain4j's `DecisionModel` (OpenAI Decisions API, Ollama): it returns
  probabilities instead of a number the model writes as text. The API is `@Experimental`, and its token usage goes to
  a `DecisionModelListener`, outside EDDI's cost accounting. Revisit when it stabilises.
- **The agentic layer** (`ConfidenceCascadePlanner`, decision agents, activation conditions) and AiServices-only
  features (tool search strategy, `AiServiceInvocationSummaryEvent`): EDDI's orchestration is config-driven and does
  not use `AiServices`.

## Update check is on by default, with a server-side opt-out and a notification (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI — branch `feat/update-check-default-on`.

**What changed**

- The "is a newer EDDI out?" check used to be browser-only and opt-in (a `localStorage` flag). It is now **on by default** and EDDI itself runs it: `UpdateCheckService` (`engine/updatecheck/`) asks `api.github.com` once a day (first run a minute after boot), compares with `quarkus.application.version`, and raises a notification when the release is newer. The announced version is stored, so a dismissed notice is not raised again by the next day's check or a restart.
- **Two opt-outs.** `eddi.update-check.enabled=false` (env `EDDI_UPDATE_CHECK_ENABLED`, wired through `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example` and the Helm value `eddi.updateCheck.enabled`, chart 2.14.0) stops it entirely — it is tested before the database is read, so the first check after the first boot is suppressed too — and wins over the Manager's switch, which shows as locked. The Manager's Updates page has the other switch (administrators), stored through the new `ISystemSettingStore` (`system_settings`, Mongo and Postgres), so it holds across restarts, browsers and nodes. `GET/PUT /administration/update-check`.
- **Notification reuses the existing inbox** (`WorkspaceNotifications`): new type `UPDATE_AVAILABLE`, addressed to the shared recipient `system:admins`, which administrators read in addition to their own inbox (with authorization off, everyone is an administrator). The bell no longer requires workspaces; it shows while workspaces or the update check are on.
- White-labelled deployments that did not set `eddi.branding.show-update-check` neither check nor notify.
- Manager: the banner/card read the server's switch instead of `localStorage`; the old key is ignored. `api.github.com` is still the only off-origin host the Manager contacts.

**Design decisions**

- State lives on the backend because only the backend can honour the env var before anyone reaches the UI.
- Platform Operator checked (§4.6.6): no prompt or allow-list change. The operator cannot write this switch (`PUT` stays off its allow-list on purpose) and the behaviour is documented in `docs/docker.md`, which it can read.
- 11 locales updated for the reworded `updates.autoCheck*` strings and the two new ones.

**Not done:** the browser still makes its own per-load request to GitHub for the card's release notes while the switch is on (a second, cheap call; the server's notification is the primary signal).

## Workforce dashboard shows every agent, ordered by your last use (2026-11-03)

**Repo:** EDDI, `ui/manager`

`WorkforceSection` in `pages/workforce/workforce-dashboard.tsx` cut the grouped agent list with `.slice(0, 10)`, so an eleventh agent was silently missing from "Your Digital Workforce" and the counter read "10 experts". Now:

- every agent is listed: the section pages through all descriptors with `useAllAgentDescriptors` (descriptors are per version, so a single page can hold fewer distinct agents than its size);
- if a descriptor page fails to load, the agents already loaded stay visible with an inline "Some experts could not be loaded" notice and a Retry button (`workforce.loadIncomplete`);
- above 12 agents the section collapses to the first 12 with a "Show all N" toggle, and expands into a height-capped scroll area (`workforce.showAll`, `workforce.showLess`, all 11 locales);
- order is most recently used by this user first (new `use-recent-agents.ts`, per browser in `localStorage`, recorded by `workforce-chat.tsx` once the chat has resolved which agent is open), then last modified for agents never used. The Manager's agent list keeps its last-modified order.

The Workforce sidebar keeps its own compact top-10.
